# Midpoint lenses in segment spaces

Coordinate `EN-111-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-B-ML-M-0659` | `PM-331.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Midpoint-lenses-in-segment-spaces-September-27-2026` | date 2026-09-27 | derived from the openai/math preprint Midpoint-lenses-in-segment-spaces-September-27-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "For a real segment-forest dual with unbounded finite component heights and the real infinite-height coordinate predual, we bound the tail of an arbitrary displacement in a symmetric lens by $`2\\sqrt{R^2-\\|x\\|^2}`$, where <i>R</i> is the lens radius and <i>x</i> is its finitely supported center. Both given norms are asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex, although neither space admits an equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. The finite-height forest dual is reflexive.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For a real segment-forest dual with unbounded finite component heights and the real infinite-height coordinate predual, we bound the tail of an arbitrary displacement in a symmetric lens by $`2\sqrt{R^2-\|x\|^2}`$, where <i>R</i> is the lens radius and <i>x</i> is its finitely supported center. Both given norms are asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex, although neither space admits an equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. The finite-height forest dual is reflexive.

- **claim-joint-tail-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-mol-prop-joint-budget)): Let $Y$ be either the finite-height forest dual $X=J^*$ or the infinite-height coordinate predual $B_\infty$. For every finite ancestral $H$, every $x,y\in Y$ with $x=P_Hx$, and every $R\ge0$ with $\\|x+y\\|,\\|x-y\\|\le R$, $$\label{eq:main-squared-tail} \\|Q_Hy\\|\le2\sqrt{R^2-\\|x\\|^2}.$$
  - Explanation: The squared-radius deficit controls the entire exterior displacement in either specified coordinate completion, uniformly over the lens.
- **claim-forest-coordinate-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-forest-structure)): The space $X$ defined by <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a> is infinite dimensional, separable, and reflexive, and the coordinate functionals have dense span in $X$. Let $H\subset\mathcal F$ be finite and *ancestral*, meaning that it contains every ancestor of each of its vertices. The coordinate projections $P_H$ and $Q_H$ are contractive on both $J$ and $X$. Moreover, $P_Hx\to x$ along any increasing ancestral exhaustion of $\mathcal F$.
  - Explanation: Finite component heights give reflexivity and dense coordinates; ancestral projections are contractive and exhaust the space in norm.
- **claim-forest-cone-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cones)): For every finite ancestral $H$, $$\label{eq:cone-decomposition} Q_HJ=\left(\bigoplus_{g\in G_H}J(U_g)\right)_{\ell_2}, \qquad \\|Q_Hx\\|^2=\sum_{g\in G_H}\\|x\|_{J(U_g)}\\|^2\quad(x\in X).$$ The gate-coordinate map $x\mapsto(x(e_g))_{g\in G_H}$ is a contraction from $X$ to $\ell_2(G_H)$.
  - Explanation: Exterior descendant cones combine by an exact square sum, and gate-coordinate restriction is contractive.
- **claim-infinite-coordinate-projections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-binf-projections)): Let $H\subset T_\infty$ be finite and ancestral. The coordinate projections $P_H$ and $Q_H=I-P_H$ are contractive on $J_\infty$ and $B_\infty$. The range of $P_H$ is finite dimensional, and finite coefficient vectors supported outside $H$ are dense in $\ker P_H$. Along every increasing ancestral exhaustion, $P_Hx\to x$ in $B_\infty$. A nonzero vector $x=P_Hx$ has a norming test $f=P_Hf$ with $\rho(f)=1$ and $x(f)=\\|x\\|$.
  - Explanation: The coordinate predual has contractive ancestral projections, dense finite tails, norm-convergent exhaustions, and finite-head norming tests.
- **claim-infinite-finite-atomic-hull** (theorem, [source](#stmt-binf-finite-hull)): For finite ancestral $H$, the unit ball of $P_HB_\infty$ is the convex hull of represented atoms with all segments in $H$. Each point is a convex combination of at most $\|H\|+1$ such atoms. The full unit ball of $B_\infty$ is the norm-closed convex hull of finite atoms.* *On $P_HB_\infty$ the norm also equals the gauge $$\inf\left\{\sum_j\lambda_j: x=\sum_j\lambda_j A_j,\quad \lambda_j\ge0,\quad A_j\text{ atoms supported in }H\right\}.$$ In particular, a vector of norm strictly below one has such a finite representation with $\sum_j\lambda_j<1$. The gauge defined on $c_{00}(T_\infty)$ by allowing all finite atoms has the same value. Its completion is therefore isometric to $B_\infty$.
  - Explanation: Finite-head balls have exact finite atomic representations, while the completed ball is a norm-closed convex hull; the corresponding gauges agree.
- **claim-infinite-cone-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-binf-cones)): For a finite test $u=Q_Hu$, $$\label{binf:primal-cones} \rho(u)^2=\sum_{g\in G_H}\rho(u\mathbf1_{U_g})^2.$$ After completion, $Q_HJ_\infty=(\bigoplus_{g\in G_H}J_\infty(U_g))_{\ell_2}$. For $x\in B_\infty$, $$\label{binf:dual-cones} \\|Q_Hx\\|^2=\sum_{g\in G_H}\\|x\|_{J_\infty(U_g)}\\|^2.$$ The map $x\mapsto(x(e_g))_{g\in G_H}$ is a contraction to $\ell_2(G_H)$.
  - Explanation: Exterior square-sum formulas hold in the infinite-tree coordinate completion without identifying it with the full dual.
- **claim-forest-finite-atomic-hull** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-hull)): For a finite ancestral set $K$, the unit ball of $P_KX$ is the convex hull of the atoms whose segments lie in $K$. Each member is a convex combination of at most $\|K\|+1$ such atoms. If $0\ne x=P_Hx$ for a finite ancestral $H$, there is $p=P_Hp\in J$ with $\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=\\|x\\|$.
  - Explanation: Finite forest balls are convex hulls of supported represented atoms and nonzero finite heads admit supported norming tests.
- **claim-forest-closed-atomic-ball** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-closed-atomic-ball)): Let $\mathcal A$ be the set of all represented atoms in $X$. Then $$B_X=\overline{\operatorname{conv}\mathcal A}^{\,\\|\cdot\\|}.$$ Every segment functional $d_S$ has norm one.
  - Explanation: The completed forest unit ball is the norm-closed atomic convex hull, and every nonempty segment functional has unit norm.
- **claim-opposite-crossing-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-opposite-crossing)): With the preceding notation, two disjoint segment families $\mathcal S,\mathcal S'$ satisfy $$\label{eq:opposite-crossing} 2\sum_{g:\,b_{\mathcal S}(g)b_{\mathcal S'}(g)<0} \|b_{\mathcal S}(g)b_{\mathcal S'}(g)\| \le L_{\mathcal S}+L_{\mathcal S'}.$$
  - Explanation: Simultaneous splits of nested crossing heads charge opposite-sign products to the available squared-test deficits.
- **claim-all-radius-averaged-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-mol-cor-all-radius-averaged)): In either of the segment-atomic completions considered above, $$\label{mol:eq:all-radius-averaged} \widehat\delta(t)\ge \frac{\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1}{2}\qquad(t>0).$$
  - Explanation: The joint lens estimate yields positive averaged midpoint growth at every positive radius in both completions.
- **claim-order-convex-projections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-foundation-order-convex)): Let $E$ be a set of vertices whose intersection with every segment is empty or a segment. The coordinate projection $P_E$ is contractive on both $J$ and $X=J^*$. Its range on $J$ is the completion of $c_{00}(E)$ under the segment norm restricted to $E$, and $$\\|P_Ex\\|=\\|x\|_{P_EJ}\\| \qquad (x\in X).$$ In particular these assertions apply to an ancestral set, its complement, a difference of nested ancestral sets, a descendant subtree, and an antichain. On an antichain the coordinate norm on either space is exactly the usual $\ell_2$ norm.
  - Explanation: Segment-preserving vertex restrictions induce contractive primal and dual projections; antichains carry the Euclidean coordinate norm.
- **claim-height-variant-identification** (classification, [source](#stmt-foundation-height-variants)): Let $I$ be countable and let $(h_i)_{i\in I}$ be nonnegative integers. On the disjoint forest $$\mathcal F_{\boldsymbol h} =\coprod_{i\in I}(\{i\}\times\mathbb N^{\le h_i})$$ define $J_{\boldsymbol h}$ by the same disjoint-segment norm and set $X_{\boldsymbol h}=J_{\boldsymbol h}^*$. If $J(h)$ denotes the single-tree completion on $\mathbb N^{\le h}$, then $$J_{\boldsymbol h} =\Big(\bigoplus_{i\in I}J(h_i)\Big)_{\ell_2}, \qquad X_{\boldsymbol h} =\Big(\bigoplus_{i\in I}J(h_i)^*\Big)_{\ell_2}$$ isometrically under coordinate restriction. Both spaces are reflexive, and finite coordinate vectors are dense in each. The projection and complementary-subtree formulas remain valid for this forest.* *The convention of one tree with $n$ levels, including the root, for every $n\ge1$ gives exactly $$J_{\mathrm{levels}}=\mathbb R\oplus_2 J, \qquad X_{\mathrm{levels}}=\mathbb R\oplus_2 X.$$ Its component estimate is $\\|u\\|_2\le\rho(u)\le\sqrt n\,\\|u\\|_2$, and a root-to-leaf path has $n-1$ extension steps.
  - Explanation: Countable forests of finite heights are square sums of Hilbert-isomorphic components; the alternative level convention splits off a singleton component.
- **claim-weak-null-modulus-growth** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-foundation-weak-null-growth)): Let $x\in S_N$, let $(z_j)$ be weakly null, and suppose $N(z_j)\ge t>0$ for every $j$. For every $0<\gamma<\overline\delta_N(t)$, all sufficiently large $j$ satisfy $$N(x+z_j)>1+\gamma.$$
  - Explanation: A positive one-sided modulus transfers to sufficiently late weakly null increments whose norms stay above the stated radius.
- **claim-bounded-tree-renorming-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-foundation-bounded-tree)): Suppose an infinite-dimensional real Banach space $(Z,\\|\cdot\\|)$ contains, for every positive integer $k$, vectors $(u_s)_{s\in\mathbb N^{\le k}}$ such that $$0<m\le\\|u_s\\|\le M, \qquad \\|u_{s^\frown j}-u_s\\|\ge c>0,$$ where $m,M,c$ are independent of $k$, and for each nonterminal $s$ the sequence $(u_{s^\frown j}-u_s)_j$ is weakly null against the whole continuous dual of $Z$. If $0<a\le b<\infty$ and $a\\|z\\|\le N(z)\le b\\|z\\|$, then $$\overline\delta_N\!\left(\frac{ac}{bM}\right)=0.$$ In particular the same conclusion holds at $ac/(2bM)$, and $Z$ admits no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.
  - Explanation: Arbitrarily deep uniformly bounded trees with weakly null nontrivial child increments force vanishing one-sided moduli under every equivalent norm.
- **claim-unbounded-forest-no-auc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-foundation-forest-obstruction)): If the finite component heights $h_i$ in Proposition <a href="#foundation:height-variants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="foundation:height-variants">12</a> are unbounded, then every equivalent norm $N$ on $X_{\boldsymbol h}$ with $a\\|x\\|\le N(x)\le b\\|x\\|$ satisfies $$\overline\delta_N(a/b)=\overline\delta_N(a/(2b))=0.$$
  - Explanation: Unbounded finite component heights supply the bounded trees that prevent an equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.
- **claim-infinite-path-no-auc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-binf-obstruction)): The unit root-path vectors in $B_\infty$ have unit child increments which are weakly null among the children of each fixed vertex. Every sequence of distinct coordinate vectors is in fact weakly null. If $a\\|\cdot\\|\le N\le b\\|\cdot\\|$, with $0<a\le b<\infty$, then the one-sided asymptotic modulus of $N$ vanishes at $a/(2b)$ and at $a/b$. In particular, $B_\infty$ has no equivalent AUC norm. The same obstruction holds for the finite-height forest, using trees of arbitrarily large finite height.
  - Explanation: Unit root paths have weakly null unit child increments against the whole dual, obstructing equivalent asymptotic uniform convexity in the coordinate predual.
- **claim-raw-child-transfer-budgets** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-stab-sign-obstruction)): In $B_\infty$, the root paths $h_v=d_{[o,v]}$ and the coordinates $e_v^*$ have norm one, and the children $(e_{v^\frown j}^*)_j$ of each fixed vertex are weakly null. If $a\\|z\\|\le N(z)\le b\\|z\\|$, with $0<a\le b<\infty$, then $\overline\delta_N(a/(2b))=0$.* *More precisely, put $t_0=a/(2b)$, with $d,\varepsilon>0$ below. Each of the following assumptions would permit a finite-codimensional subspace and a child whose approximation error and growth factor satisfy the corresponding row: $$\begin{array}{c\|c\|c} \text{assumed positive lower bound}& \text{error less than}&\text{growth factor}\\\hline 0<d<\overline\delta_N(t_0)&\min(a/2,ad/2)&1+d/2\rule{0pt}{13pt}\\ 2\varepsilon<\overline\delta_N(t_0)& \min(a/2,a\varepsilon/2)&1+\varepsilon/2\\ d=\overline\delta_N(t_0)>0&\min(a/2,ad/4)&1+d/4 \end{array}$$ Here the error is $N(e_v^*-f)$ for the chosen child and its approximant $f$ in the finite-codimensional subspace. Growth is measured relative to the $N$-norm of the parent root-path vector.
  - Explanation: The displayed raw-child error budgets turn hypothetical positive cofinite gains into incompatible growth of bounded root paths.
- **claim-inward-crossing-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-clip-crossing)): Let $f=P_Hf$ and $v=Q_Hv$ be finite tests. Use either the cone budgets $m_g$ or $q_g$, or the last-head budgets in <a href="#clip:group-budgets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="clip:group-budgets">[clip:group-budgets]</a>; write $c_j$ for the chosen budget of a cone or region. Fix $s>0$. At each attached group choose the shortest head suffix ending at its attachment whose $f$-sum has magnitude at least $sc_j$, if one exists and $c_j>0$. Let $\sigma_j$ be the opposite sign of that sum, and otherwise choose either sign.* *Suppose a finite exterior test $w$ satisfies $\rho(w)\le\rho(v)$, and every root prefix in group $j$ has $w$-sum in $\sigma_j[0,c_j]$. Then $$\label{clip:crossing-bound} \rho(f+sw)^2\le\rho(f)^2+3s^2\rho(v)^2.$$ The same conclusion holds with a strictly greater suffix threshold, provided the small-crossing case includes equality. With a non-strict suffix threshold, equality may also be assigned to the small-crossing case, leaving only strictly larger terms to be split.
  - Explanation: Inward root-prefix signs absorb large crossing terms through head splits, leaving only a quadratic perturbation cost.
- **claim-two-cumulative-clips** (construction, [source](#stmt-clip-two-clips)): For every $s>0$ and finite tests $f=P_Hf$, $v=Q_Hv$, there are $h_1,h_2$ supported on $\operatorname{supp}v$ such that $$\label{clip:difference-split} v=h_1-h_2,\qquad \rho(h_j)\le\rho(v),\qquad \rho(f+sh_j)^2\le\rho(f)^2+3s^2\rho(v)^2\quad(j=1,2).$$ Equivalently, $v=k_1+k_2$ with $k_1=h_1$, $k_2=-h_2$, and the two admissible perturbations are $f+sk_1$ and $f-sk_2$.
  - Explanation: Successive differences of clipped cumulative potentials split the exterior test without introducing new support, while retaining controlled inward perturbations.
- **claim-clipping-averaged-modulus** (bound, [source](#stmt-clip-paired-modulus)): For the infinite-height coordinate predual $B_\infty$ and the finite-height forest space $X$, $$\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1\qquad(t>0).$$
  - Explanation: Paired endpoint testing with the cumulative clips yields an explicit averaged midpoint modulus for both models.
- **claim-homogeneous-head-tail-lens** (bound, [source](#stmt-dir-primal-add-head-tail-lens)): If $x=P_Hx$ and $y=Q_Hy$, then $$\label{dir:primal:add:optimized} \max_{\pm}\\|x\pm y\\|\ge\sqrt{\\|x\\|^2+\\|y\\|^2/12}.$$ If $0<\sigma\le1$ and $w,Z_1,Z_2,\ldots\in X$ satisfy $\\|w\pm Z_j\\|\le1$ and $\\|Z_j-Z_l\\|\ge2\sigma$ for $j\ne l$, then $\\|w\\|\le1-\sigma^2/24$.
  - Explanation: Optimizing the clipped tests gives a homogeneous head-tail estimate and a center deficit for infinite separated lens families.
- **claim-selected-inward-test** (construction, [source](#stmt-clip-selection-prop)): Suppose $x=P_Hx$, $\\|x\\|=1$, $y\in\ker P_H$, and $v=Q_Hv$, $\rho(v)\le1$, and $y(v)>\\|y\\|/2$. For every $s>0$, each of the following constructions produces an exterior test $w$ with $$\|y(w)\|>\\|y\\|/8,\qquad \rho(w)\le1, \qquad \rho(f+sw)^2\le1+3s^2,$$ where $f=P_Hf$ norms $x$ and the inward signs are chosen with full cone budgets $q_g$.* 1. *Take the positive and negative cumulative parts globally, choose one by its total pairing, then retain one of the two groups of cones having the same prescribed inward sign.* 2. *Form all four combinations of a cumulative positive/negative part and a prescribed inward-sign group, and select one by its pairing.* 3. *Choose a cumulative part separately in each cone, orient it inward, then retain the cones on which the resulting pairings have one common sign.
  - Explanation: The stated selection orders retain a useful tail pairing while imposing inward prefix signs; endpoint selection follows the construction.
- **claim-frontier-shift-construction** (construction, [source](#stmt-clip-frontier-prop)): For finite $f=P_Hf$ and $v=Q_Hv$ with $\rho(f),\rho(v)\le1$, and every $s>0$, there is a finitely supported gate vector $a$ such that $$\label{clip:frontier-tests} \rho(a)\le1,\qquad \rho(a\pm v)\le2,\qquad \rho\bigl(f+s(a\pm v)\bigr)^2\le1+12s^2.$$ For either $Z=B_\infty$ or the finite-height forest space, this selected-endpoint method gives $$\label{clip:frontier-modulus} \widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge \frac{\sqrt{1+t^2/48}-1}{4}\qquad(0<t<1).$$
  - Explanation: A compensating gate vector orients shifted tail prefixes and gives a selected-endpoint midpoint estimate.
- **claim-independent-threshold-root-shifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-dir-primal-add-independent-threshold)): On either the finite-height forest or the infinite tree, let $f=P_Hf$, $v=Q_Hv$ be finite tests with $\rho(f),\rho(v)\le1$. Let finitely many $a_g\ge0$ satisfy $\sum_ga_g^2\le1$ and $\|v([g,u])\|\le a_g$ on every root prefix. Fix $\alpha>0$. For $a_g>0$, choose the shortest attached head suffix of magnitude at least $\alpha a_g$, if one exists, and let $\tau_g$ be its sign; otherwise put $\tau_g=1$. Strict thresholds may be used instead. Set $$a=-\sum_g\tau_ga_ge_g,\qquad h_+=a+v,\qquad h_-=a-v.$$ For $h\in\{a,h_+,h_-\}$ and $0<c\le\alpha/2$, $$\label{dir:primal:add:independent-cost} \rho(h)\le2,\qquad \rho(f+ch)^2\le1+4c\alpha+4c^2.$$ Both the full cone norms $q_g$ and the prefix maxima $m_g$ may be used for $a_g$.
  - Explanation: Separating the suffix threshold from the later multiplier controls root-shift perturbations using either full cone norms or prefix maxima.
- **claim-bounded-separated-family-growth** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-dir-primal-add-separated)): For each $D\ge1$, there exists $c_D>0$ such that, if $x,z_i\in X$, $1\le\\|x\\|\le D$, $\\|z_i\\|\le2D$, and $\\|z_i-z_j\\|\ge1$ for distinct indices in an infinite sequence, then some $i$ satisfies $\max_{\pm}\\|x\pm z_i\\|>\\|x\\|+c_D$.
  - Explanation: Finite-head compactness extracts a tail witness, and complementary endpoint tests cancel its unknown head evaluation to force uniform growth.
- **claim-radial-deficit-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-tail)): For a finite ancestral $H$, $x=P_Hx$, and arbitrary $y\in X$ with $\\|x\pm y\\|\le R$, one has $$\label{eq:atomic-tail} \\|Q_Hy\\|\le(\sqrt2+\sqrt6)\sqrt{R(R-\\|x\\|)}.$$
  - Explanation: Replacing coefficients by unscaled head sums and estimating errors separately gives the radial-deficit tail bound in the forest dual.
- **claim-radial-maximum-modulus** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-max-modulus)): For the specified dual norm on $X$, $$\overline\theta_X(t)\ge\frac{\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1}{2}\qquad(t>0).$$
  - Explanation: The radial lens bound gives the stated lower estimate for the maximum-based midpoint modulus of the specified forest norm.
- **claim-separate-endpoint-law-budgets** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-endpoint)): Let $0\le d<1$, let $H$ be finite and ancestral, and suppose $x=P_Hx$, $\\|x\\|\ge1-d$, and $\\|x\pm y\\|\le1$. Fix a head norming test $p$ with $\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=\\|x\\|$, and a finite ancestral set $K\supset H$. For each $\tau\in\{-1,1\}$ choose any finite represented atom law with mean $P_K(x+\tau y)$, and denote its expectation by $\mathbb E_\tau$. For an atom $A=\sum_i a_i d_{S_i}$, put $b_i=p(S_i)$, $A^\circ=\sum_i b_i d_{S_i}$, and $L=1-\sum_i b_i^2$. Then $$\label{eq:endpoint-coefficients} \mathbb E_\tau\!\left[L+\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2\right]\le4d, \qquad \mathbb E_\tau\\|A-A^\circ\\|\le2\sqrt d.$$ If $b(g)$ is the replaced crossing sum at gate $g$, put $m_\tau(g)=\mathbb E_\tau\|b(g)\|$ and $m=(m_1+m_{-1})/2$. These nonnegative vectors satisfy $$\label{eq:endpoint-gates} m_\tau\le2m\text{ coordinatewise},\qquad\\|m\\|_2\le\sqrt{6d}.$$
  - Explanation: Each conditional endpoint law has its own replacement budget; its absolute crossing mass is compared with the pooled nonnegative mass.
- **claim-conditional-joint-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-mol-prop-conditional-joint)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#mol:prop:joint-budget" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mol:prop:joint-budget">1</a>, assume in addition that $P_Hy=0$ and $r=\\|x\\|>0$, and put $\Delta=R^2-r^2$. Fix a head norming test $p=P_Hp$ with $\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=r$. For every finite ancestral $K\supset H$ and each sign $\tau\in\{-1,1\}$, choose any finite law of represented vectors $$A=\sum_i a_i d_{S_i},\qquad \sum_i a_i^2\le R^2, \qquad \mathbb E_\tau A=P_K(x+\tau y),$$ where each represented family consists of disjoint segments. Put $$b_i=r p(S_i),\qquad A^\circ=\sum_i b_i d_{S_i},\qquad V=\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2,\qquad L=r^2-\sum_i b_i^2.$$ These laws exist by the finite atomic-ball formula. For every such choice, the replacement satisfies $$\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint} \mathbb E_\tau(V+L)\le\Delta, \qquad \mathbb E_\tau\\|Q_H(A-A^\circ)\\|\le\sqrt\Delta.$$ At each gate $g$, let $b(g)$ be the replaced crossing coefficient, or zero when there is no crossing segment. The mixed absolute crossing vector $m(g)=\bigl(\mathbb E_1\|b(g)\|+\mathbb E_{-1}\|b(g)\|\bigr)/2$ satisfies $\\|m\\|_2\le\sqrt{2\Delta}$. Consequently a separate endpoint argument, followed by ancestral exhaustion, yields $$\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint-tail} \\|y\\|\le(1+2\sqrt2)\sqrt\Delta.$$ If the same two nonnegative errors are bounded separately, without retaining their sum, the corresponding calculation gives $$\label{mol:eq:strict-gauge-tail} \\|y\\|\le(1+2\sqrt3)\sqrt\Delta.$$
  - Explanation: A zero-head displacement makes each conditional evaluation exact; preserving the joint budget improves the separate-endpoint estimate over bounding its errors individually.
- **claim-gauge-polar-dual-identity** (classification, [source](#stmt-presentation-identity)): The coordinate identity satisfies $a(x)=q(x)=\\|x\\|$ on $c_{00}(\mathcal F)$ and extends to surjective linear isometries $$X_a=X_q=X=J^*.$$ The finite unit balls and completed atomic ball are therefore exactly those of Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-hull" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-hull">7</a> and Corollary <a href="#cor:closed-atomic-ball" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:closed-atomic-ball">8</a>.
  - Explanation: The atomic gauge, finite-coordinate polar norm, and forest dual norm agree before completion and yield the same completed space.
- **claim-incomparable-subtree-square-sum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-presentation-subtrees)): Let $(r_j)$ be a finite or countable family of incomparable vertices, and put $U_j=\{v:r_j\preceq v\}$. For $x\in X_a$ supported in $\bigcup_jU_j$, $$a(x)^2=\sum_j a(P_{U_j}x)^2.$$
  - Explanation: Independent finite atomic products and coordinate approximation give an exact gauge square sum across incomparable descendant subtrees.
- **claim-pointwise-coefficient-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-pointwise)): For every represented atom, the exact identity is $$\label{stab:deficit-identity} 2L(A)=1-\sum_i c_i^2+D(A)+E(A).$$ In particular, $$\label{stab:coef:budget} D(A)+\sum_i(c_i-s_i)^2 =1+\sum_i c_i^2-2A(p)\le2L(A).$$ The full coefficient replacement satisfies $$\label{stab:toolkit:replacement} \max\{\\|A-A^\circ\\|,\\|Q_H(A-A^\circ)\\|\} \le\sqrt{E(A)}\le\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ Consequently, $$\label{stab:coef:exterior} \\|O(A)\\|\le\sqrt{2L(A)},\qquad \\|c(A)-s(A)\\|_2\le\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ At the gates one has $$\label{stab:coef:gate-coordinates} A(e_g)=c_g(A)+O(A)(e_g),\qquad \\|(O(A)(e_g))_g\\|_2\le\\|O(A)\\|.$$
  - Explanation: Expanding the coefficient square separates evaluation deficit into unused coefficient energy, test slack, and replacement error; exterior and gate estimates follow.
- **claim-head-conflict-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-head-conflict)): Two represented atoms $A,B$, tested against the same $p$, satisfy $$\label{stab:coef:head-conflict-eq} 2\sum_{g:s_g(A)s_g(B)<0}\|s_g(A)s_g(B)\|\le D(A)+D(B).$$ There are two direct proofs: one discards a suffix of the longer head, and the other splits the longer segment into two pieces.
  - Explanation: Opposite head-sum products are bounded by test slack, with either truncation or splitting providing the same comparison.
- **claim-original-coefficient-products** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-original-products)): For the original crossing coefficients, $$\label{stab:coef:original-products-eq} \sum_g(-c_g(A)c_g(B))_+ \le L(A)+L(B)+\sqrt{2L(A)}+\sqrt{2L(B)},$$ where $r_+=\max(r,0)$.
  - Explanation: Transferring the head-sign comparison to original coefficients adds square-root evaluation-deficit errors.
- **claim-finite-law-gate-mass-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-gate-bound)): Let $A$ have a finite law of represented atoms and suppose $\mathbb E L(A)\le k$, where $k\ge0$. Write $$\beta=\\|\mathbb E c(A)\\|_2,\qquad M_c=(\mathbb E\|c_g(A)\|)_g,\qquad M_s=(\mathbb E\|s_g(A)\|)_g.$$ Then $$\begin{aligned} \\|\mathbb E s(A)\\|_2&\le\beta+\sqrt{2k}, \label{stab:coef:mean-head}\\ \\|M_s\\|_2^2&\le(\beta+\sqrt{2k})^2+4k, \label{stab:coef:absolute-head}\\ \\|M_c\\|_2&\le\sqrt{(\beta+\sqrt{2k})^2+4k}+\sqrt{2k}, \label{stab:coef:via-head}\\ \\|M_c\\|_2^2&\le\beta^2+4k+4\sqrt{2k}. \label{stab:coef:via-products} \end{aligned}$$ The last inequality is obtained directly from the original products, whereas the preceding one first controls the signs of the head sums.
  - Explanation: Finite expectations convert head conflicts into absolute-mass estimates, distinguishing head replacement from direct product transfer.
- **claim-rooted-tail-assembly** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-sign-rooted)): Let $G$ be a finite antichain and, for each $g\in G$, let $$f_g=\sum_j a_{g,j}d_{[g,w_{g,j}]},\qquad M_g=\sum_j\|a_{g,j}\|,$$ where every sum is finite. Then $$\label{stab:sign:absolute-masses} \left\\|\sum_g f_g\right\\|\le\\|(M_g)_g\\|_2.$$ If the nonzero coefficients have one common sign for each fixed $g$, then $$\label{stab:sign:rooted-equality} \left\\|\sum_g f_g\right\\| =\left(\sum_g\|f_g(e_g)\|^2\right)^{1/2}.$$ Consequently, adding another rooted mixture with the same sign at each $g$ cannot decrease the norm.* *In particular, for a finite law of represented atoms, put $M_c=(\mathbb E\|c_g(A)\|)_g$. For any real random variable $\theta$ with $\|\theta\|\le1$, $$\label{stab:coef:tail-assembly-eq} \\|\mathbb E(\theta C(A))\\|\le\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ If the law is an equal mixture of two laws with expectations $\mathbb E_+,\mathbb E_-$, then $$\label{stab:coef:conditional-assembly} \\|\mathbb E_\pm C(A)\\| \le\\|(\mathbb E_\pm\|c_g(A)\|)_g\\|_2\le2\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ The same estimates pass to norm limits of finite rooted mixtures when their absolute-mass vectors converge in $\ell_2$. In particular, countably many roots are permitted when their masses are square summable and the series at each root is absolutely summable.
  - Explanation: Rooted segment mixtures are controlled by their Euclidean absolute masses; common signs give exact recovery from root coordinates and conditional laws incur the stated mass loss.
- **claim-weighted-endpoint-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-weighted)): Let $z_1,\ldots,z_m\in B_Z$ and let $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_m>0$ with $\sum_i\lambda_i=1$. Put $$z=\sum_i\lambda_i z_i,\qquad \delta_i=1-z_i(p),\qquad \delta=1-z(p)=\sum_i\lambda_i\delta_i, \qquad \eta=\\|Q_Hz\\|.$$ Then, for each $i$, $$\label{stab:weighted-bound} \\|Q_Hz_i\\| \le\lambda_i^{-1} \sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2\delta})^2+4\delta} +\sqrt{2\delta_i}.$$ All deficits are nonnegative, and $\delta_i\le\delta/\lambda_i$. For two equally weighted endpoints this gives $$\label{stab:nonzero-midpoint} \\|Q_Hz_\pm\\| \le2\sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2\delta})^2+4\delta}+2\sqrt\delta.$$
  - Explanation: A common nearly norming head test bounds every endpoint tail using the mixture tail, evaluation deficits, and the endpoint's weight.
- **claim-bounded-multiplier-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-bounded-multiplier)): Let $A$ be a finite random represented atom, and let $\nu$ be a real random variable on the same probability space with $\|\nu\|\le1$. Set $x=\mathbb EA$, $u=\mathbb E(\nu A)$, $\delta=1-x(p)$, and $\eta=\\|Q_Hx\\|$. Then $$\label{stab:bounded-multiplier-bound} \\|Q_Hu\\| \le\sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2\delta})^2+4\delta}+\sqrt{2\delta} \le\eta+(2+2\sqrt2)\sqrt\delta.$$ Consequently, whenever $x,u\in Z$ satisfy $x\pm u\in B_Z$, $$\label{stab:symmetric-tail} \\|Q_Hu\\|\le\\|Q_Hx\\|+(2+2\sqrt2)\sqrt{1-x(p)}.$$
  - Explanation: Signed recovery extends to any bounded multiplier on the same finite probability space; independence from the represented atom is unnecessary.
- **claim-supported-midpoint-endpoint-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-supported-pair)): Let $z_+,z_-\in B_Z$. If $z=(z_++z_-)/2$ is supported on $H$ and $\\|z\\|\ge1-\varepsilon$, where $0<\varepsilon<1$, then $$\label{stab:supported-bound} \\|Q_Hz_\pm\\|\le(2+2\sqrt6)\sqrt\varepsilon.$$ More generally, let $R>0$, $d\ge0$, and $h_+,h_-\in Z$ satisfy $\\|h_\pm\\|\le R$, with $h_++h_-$ supported on $H$. If a common head test $p$, $\rho(p)=1$, satisfies $h_\pm(p)\ge(1-d)R$, then $$\label{stab:common-test-bound} \\|Q_Hh_\pm\\|\le R(2\sqrt{6d}+\sqrt{2d}).$$
  - Explanation: A nearly unit head-supported midpoint controls both endpoint tails; separate common-test deficits give the more general radius-dependent estimate.
- **claim-original-crossing-endpoint-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-original-crossing)): Let $z_1,z_2\in B_Z$, $z=(z_1+z_2)/2$, and let $H$ be finite ancestral. For a head test $p$, put $\delta=1-z(p)$ and $\kappa=\\|(z(e_g))_{g\in G_H}\\|_2$. Then $$\label{stab:gate-original-bound} \\|Q_Hz_i\\|\le2\kappa+(6\sqrt2+6)\sqrt\delta.$$ In particular, choosing a norming head test gives $$\label{stab:original-crossing-bound} \\|Q_Hz_i\\| \le2\\|Q_Hz\\|+(6\sqrt2+6)\sqrt{1-\\|P_Hz\\|} \le2\\|Q_Hz\\|+15\sqrt{1-\\|P_Hz\\|}.$$
  - Explanation: Keeping original crossing coefficients separates the wholly exterior error and controls endpoint tails using pooled gate coordinates and head deficit.
- **claim-small-center-tail-lens** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-small-center-tail)): Let $0<e<1/4$, let $x\in Z$ satisfy $\\|x\\|=1$, and let $H$ be finite ancestral with $\\|Q_Hx\\|\le e$. Suppose $p=P_Hp$, $\rho(p)=1$, and $x(p)\ge1-e$. For every $u\in\ker P_H$, $$\label{stab:small-center-implication} \max\{\\|x+u\\|,\\|x-u\\|\}\le1+e \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|u\\|\le25\sqrt e.$$
  - Explanation: At a unit center with a small exterior tail and an accurate supported test, a slightly enlarged lens cannot contain a large pure-tail displacement.
- **claim-stability-averaged-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-averaged-gain)): For either coordinate model, $$\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge t^2/1024\qquad(0<t<1).$$ For every $t>0$ and every $0<e<1/4$ with $25\sqrt e<t$, one also has $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge e/2$.
  - Explanation: Endpoint stability yields a quadratic averaged gain and a parameterized positive gain when the small-center-tail estimate excludes the perturbation.
- **claim-norm-weighted-endpoint-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-molw-average)): Let $A\subset T_\infty$ be finite and ancestral, let $x\in B_\infty$ be supported in $A$, and let $z\in\ker P_A$. Write $$r=\\|x\\|,\qquad L_+=\\|x+z\\|,\qquad L_-=\\|x-z\\|, \qquad L=\frac{L_++L_-}{2}.$$ Then $L_+,L_-\ge r$ and $$\label{molw:lens} \\|z\\|\le C\sqrt{L(L-r)},\qquad C=\sqrt2+\sqrt6.$$
  - Explanation: Weighting endpoint atom laws by their actual norms cancels the pooled tail and recovers the displacement through a signed expectation.
- **claim-norm-weighted-all-radius-modulus** (bound, [source](#stmt-molw-modulus)): For every $t>0$, put $$\label{molw:rho} \omega(t)=\frac{\sqrt{1+4(t/C)^2}-1}{2},\qquad C=\sqrt2+\sqrt6.$$ Then the averaged asymptotic modulus of $B_\infty$ satisfies $\widehat\delta_{B_\infty}(t)\ge\omega(t)/2>0$. The bound holds when the cofinite infimum allows every perturbation direction of norm at least one.
  - Explanation: Solving the norm-weighted average inequality and approximating the center gives a positive averaged modulus at every positive radius.
- **claim-approximate-conditional-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-mola-conditional)): Let $H$ be finite and ancestor-closed, $x=P_Hx$, and $x+h,x-h\in B_X$, with arbitrary $h\in X$. Suppose $0<d<1$ and $\\|x\\|\ge1-d$. For every $\eta>0$, choose finite represented-atom laws with means $z_\pm$ such that $\\|z_\pm-(x\pm h)\\|\le\eta$. Such laws exist, and every such pair gives $$\label{mola:conditional-prelimit} \\|Q_Hh\\|\le\eta+2\sqrt{d+\eta} +2\sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2(d+\eta)})^2+4(d+\eta)}.$$ Consequently, $$\\|Q_Hh\\|\le(2+2\sqrt6)\sqrt d.$$
  - Explanation: Approximate endpoint laws retain a pooled tail error and separate replacement errors; their limit controls arbitrary displacement tails in the forest.
- **claim-closed-hull-signed-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-mol-closed-hull-signed)): In the forest space $X=J^*$, let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$, $y=Q_Hy$, and $x\pm y\in B_X$. Put $d=1-\\|x\\|$. For every $\varepsilon>0$, the finite atomic approximation argument gives $$\label{mol:closed-hull-error} \\|y\\|\le e_\varepsilon+ \sqrt{e_\varepsilon^2+4(d+\varepsilon)},\qquad e_\varepsilon=\varepsilon+\sqrt{2(d+\varepsilon)}.$$ Consequently $\\|y\\|\le(\sqrt2+\sqrt6)\sqrt d$.
  - Explanation: Keeping the endpoint sign in finite atomic approximations recovers the displacement directly and avoids conditional absolute-mass loss.
- **claim-unscaled-common-head-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-molb-unscaled-tail)): Let $\delta>0$ and $x\in X$ satisfy $\\|x\\|=1$. There are a finite ancestral head $H$ and a test $p=P_Hp\in J$ such that $$\label{molb:unscaled-head} \rho(p)\le1,\qquad x(p)\ge1-\delta,\qquad \\|Q_Hx\\|\le\delta.$$ For this same head and every $y\in X$ satisfying $\\|x+y\\|,\\|x-y\\|\le1+\delta$, put $h=6\delta+\delta^2$. Then $$\label{molb:unscaled-bound} \max\{\\|Q_H(x+y)\\|,\\|Q_H(x-y)\\|,\\|Q_Hy\\|\} \le F(\delta):= \delta+\sqrt{2h}+2\sqrt{(2\delta+\sqrt h)^2+2h}.$$ In particular, for every $\varepsilon>0$ one can choose $\delta>0$ independently of $x$ so that this head satisfies $\\|Q_Hy\\|<\varepsilon$ for every such $y$.
  - Explanation: A head fixed from the unit center and tolerance controls both endpoint tails and every displacement tail in the enlarged lens through an unscaled joint budget.
- **claim-original-coefficient-exact-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-exact-tail)): Let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$, $P_Hz=0$, and $\\|x\pm z\\|\le1$. Put $d=1-\\|x\\|\in[0,1]$ and $$\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(d)=2\bigl(\sqrt{2d}+\sqrt{6d+4\sqrt{2d}}\bigr) \qquad(d\ge0).$$ The original coefficients give both $$\begin{aligned} \\|z\\|&\le(\sqrt{12}+2\sqrt2)\sqrt d, \label{stab:coef:square-root}\\ \\|z\\|&\le\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(d). \label{stab:coef:fourth-root} \end{aligned}$$ More generally, if $0\le d_0<1$ and $\\|x\\|\ge1-d_0$, the right side of <a href="#stab:coef:fourth-root" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:fourth-root">[stab:coef:fourth-root]</a> may be replaced by $\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(d_0)$.
  - Explanation: Head-mass transfer yields a square-root bound; original-product transfer yields a weaker fourth-root bound with a separate conditional-recovery loss.
- **claim-signed-original-product-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-mola-actual)): In the forest space $X$, suppose $H$ is finite and ancestor-closed, $x=P_Hx$, $y=Q_Hy$, and $\\|x+y\\|,\\|x-y\\|\le1$. Then $$\\|y\\|\le g(1-\\|x\\|),\qquad g(s)=\sqrt{2s}+\sqrt{6s+4\sqrt{2s}}\quad(s\ge0).$$
  - Explanation: Signed recovery removes the conditional factor from the original-product mass estimate while retaining its fourth-root behavior.
- **claim-original-coefficient-unit-head-radius** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-unit-head-lens)): If $x=P_Hx$ has norm one, $P_Hy=0$, and $\\|x\pm y\\|\le R$ for $R\ge1$, then $$\label{stab:coef:unit-head-lens-eq} \\|y\\|\le(\sqrt{12}+2\sqrt2)\sqrt{R(R-1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Scaling the exact coefficient estimate gives a radial-deficit bound for a unit finite-head center and a pure-tail displacement.
- **claim-completed-atomic-approximate-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-approximate-tail)): Let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$ have norm one, and $P_Hy=0$. For $d>0$, the assumptions $\\|x\pm y\\|\le1+d$ imply $$\label{stab:coef:approximate-tail-eq} \\|y\\|\le(1+d)(7\sqrt2+4)\sqrt d.$$ In particular, $\\|y\\|\le32\sqrt d$ when $0<d\le1$.
  - Explanation: Direct finite atomic approximation keeps completion errors in the cancellation budget; the simplified estimate requires the additional stated radius restriction.
- **claim-original-products-common-head-lens** (bound, [source](#stmt-molb-prop-original-lens)): For $0<\delta<1$ and every unit $x\in X$, one can choose a finite ancestor-closed $H$ such that $\\|x\pm y\\|\le1+\delta$ implies $$\\|Qy\\|\le\eta(\delta):=(1+\delta) \left\{2\sqrt{(2\delta+\sqrt{6\delta})^2 +4(3\delta+\sqrt{6\delta})} +2\sqrt{6\delta}+\delta\right\}.$$ In particular $\eta(\delta)\to0$ as $\delta\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: The original-product method fixes a head before the displacement and gives uniform tail decay for enlarged lenses at unit forest centers.
- **claim-near-unit-actual-center-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-molx-actual-center-fourth)): For $0<\lambda<1$ and $x\in B_X$ with $\\|x\\|\ge1-\lambda$, there is a finite ancestral set $H$, depending only on $x$ and $\lambda$, such that every $y\in X$ with $x\pm y\in B_X$ satisfies $$\label{molx:actual-center-fourth-bound} \\|Q_Hy\\|\le G_{\mathrm{orig}}(\lambda) :=\lambda+2\sqrt{6\lambda} +2\sqrt{(2\lambda+\sqrt{6\lambda})^2 +12\lambda+4\sqrt{6\lambda}}.$$ In particular $G_{\mathrm{orig}}(\lambda)\to0$ as $\lambda\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: A head depending only on the near-unit center and its deficit controls every displacement in the unit lens through the original-coefficient budget.
- **claim-coefficient-modulus-conversions** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-modulus-conversions)): For $0<t<1$, each of the following conclusions holds.* 1. *If $e>0$ satisfies $$(\sqrt{12}+2\sqrt2)\sqrt{(1+e)e}<t/2,$$ then $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge e/4$.* 2. *If $0<c<1$, $R=1+4c$, and $R\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(5c/R)<t$, then $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge c$.* 3. *If $d>0$, $3d<1$, and $(1+2d)\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(3d)<t$, then $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge d/2$.* 4. *The completed atomic-ball route gives $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge t^2/16384$.
  - Explanation: The listed conversions retain the distinct endpoint lower bounds, normalization losses, and parameter restrictions of their coefficient-based arguments.
- **claim-conditional-prefix-modulus** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-coef-conditional-modulus)): For $0<t<1$, set $\delta=(t/25)^2$. If $x_0=P_Hx_0$ and $y$ are unit vectors, $P_Hy=0$, and both are finitely supported, then $$\label{stab:coef:finite-strict} \max\{\\|x_0+ty\\|,\\|x_0-ty\\|\}>1+\delta.$$ For general unit $y\in\ker P_H$, the same inequality holds with $\ge$ in place of $>$. Moreover, $$\label{stab:coef:conditional-modulus-eq} \widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge t^2/2500.$$
  - Explanation: A separate conditional exterior budget gives strict finite endpoint growth; completion preserves a non-strict inequality and yields the stated averaged modulus.
- **claim-fractional-matching-capacity** (bound, [source](#stmt-molb-lem-matching)): Fix a primal unit vector $z$ supported on $H$, and a finite probability distribution of represented atoms $A_\omega=\sum_Ic_{\omega I}\mathbf1_I$, with probabilities $p_\omega$. Write $$b_{\omega I}=z(I),\qquad L_\omega=1-\sum_Ib_{\omega I}^2,\qquad B_\omega=\sum_Ib_{\omega I}Q\mathbf1_I.$$ At each gate let $P_g$ and $M_g$ be the positive and absolute negative coefficient masses in $\mathbb E B$. Then $$\label{molb:eq:matching-capacity} \sum_g\min(P_g,M_g)^2\le\mathbb E L.$$
  - Explanation: A finite fractional matching charges opposite crossing supplies to the individual outcomes' available splitting gains.
- **claim-matching-common-head-lens** (bound, [source](#stmt-molb-prop-matching-lens)): For $0<h<1/4$ and every unit $x\in X$, there is a finite ancestor-closed $H$, depending only on $x,h$, such that $\\|x\pm y\\|\le1+h$ implies $$\label{molb:eq:matching-lens} \\|Qy\\|\le h+(1+h) \bigl[h+2\sqrt{6h}+2(2h+3\sqrt{6h})\bigr].$$
  - Explanation: Matching capacity bounds pooled absolute crossing mass and then conditional recovery controls the whole enlarged lens using a head selected beforehand.
- **claim-cross-law-minimum-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-molx-minimum-tail)): Let $X=J^*$ be the finite-height forest space defined by <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a>, with the atomic and polar norm identifications of Proposition <a href="#presentation:identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:identity">31</a>. For a finite ancestor-closed set $A\subset\mathcal F$, let $P_A$ be coordinate restriction on $X$ and let $Q_A=I-P_A$. If $$P_Ax=x,\qquad Q_Ay=y,\qquad \\|x+y\\|,\\|x-y\\|\le1,$$ then, with $d=1-\\|x\\|$, $$\label{molx:minimum-conclusion} \\|y\\|\le(\sqrt6+2\sqrt2)\sqrt d\le6\sqrt d.$$
  - Explanation: Comparing samples from distinct endpoint laws bounds the smaller absolute mass in each cone, giving a forest tail estimate.
- **claim-ordered-sign-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-molc-oriented-lens)): Let $A$ be a nonempty finite ancestral set and let $x,y\in B_\infty$ with $x=P_Ax$ and $P_Ay=0$. For every $\varepsilon\ge0$, $$\max\{\\|x+y\\|,\\|x-y\\|\}\le1, \qquad \\|x\\|\ge1-\varepsilon \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|y\\|\le (\sqrt2+\sqrt{10})\sqrt\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Keeping only an ordered orientation of head-sign conflicts gives the stated larger tail constant in the infinite-height coordinate predual.
- **claim-target-sign-tail-and-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-molb-prop-target-signs)): Suppose $Px=x$, $Qy=y$, and $\\|x\pm y\\|\le1$. Then $$\label{molb:eq:target-sign-bound} \\|y\\|\le(2\sqrt2+2\sqrt6)\sqrt{1-\\|x\\|} \le8\sqrt{1-\\|x\\|}.$$ Consequently, if $\\|p\pm u_j\\|\le1$ and $\\|u_i-u_j\\|\ge\varepsilon>0$ for all distinct $i,j$ in an infinite sequence, then $$\label{molb:eq:target-separated} \\|p\\|\le1-\varepsilon^2/256.$$
  - Explanation: Signs determined from the target cone norms control the forest tail and imply a quantitative center deficit for infinite separated lens families.
- **claim-preferred-parent-sign-lens** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-sign-parent-lens)): Let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$ with $\\|x\\|=1$, and $u\in\ker P_H$. For every $L>1$, $$\label{stab:sign:parent-lens-bound} \max\{\\|x+u\\|,\\|x-u\\|\}<L \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|u\\|\le 3\left(\sqrt{L^2-1} +2\sqrt{\frac{1-L^{-2}}2}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Preferred signs at last head vertices isolate a common-sign retained tail; replacement and minority mass bound the remainder.
- **claim-preferred-parent-modulus** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-stab-sign-parent-modulus)): Let $b_L=\sqrt{L^2-1}+2\sqrt{(1-L^{-2})/2}$ for $L>1$. If $0<t<1$ and $L$ is chosen so that $3b_L<t$, then $$\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge (L-1)/8>0.$$
  - Explanation: Choosing a radius whose preferred-parent tail allowance is smaller than the perturbation gives a positive averaged midpoint gain.
- **claim-forest-preferred-parent-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-sign-forest-parent-lens)): In the forest space $X$, let $h=P_Hh$, $P_Hu=0$, and $h+u,h-u\in B_X$. Then $$\label{stab:sign:forest-parent-bound} \\|u\\|\le3(\sqrt2+2)\sqrt{1-\\|h\\|}.$$
  - Explanation: The preferred-parent construction passes through finite atomic approximations to give a forest unit-lens deficit estimate.
- **claim-first-exit-majority-lens** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-sign-exit-lens)): Let $x=P_Hx$, $\\|x\\|=1$, and $y\in\ker P_H$. Put $C_{\mathrm{ex}}=2+3\sqrt2$. For every $\delta>0$, with $\eta=\delta/(1+\delta)$, $$\label{stab:sign:exit-lens-bound} \max\{\\|x+y\\|,\\|x-y\\|\}\le1+\delta \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \frac{\\|y\\|}{1+\delta}\le3C_{\mathrm{ex}}\sqrt\eta.$$
  - Explanation: Majority signs at individual first exterior vertices separate wholly exterior coefficients from wrong-sign crossing mass in the infinite-tree model.
- **claim-first-exit-quadratic-modulus** (bound, [source](#stmt-stab-sign-exit-modulus)): For $0<t<1$ and $C_{\mathrm{ex}}=2+3\sqrt2$, $$\widehat\delta_{B_\infty}(t)\ge\frac{t^2}{576C_{\mathrm{ex}}^2}.$$
  - Explanation: The first-exit lens estimate excludes large pure-tail perturbations at an enlarged radius and yields a quadratic averaged gain.
- **claim-maximal-gain-vanishing-lens** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stab-trim-vanishing-lens)): There are no sequences of finite ancestral sets $H_n$, finite-head unit vectors $x_n=P_{H_n}x_n$, and finitely supported $z_n\in\ker P_{H_n}$ such that $$\max\{\\|x_n+z_n\\|,\\|x_n-z_n\\|\}\longrightarrow1, \qquad \liminf_n\\|z_n\\|>0.$$ The same assertion holds with $z_n$ in the completed tail spaces.
  - Explanation: Maximal-gain trimming rules out nonvanishing tails when finite-head unit lenses converge to unit radius, including completed tails.
- **claim-trimming-positive-modulus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stab-trim-modulus)): For the coordinate segment space, $\widehat\delta_{B_\infty}(t)>0$ for every $0<t<1$.
  - Explanation: The vanishing-lens contradiction proves positive averaged midpoint modulus in the infinite-height coordinate space.
- **claim-finite-head-close-pair** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mol-finite-head-pair)): Let $P$ be a bounded finite-rank projection on a Banach space and let $(u_j)$ be an infinite bounded sequence. For every $\varepsilon>0$ there are distinct $i,j$ with $\\|P(u_i-u_j)\\|<\varepsilon$. If in addition $x\pm u_j\in R B$ and $\\|u_i-u_j\\|\ge\theta$ for all distinct indices, then $v=(u_i-u_j)/2$ satisfies $$\label{mol:half-difference} x\pm v\in R B,\qquad \\|Pv\\|<\varepsilon/2, \qquad \\|v\\|\ge\theta/2.$$
  - Explanation: Total boundedness of finite-rank images gives a close head pair; its half-difference remains in the lens and retains separation in norm.
- **claim-varying-head-sequential-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mola-sequential)): For each positive integer $n$, let $H_n$ be a finite ancestor-closed subset of the finite-height forest, and let $x_n,y_n\in X$ satisfy $$P_{H_n}x_n=x_n,\qquad P_{H_n}y_n=0,\qquad \\|x_n+y_n\\|,\\|x_n-y_n\\|\le1.$$ If $\\|x_n\\|\longrightarrow1$, then $\\|y_n\\|\longrightarrow0$. The heads and the heights of the trees meeting them may vary with $n$.
  - Explanation: Vanishing head deficit forces vanishing forest tails uniformly even as ancestral heads and component heights vary.
- **claim-uniform-sequential-midpoint-convexity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mola-uniform-sequential)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta>0$ such that, for every finite ancestor-closed $H$ and all $x=P_Hx$, $y=Q_Hy$ with $\\|x\pm y\\|\le1$ and $\\|x\\|>1-\delta$, one has $\\|y\\|<\varepsilon$. The given norm is asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex, with the maximum of the two endpoint norms in the definition.
  - Explanation: The sequential contradiction gives a head-independent near-unit lens implication and maximum-based asymptotic midpoint uniform convexity.
- **claim-separated-sequential-center-deficit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mola-separated-sequential)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta>0$ such that any infinite $\varepsilon$-separated family $(v_j)$ satisfying $x\pm v_j\in B_X$ has $\\|x\\|\le1-\delta$.
  - Explanation: An infinite uniformly separated family inside a symmetric unit lens forces a uniform deficit in the forest center norm.
- **claim-coordinate-null-limsup-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-molx-coordinate-null)): Let $x\in X$ and let $(z_j)\subset X$ satisfy $x\pm z_j\in B_X$ and $z_j(e_v)\to0$ for every forest vertex $v$. Then, with $C=\sqrt2+2\sqrt6<7$, $$\label{molx:coordinate-null-bound} \limsup_{j\to\infty}\\|z_j\\| \le C\sqrt{1-\\|x\\|}\le7\sqrt{1-\\|x\\|}.$$
  - Explanation: Coordinatewise-null displacements become small on each fixed finite head, so a separate-endpoint tail estimate controls their norm upper limit.
- **claim-separate-normalizations-positive-modulus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-molc-sequential-positive)): For each fixed $0<t<1$, the space $B_\infty$ satisfies $\widehat\delta_{B_\infty}(t)>0$.
  - Explanation: Separate normalization of the endpoint laws leaves a pooled tail that tends to zero; conditional stability contradicts the persistent perturbation norm.

Each point is a convex combination of at most $|H|+1$ such atoms. The full unit ball of $B_\\infty$ is the norm-closed convex hull of finite atoms.*\n\n*On $P_HB_\\infty$ the norm also equals the gauge $$\\inf\\left\\{\\sum_j\\lambda_j:\n   x=\\sum_j\\lambda_j A_j,\\quad \\lambda_j\\ge0,\\quad\n   A_j\\text{ atoms supported in }H\\right\\}.$$ In particular, a vector of norm strictly below one has such a finite representation with $\\sum_j\\lambda_j<1$. The gauge defined on $c_{00}(T_\\infty)$ by allowing all finite atoms has the same value. Its completion is therefore isometric to $B_\\infty$.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-foundation-order-convex",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a set of vertices whose intersection with every segment is empty or a segment. The coordinate projection $P_E$ is contractive on both $J$ and $X=J^*$. Its range on $J$ is the completion of $c_{00}(E)$ under the segment norm restricted to $E$, and $$\\|P_Ex\\|=\\|x|_{P_EJ}\\| \\qquad (x\\in X).$$ In particular these assertions apply to an ancestral set, its complement, a difference of nested ancestral sets, a descendant subtree, and an antichain. On an antichain the coordinate norm on either space is exactly the usual $\\ell_2$ norm.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-foundation-order-convex",

On an antichain the coordinate norm on either space is exactly the usual $\\ell_2$ norm.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-foundation-height-variants",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I$ be countable and let $(h_i)_{i\\in I}$ be nonnegative integers. On the disjoint forest $$\\mathcal F_{\\boldsymbol h}\n   =\\coprod_{i\\in I}(\\{i\\}\\times\\mathbb N^{\\le h_i})$$ define $J_{\\boldsymbol h}$ by the same disjoint-segment norm and set $X_{\\boldsymbol h}=J_{\\boldsymbol h}^*$. If $J(h)$ denotes the single-tree completion on $\\mathbb N^{\\le h}$, then $$J_{\\boldsymbol h}\n   =\\Big(\\bigoplus_{i\\in I}J(h_i)\\Big)_{\\ell_2},\n \\qquad\n X_{\\boldsymbol h}\n   =\\Big(\\bigoplus_{i\\in I}J(h_i)^*\\Big)_{\\ell_2}$$ isometrically under coordinate restriction. Both spaces are reflexive, and finite coordinate vectors are dense in each. The projection and complementary-subtree formulas remain valid for this forest.*\n\n*The convention of one tree with $n$ levels, including the root, for every $n\\ge1$ gives exactly $$J_{\\mathrm{levels}}=\\mathbb R\\oplus_2 J,\n \\qquad X_{\\mathrm{levels}}=\\mathbb R\\oplus_2 X.$$ Its component estimate is $\\|u\\|_2\\le\\rho(u)\\le\\sqrt n\\,\\|u\\|_2$, and a root-to-leaf path has $n-1$ extension steps.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-foundation-height-variants",

If $J(h)$ denotes the single-tree completion on $\\mathbb N^{\\le h}$, then $$J_{\\boldsymbol h}\n   =\\Big(\\bigoplus_{i\\in I}J(h_i)\\Big)_{\\ell_2},\n \\qquad\n X_{\\boldsymbol h}\n   =\\Big(\\bigoplus_{i\\in I}J(h_i)^*\\Big)_{\\ell_2}$$ isometrically under coordinate restriction. Both spaces are reflexive, and finite coordinate vectors are dense in each.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-foundation-bounded-tree",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose an infinite-dimensional real Banach space $(Z,\\|\\cdot\\|)$ contains, for every positive integer $k$, vectors $(u_s)_{s\\in\\mathbb N^{\\le k}}$ such that $$0<m\\le\\|u_s\\|\\le M,\n \\qquad \\|u_{s^\\frown j}-u_s\\|\\ge c>0,$$ where $m,M,c$ are independent of $k$, and for each nonterminal $s$ the sequence $(u_{s^\\frown j}-u_s)_j$ is weakly null against the whole continuous dual of $Z$. If $0<a\\le b<\\infty$ and $a\\|z\\|\\le N(z)\\le b\\|z\\|$, then $$\\overline\\delta_N\\!\\left(\\frac{ac}{bM}\\right)=0.$$ In particular the same conclusion holds at $ac/(2bM)$, and $Z$ admits no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-foundation-bounded-tree",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose an infinite-dimensional real Banach space $(Z,\\|\\cdot\\|)$ contains, for every positive integer $k$, vectors $(u_s)_{s\\in\\mathbb N^{\\le k}}$ such that $$0<m\\le\\|u_s\\|\\le M,\n \\qquad \\|u_{s^\\frown j}-u_s\\|\\ge c>0,$$ where $m,M,c$ are independent of $k$, and for each nonterminal $s$ the sequence $(u_{s^\\frown j}-u_s)_j$ is weakly null against the whole continuous dual of $Z$. If $0<a\\le b<\\infty$ and $a\\|z\\|\\le N(z)\\le b\\|z\\|$, then $$\\overline\\delta_N\\!\\left(\\frac{ac}{bM}\\right)=0.$$ In particular the same conclusion holds at $ac/(2bM)$, and $Z$ admits no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Suppose an infinite-dimensional real Banach space $(Z,\\|\\cdot\\|)$ contains, for every positive integer $k$, vectors $(u_s)_{s\\in\\mathbb N^{\\le k}}$ such that $$0<m\\le\\|u_s\\|\\le M,\n \\qquad \\|u_{s^\\frown j}-u_s\\|\\ge c>0,$$ where $m,M,c$ are independent of $k$, and for each nonterminal $s$ the sequence $(u_{s^\\frown j}-u_s)_j$ is weakly null against the whole continuous dual of $Z$.         "anchor": "stmt-binf-obstruction",
        "exact_quote": "The unit root-path vectors in $B_\\infty$ have unit child increments which are weakly null among the children of each fixed vertex.         "exact_quote": "Let $f=P_Hf$ and $v=Q_Hv$ be finite tests. Let $\\sigma_j$ be the opposite sign of that sum, and otherwise choose either sign.*\n\n*Suppose a finite exterior test $w$ satisfies $\\rho(w)\\le\\rho(v)$, and every root prefix in group $j$ has $w$-sum in $\\sigma_j[0,c_j]$. Then $$\\label{clip:crossing-bound}\n \\rho(f+sw)^2\\le\\rho(f)^2+3s^2\\rho(v)^2.$$ The same conclusion holds with a strictly greater suffix threshold, provided the small-crossing case includes equality.         "anchor": "stmt-clip-selection-prop",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $x=P_Hx$, $\\|x\\|=1$, $y\\in\\ker P_H$, and $v=Q_Hv$, $\\rho(v)\\le1$, and $y(v)>\\|y\\|/2$. For every $s>0$, each of the following constructions produces an exterior test $w$ with $$|y(w)|>\\|y\\|/8,\\qquad \\rho(w)\\le1,\n \\qquad \\rho(f+sw)^2\\le1+3s^2,$$ where $f=P_Hf$ norms $x$ and the inward signs are chosen with full cone budgets $q_g$.*\n\n1.  *Take the positive and negative cumulative parts globally, choose one by its total pairing, then retain one of the two groups of cones having the same prescribed inward sign.*\n\n2.  *Form all four combinations of a cumulative positive/negative part and a prescribed inward-sign group, and select one by its pairing.*\n\n3.  *Choose a cumulative part separately in each cone, orient it inward, then retain the cones on which the resulting pairings have one common sign.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-clip-frontier-prop",
        "exact_quote": "For finite $f=P_Hf$ and $v=Q_Hv$ with $\\rho(f),\\rho(v)\\le1$, and every $s>0$, there is a finitely supported gate vector $a$ such that $$\\label{clip:frontier-tests}\n \\rho(a)\\le1,\\qquad \\rho(a\\pm v)\\le2,\\qquad\n \\rho\\bigl(f+s(a\\pm v)\\bigr)^2\\le1+12s^2.$$ For either $Z=B_\\infty$ or the finite-height forest space, this selected-endpoint method gives $$\\label{clip:frontier-modulus}\n \\widehat\\delta_Z(t)\\ge\n \\frac{\\sqrt{1+t^2/48}-1}{4}\\qquad(0<t<1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-clip-frontier-prop",

        "anchor": "stmt-dir-primal-add-independent-threshold",
        "exact_quote": "On either the finite-height forest or the infinite tree, let $f=P_Hf$, $v=Q_Hv$ be finite tests with $\\rho(f),\\rho(v)\\le1$. Let finitely many $a_g\\ge0$ satisfy $\\sum_ga_g^2\\le1$ and $|v([g,u])|\\le a_g$ on every root prefix. Fix $\\alpha>0$. For $a_g>0$, choose the shortest attached head suffix of magnitude at least $\\alpha a_g$, if one exists, and let $\\tau_g$ be its sign; otherwise put $\\tau_g=1$. Strict thresholds may be used instead. Set $$a=-\\sum_g\\tau_ga_ge_g,\\qquad h_+=a+v,\\qquad h_-=a-v.$$ For $h\\in\\{a,h_+,h_-\\}$ and $0<c\\le\\alpha/2$, $$\\label{dir:primal:add:independent-cost}\n \\rho(h)\\le2,\\qquad\n \\rho(f+ch)^2\\le1+4c\\alpha+4c^2.$$ Both the full cone norms $q_g$ and the prefix maxima $m_g$ may be used for $a_g$.",

      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-endpoint",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le d<1$, let $H$ be finite and ancestral, and suppose $x=P_Hx$, $\\|x\\|\\ge1-d$, and $\\|x\\pm y\\|\\le1$. Fix a head norming test $p$ with $\\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=\\|x\\|$, and a finite ancestral set $K\\supset H$. For each $\\tau\\in\\{-1,1\\}$ choose any finite represented atom law with mean $P_K(x+\\tau y)$, and denote its expectation by $\\mathbb E_\\tau$. For an atom $A=\\sum_i a_i d_{S_i}$, put $b_i=p(S_i)$, $A^\\circ=\\sum_i b_i d_{S_i}$, and $L=1-\\sum_i b_i^2$. Then $$\\label{eq:endpoint-coefficients}\n \\mathbb E_\\tau\\!\\left[L+\\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2\\right]\\le4d,\n \\qquad \\mathbb E_\\tau\\|A-A^\\circ\\|\\le2\\sqrt d.$$ If $b(g)$ is the replaced crossing sum at gate $g$, put $m_\\tau(g)=\\mathbb E_\\tau|b(g)|$ and $m=(m_1+m_{-1})/2$. These nonnegative vectors satisfy $$\\label{eq:endpoint-gates}\n m_\\tau\\le2m\\text{ coordinatewise},\\qquad\\|m\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{6d}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-endpoint",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le d<1$, let $H$ be finite and ancestral, and suppose $x=P_Hx$, $\\|x\\|\\ge1-d$, and $\\|x\\pm y\\|\\le1$. For an atom $A=\\sum_i a_i d_{S_i}$, put $b_i=p(S_i)$, $A^\\circ=\\sum_i b_i d_{S_i}$, and $L=1-\\sum_i b_i^2$. Then $$\\label{eq:endpoint-coefficients}\n \\mathbb E_\\tau\\!\\left[L+\\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2\\right]\\le4d,\n \\qquad \\mathbb E_\\tau\\|A-A^\\circ\\|\\le2\\sqrt d.$$ If $b(g)$ is the replaced crossing sum at gate $g$, put $m_\\tau(g)=\\mathbb E_\\tau|b(g)|$ and $m=(m_1+m_{-1})/2$. These nonnegative vectors satisfy $$\\label{eq:endpoint-gates}\n m_\\tau\\le2m\\text{ coordinatewise},\\qquad\\|m\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{6d}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mol-prop-conditional-joint",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#mol:prop:joint-budget\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mol:prop:joint-budget\">1</a>, assume in addition that $P_Hy=0$ and $r=\\|x\\|>0$, and put $\\Delta=R^2-r^2$. Fix a head norming test $p=P_Hp$ with $\\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=r$. For every finite ancestral $K\\supset H$ and each sign $\\tau\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, choose any finite law of represented vectors $$A=\\sum_i a_i d_{S_i},\\qquad \\sum_i a_i^2\\le R^2,\n \\qquad \\mathbb E_\\tau A=P_K(x+\\tau y),$$ where each represented family consists of disjoint segments. Put $$b_i=r p(S_i),\\qquad A^\\circ=\\sum_i b_i d_{S_i},\\qquad\n V=\\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2,\\qquad L=r^2-\\sum_i b_i^2.$$ These laws exist by the finite atomic-ball formula. For every such choice, the replacement satisfies $$\\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint}\n \\mathbb E_\\tau(V+L)\\le\\Delta,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E_\\tau\\|Q_H(A-A^\\circ)\\|\\le\\sqrt\\Delta.$$ At each gate $g$, let $b(g)$ be the replaced crossing coefficient, or zero when there is no crossing segment. The mixed absolute crossing vector $m(g)=\\bigl(\\mathbb E_1|b(g)|+\\mathbb E_{-1}|b(g)|\\bigr)/2$ satisfies $\\|m\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{2\\Delta}$. Consequently a separate endpoint argument, followed by ancestral exhaustion, yields $$\\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint-tail}\n \\|y\\|\\le(1+2\\sqrt2)\\sqrt\\Delta.$$ If the same two nonnegative errors are bounded separately, without retaining their sum, the corresponding calculation gives $$\\label{mol:eq:strict-gauge-tail}\n \\|y\\|\\le(1+2\\sqrt3)\\sqrt\\Delta.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For every such choice, the replacement satisfies $$\\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint}\n \\mathbb E_\\tau(V+L)\\le\\Delta,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E_\\tau\\|Q_H(A-A^\\circ)\\|\\le\\sqrt\\Delta.$$ At each gate $g$, let $b(g)$ be the replaced crossing coefficient, or zero when there is no crossing segment.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-presentation-identity",
        "exact_quote": "The coordinate identity satisfies $a(x)=q(x)=\\|x\\|$ on $c_{00}(\\mathcal F)$ and extends to surjective linear isometries $$X_a=X_q=X=J^*.$$ The finite unit balls and completed atomic ball are therefore exactly those of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:finite-hull\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:finite-hull\">7</a> and Corollary <a href=\"#cor:closed-atomic-ball\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:closed-atomic-ball\">8</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $(r_j)$ be a finite or countable family of incomparable vertices, and put $U_j=\\{v:r_j\\preceq v\\}$. For $x\\in X_a$ supported in $\\bigcup_jU_j$, $$a(x)^2=\\sum_j a(P_{U_j}x)^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-pointwise",
        "exact_quote": "For every represented atom, the exact identity is $$\\label{stab:deficit-identity}\n 2L(A)=1-\\sum_i c_i^2+D(A)+E(A).$$ In particular, $$\\label{stab:coef:budget}\n D(A)+\\sum_i(c_i-s_i)^2\n =1+\\sum_i c_i^2-2A(p)\\le2L(A).$$ The full coefficient replacement satisfies $$\\label{stab:toolkit:replacement}\n \\max\\{\\|A-A^\\circ\\|,\\|Q_H(A-A^\\circ)\\|\\}\n \\le\\sqrt{E(A)}\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{stab:coef:exterior}\n \\|O(A)\\|\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)},\\qquad\n \\|c(A)-s(A)\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ At the gates one has $$\\label{stab:coef:gate-coordinates}\n A(e_g)=c_g(A)+O(A)(e_g),\\qquad\n \\|(O(A)(e_g))_g\\|_2\\le\\|O(A)\\|.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-pointwise",
          "exact_quote": "For every represented atom, the exact identity is $$\\label{stab:deficit-identity}\n 2L(A)=1-\\sum_i c_i^2+D(A)+E(A).$$ In particular, $$\\label{stab:coef:budget}\n D(A)+\\sum_i(c_i-s_i)^2\n =1+\\sum_i c_i^2-2A(p)\\le2L(A).$$ The full coefficient replacement satisfies $$\\label{stab:toolkit:replacement}\n \\max\\{\\|A-A^\\circ\\|,\\|Q_H(A-A^\\circ)\\|\\}\n \\le\\sqrt{E(A)}\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{stab:coef:exterior}\n \\|O(A)\\|\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)},\\qquad\n \\|c(A)-s(A)\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ At the gates one has $$\\label{stab:coef:gate-coordinates}\n A(e_g)=c_g(A)+O(A)(e_g),\\qquad\n \\|(O(A)(e_g))_g\\|_2\\le\\|O(A)\\|.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every represented atom, the exact identity is $$\\label{stab:deficit-identity}\n 2L(A)=1-\\sum_i c_i^2+D(A)+E(A).$$ In particular, $$\\label{stab:coef:budget}\n D(A)+\\sum_i(c_i-s_i)^2\n =1+\\sum_i c_i^2-2A(p)\\le2L(A).$$ The full coefficient replacement satisfies $$\\label{stab:toolkit:replacement}\n \\max\\{\\|A-A^\\circ\\|,\\|Q_H(A-A^\\circ)\\|\\}\n \\le\\sqrt{E(A)}\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{stab:coef:exterior}\n \\|O(A)\\|\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)},\\qquad\n \\|c(A)-s(A)\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ At the gates one has $$\\label{stab:coef:gate-coordinates}\n A(e_g)=c_g(A)+O(A)(e_g),\\qquad\n \\|(O(A)(e_g))_g\\|_2\\le\\|O(A)\\|.$$",
      "explanation": "Expanding the coefficient square separates evaluation deficit into unused coefficient energy, test slack, and replacement error; exterior and gate estimates follow.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-gate-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ have a finite law of represented atoms and suppose $\\mathbb E L(A)\\le k$, where $k\\ge0$. Write $$\\beta=\\|\\mathbb E c(A)\\|_2,\\qquad\n M_c=(\\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_g,\\qquad\n M_s=(\\mathbb E|s_g(A)|)_g.$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\mathbb E s(A)\\|_2&\\le\\beta+\\sqrt{2k},\n       \\label{stab:coef:mean-head}\\\\\n \\|M_s\\|_2^2&\\le(\\beta+\\sqrt{2k})^2+4k,\n       \\label{stab:coef:absolute-head}\\\\\n \\|M_c\\|_2&\\le\\sqrt{(\\beta+\\sqrt{2k})^2+4k}+\\sqrt{2k},\n       \\label{stab:coef:via-head}\\\\\n \\|M_c\\|_2^2&\\le\\beta^2+4k+4\\sqrt{2k}.\n       \\label{stab:coef:via-products}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last inequality is obtained directly from the original products, whereas the preceding one first controls the signs of the head sums.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-gate-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ have a finite law of represented atoms and suppose $\\mathbb E L(A)\\le k$, where $k\\ge0$. Write $$\\beta=\\|\\mathbb E c(A)\\|_2,\\qquad\n M_c=(\\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_g,\\qquad\n M_s=(\\mathbb E|s_g(A)|)_g.$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\mathbb E s(A)\\|_2&\\le\\beta+\\sqrt{2k},\n       \\label{stab:coef:mean-head}\\\\\n \\|M_s\\|_2^2&\\le(\\beta+\\sqrt{2k})^2+4k,\n       \\label{stab:coef:absolute-head}\\\\\n \\|M_c\\|_2&\\le\\sqrt{(\\beta+\\sqrt{2k})^2+4k}+\\sqrt{2k},\n       \\label{stab:coef:via-head}\\\\\n \\|M_c\\|_2^2&\\le\\beta^2+4k+4\\sqrt{2k}.\n       \\label{stab:coef:via-products}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last inequality is obtained directly from the original products, whereas the preceding one first controls the signs of the head sums.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $A$ have a finite law of represented atoms and suppose $\\mathbb E L(A)\\le k$, where $k\\ge0$. Write $$\\beta=\\|\\mathbb E c(A)\\|_2,\\qquad\n M_c=(\\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_g,\\qquad\n M_s=(\\mathbb E|s_g(A)|)_g.$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\mathbb E s(A)\\|_2&\\le\\beta+\\sqrt{2k},\n       \\label{stab:coef:mean-head}\\\\\n \\|M_s\\|_2^2&\\le(\\beta+\\sqrt{2k})^2+4k,\n       \\label{stab:coef:absolute-head}\\\\\n \\|M_c\\|_2&\\le\\sqrt{(\\beta+\\sqrt{2k})^2+4k}+\\sqrt{2k},\n       \\label{stab:coef:via-head}\\\\\n \\|M_c\\|_2^2&\\le\\beta^2+4k+4\\sqrt{2k}.\n       \\label{stab:coef:via-products}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last inequality is obtained directly from the original products, whereas the preceding one first controls the signs of the head sums.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stab-sign-rooted",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"stab:coef:tail-assembly\" label=\"stab:coef:tail-assembly\"></span>Let $G$ be a finite antichain and, for each $g\\in G$, let $$f_g=\\sum_j a_{g,j}d_{[g,w_{g,j}]},\\qquad\n M_g=\\sum_j|a_{g,j}|,$$ where every sum is finite. Then $$\\label{stab:sign:absolute-masses}\n \\left\\|\\sum_g f_g\\right\\|\\le\\|(M_g)_g\\|_2.$$ If the nonzero coefficients have one common sign for each fixed $g$, then $$\\label{stab:sign:rooted-equality}\n \\left\\|\\sum_g f_g\\right\\|\n =\\left(\\sum_g|f_g(e_g)|^2\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Consequently, adding another rooted mixture with the same sign at each $g$ cannot decrease the norm.*\n\n\n\n*In particular, for a finite law of represented atoms, put $M_c=(\\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_g$. For any real random variable $\\theta$ with $|\\theta|\\le1$, $$\\label{stab:coef:tail-assembly-eq}\n \\|\\mathbb E(\\theta C(A))\\|\\le\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ If the law is an equal mixture of two laws with expectations $\\mathbb E_+,\\mathbb E_-$, then $$\\label{stab:coef:conditional-assembly}\n \\|\\mathbb E_\\pm C(A)\\|\n \\le\\|(\\mathbb E_\\pm|c_g(A)|)_g\\|_2\\le2\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ The same estimates pass to norm limits of finite rooted mixtures when their absolute-mass vectors converge in $\\ell_2$. In particular, countably many roots are permitted when their masses are square summable and the series at each root is absolutely summable.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-sign-rooted",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"stab:coef:tail-assembly\" label=\"stab:coef:tail-assembly\"></span>Let $G$ be a finite antichain and, for each $g\\in G$, let $$f_g=\\sum_j a_{g,j}d_{[g,w_{g,j}]},\\qquad\n M_g=\\sum_j|a_{g,j}|,$$ where every sum is finite. Then $$\\label{stab:sign:absolute-masses}\n \\left\\|\\sum_g f_g\\right\\|\\le\\|(M_g)_g\\|_2.$$ If the nonzero coefficients have one common sign for each fixed $g$, then $$\\label{stab:sign:rooted-equality}\n \\left\\|\\sum_g f_g\\right\\|\n =\\left(\\sum_g|f_g(e_g)|^2\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Consequently, adding another rooted mixture with the same sign at each $g$ cannot decrease the norm.*\n\n\n\n*In particular, for a finite law of represented atoms, put $M_c=(\\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_g$. For any real random variable $\\theta$ with $|\\theta|\\le1$, $$\\label{stab:coef:tail-assembly-eq}\n \\|\\mathbb E(\\theta C(A))\\|\\le\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ If the law is an equal mixture of two laws with expectations $\\mathbb E_+,\\mathbb E_-$, then $$\\label{stab:coef:conditional-assembly}\n \\|\\mathbb E_\\pm C(A)\\|\n \\le\\|(\\mathbb E_\\pm|c_g(A)|)_g\\|_2\\le2\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ The same estimates pass to norm limits of finite rooted mixtures when their absolute-mass vectors converge in $\\ell_2$. In particular, countably many roots are permitted when their masses are square summable and the series at each root is absolutely summable.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"stab:coef:tail-assembly\" label=\"stab:coef:tail-assembly\"></span>Let $G$ be a finite antichain and, for each $g\\in G$, let $$f_g=\\sum_j a_{g,j}d_{[g,w_{g,j}]},\\qquad\n M_g=\\sum_j|a_{g,j}|,$$ where every sum is finite. Then $$\\label{stab:sign:absolute-masses}\n \\left\\|\\sum_g f_g\\right\\|\\le\\|(M_g)_g\\|_2.$$ If the nonzero coefficients have one common sign for each fixed $g$, then $$\\label{stab:sign:rooted-equality}\n \\left\\|\\sum_g f_g\\right\\|\n =\\left(\\sum_g|f_g(e_g)|^2\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Consequently, adding another rooted mixture with the same sign at each $g$ cannot decrease the norm.*\n\n\n\n*In particular, for a finite law of represented atoms, put $M_c=(\\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_g$. For any real random variable $\\theta$ with $|\\theta|\\le1$, $$\\label{stab:coef:tail-assembly-eq}\n \\|\\mathbb E(\\theta C(A))\\|\\le\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ If the law is an equal mixture of two laws with expectations $\\mathbb E_+,\\mathbb E_-$, then $$\\label{stab:coef:conditional-assembly}\n \\|\\mathbb E_\\pm C(A)\\|\n \\le\\|(\\mathbb E_\\pm|c_g(A)|)_g\\|_2\\le2\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ The same estimates pass to norm limits of finite rooted mixtures when their absolute-mass vectors converge in $\\ell_2$. In particular, countably many roots are permitted when their masses are square summable and the series at each root is absolutely summable.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $z_1,\\ldots,z_m\\in B_Z$ and let $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_m>0$ with $\\sum_i\\lambda_i=1$.         "anchor": "stmt-stab-bounded-multiplier",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a finite random represented atom, and let $\\nu$ be a real random variable on the same probability space with $|\\nu|\\le1$. Set $x=\\mathbb EA$, $u=\\mathbb E(\\nu A)$, $\\delta=1-x(p)$, and $\\eta=\\|Q_Hx\\|$. Then $$\\label{stab:bounded-multiplier-bound}\n \\|Q_Hu\\|\n \\le\\sqrt{(\\eta+\\sqrt{2\\delta})^2+4\\delta}+\\sqrt{2\\delta}\n \\le\\eta+(2+2\\sqrt2)\\sqrt\\delta.$$ Consequently, whenever $x,u\\in Z$ satisfy $x\\pm u\\in B_Z$, $$\\label{stab:symmetric-tail}\n \\|Q_Hu\\|\\le\\|Q_Hx\\|+(2+2\\sqrt2)\\sqrt{1-x(p)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-stab-supported-pair",
        "exact_quote": "Let $z_+,z_-\\in B_Z$. If $z=(z_++z_-)/2$ is supported on $H$ and $\\|z\\|\\ge1-\\varepsilon$, where $0<\\varepsilon<1$, then $$\\label{stab:supported-bound}\n \\|Q_Hz_\\pm\\|\\le(2+2\\sqrt6)\\sqrt\\varepsilon.$$ More generally, let $R>0$, $d\\ge0$, and $h_+,h_-\\in Z$ satisfy $\\|h_\\pm\\|\\le R$, with $h_++h_-$ supported on $H$. If a common head test $p$, $\\rho(p)=1$, satisfies $h_\\pm(p)\\ge(1-d)R$, then $$\\label{stab:common-test-bound}\n \\|Q_Hh_\\pm\\|\\le R(2\\sqrt{6d}+\\sqrt{2d}).$$",

        "exact_quote": "Let $z_1,z_2\\in B_Z$, $z=(z_1+z_2)/2$, and let $H$ be finite ancestral. For a head test $p$, put $\\delta=1-z(p)$ and $\\kappa=\\|(z(e_g))_{g\\in G_H}\\|_2$. Then $$\\label{stab:gate-original-bound}\n \\|Q_Hz_i\\|\\le2\\kappa+(6\\sqrt2+6)\\sqrt\\delta.$$ In particular, choosing a norming head test gives $$\\label{stab:original-crossing-bound}\n \\|Q_Hz_i\\|\n \\le2\\|Q_Hz\\|+(6\\sqrt2+6)\\sqrt{1-\\|P_Hz\\|}\n \\le2\\|Q_Hz\\|+15\\sqrt{1-\\|P_Hz\\|}.$$",

        "exact_quote": "Let $0<e<1/4$, let $x\\in Z$ satisfy $\\|x\\|=1$, and let $H$ be finite ancestral with $\\|Q_Hx\\|\\le e$. Suppose $p=P_Hp$, $\\rho(p)=1$, and $x(p)\\ge1-e$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-molw-average",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subset T_\\infty$ be finite and ancestral, let $x\\in B_\\infty$ be supported in $A$, and let $z\\in\\ker P_A$.         "anchor": "stmt-mola-conditional",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be finite and ancestor-closed, $x=P_Hx$, and $x+h,x-h\\in B_X$, with arbitrary $h\\in X$. Suppose $0<d<1$ and $\\|x\\|\\ge1-d$.       },

        "exact_quote": "In the forest space $X=J^*$, let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$, $y=Q_Hy$, and $x\\pm y\\in B_X$. Put $d=1-\\|x\\|$.       },

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\delta>0$ and $x\\in X$ satisfy $\\|x\\|=1$.         "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$, $P_Hz=0$, and $\\|x\\pm z\\|\\le1$.         "source_id": "PM-331.2"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-exact-tail",

        "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-modulus-conversions",
        "exact_quote": "For $0<t<1$, each of the following conclusions holds.*\n\n1.  *If $e>0$ satisfies $$(\\sqrt{12}+2\\sqrt2)\\sqrt{(1+e)e}<t/2,$$ then $\\widehat\\delta_Z(t)\\ge e/4$.*\n\n2.  *If $0<c<1$, $R=1+4c$, and $R\\omega_{\\mathrm{coef}}(5c/R)<t$, then $\\widehat\\delta_Z(t)\\ge c$.*\n\n3.  *If $d>0$, $3d<1$, and $(1+2d)\\omega_{\\mathrm{coef}}(3d)<t$, then $\\widehat\\delta_Z(t)\\ge d/2$.*\n\n4.  *The completed atomic-ball route gives $\\widehat\\delta_Z(t)\\ge t^2/16384$.",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Disjoint-segment norm and forest dual** ([source](#eq:segment-norm)): For a finitely supported real function $u$ on $\mathcal F$, set $u(S)=\sum_{v\in S}u(v)$ and define $$\label{eq:segment-norm} \rho(u)=\sup\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^m u(S_i)^2\right)^{1/2}: m\ge1,\ S_1,\ldots,S_m\text{ pairwise disjoint segments}\right\}.$$
- **Ancestral segment** ([source](#eq:segment-norm)): A *segment* is a nonempty interval $[a,b]=\{v:a\preceq v\preceq b\}$ in an ancestral chain.
- **Infinite-height coordinate predual** ([source](#binf:polar)): Let $J_\infty$ be its completion and define $$B_\infty=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{e_v^*:v\in T_\infty\} \subset J_\infty^*.$$
- **Represented segment atom** ([source](#eq:atom-coefficients)): Call such a represented functional an *atom* when $\sum_i a_i^2\le1$.
- **Maximum-based asymptotic midpoint modulus** ([source](#eq:max-modulus)): For an infinite-dimensional real Banach space $Y$ and $t>0$, define the maximum-based asymptotic midpoint modulus by $$\label{eq:max-modulus} \overline\theta_Y(t)=\inf_{\\|x\\|=1} \sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(Y)} \inf_{y\in F,\,\\|y\\|=1} \left(\max\{\\|x+ty\\|,\\|x-ty\\|\}-1\right),$$ where $\operatorname{cof}(Y)$ denotes the closed finite-codimensional subspaces.
- **Averaged asymptotic midpoint modulus** ([source](#eq:averaged-modulus)): We shall also use the averaged convention $$\label{eq:averaged-modulus} \widehat\delta_Y(t)=\inf_{\\|x\\|=1}\sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(Y)} \inf_{y\in F,\,\\|y\\|=1} \left(\frac{\\|x+ty\\|+\\|x-ty\\|}{2}-1\right).$$
- **One-sided asymptotic modulus** ([source](#eq:one-sided-modulus)): Define $$\label{eq:one-sided-modulus} \overline\delta_N(t)=\inf_{x\in S_N} \sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(Z)} \inf_{y\in F\cap S_N}\bigl(N(x+ty)-1\bigr),\qquad t>0.$$
- **Atomic gauge and coordinate polar** ([source](#presentation:constructions)): Define $$a(x)=\inf\{\lambda>0:x\in\lambda\operatorname{conv}\mathcal A\}, \qquad q(x)=\sup_{\substack{u\in c_{00}(\mathcal F)\\\rho(u)\le1}} \left\|\sum_v x(v)u(v)\right\|.$$
- **Evaluation deficit, test slack, and coefficient error** ([source](#stab:coef:boundary)): For a represented atom put $$\begin{gathered} s_i=p(S_i),\qquad L(A)=1-A(p),\qquad D(A)=1-\sum_i s_i^2,\\ E(A)=\sum_i(c_i-s_i)^2,\qquad A^\circ=\sum_i s_i d_{S_i}. \end{gathered}$$
- **Wholly exterior and crossing parts** ([source](#stab:coef:boundary)): Write $c(A)=(c_g(A))_g$ and $s(A)=(s_g(A))_g$, and define $$O(A)=\sum_{S_i\cap H=\varnothing}c_i d_{S_i},\qquad C(A)=\sum_{S_i\text{ crossing}}c_i d_{S_i\setminus H}.$$
