|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main-squared-radius-tail | Exterior displacement norm | ≤ | $2\sqrt{R^2-\\|x\\|^2}$ | A finite ancestral head supporting the center; the displacement may have a nonzero head. |
| q-joint-budget-averaged-modulus | Averaged asymptotic midpoint modulus | ≥ | $\frac{\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1}{2}$ | Every positive perturbation radius. |
| q-clipping-averaged-modulus | Averaged asymptotic midpoint modulus | ≥ | $\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1$ | Every positive perturbation radius. |
| q-cumulative-clipping-cost | Squared norm of the perturbed primal test | ≤ | $\rho(f)^2+3s^2\rho(v)^2$ | Positive test multiplier, selected inward signs, and an admissible cone or region budget. |
| q-homogeneous-head-tail-growth | Maximum endpoint norm | ≥ | $\sqrt{\\|x\\|^2+\\|y\\|^2/12}$ | An ancestral head and a displacement supported outside it. |
| q-radial-deficit-tail | Exterior displacement norm | ≤ | $(\sqrt2+\sqrt6)\sqrt{R(R-\\|x\\|)}$ | The original lens radius and the radial center deficit. |
| q-conditional-joint-tail | Zero-head displacement norm from conditional recovery | ≤ | $(1+2\sqrt2)\sqrt\Delta$ | Positive supported center norm and a zero-head displacement. |
| q-supported-midpoint-tail | Endpoint tail norm | ≤ | $(2+2\sqrt6)\sqrt\varepsilon$ | A head-supported midpoint whose norm has the stated unit deficit. |
| q-small-center-lens-tail | Pure-tail displacement norm | ≤ | $25\sqrt e$ | The endpoint maximum is bounded by the stated enlarged unit radius. |
| q-norm-weighted-average-tail | Displacement norm | ≤ | $C\sqrt{L(L-r)}$ | The center norm, actual endpoint average, and the constant defined in the statement. |
| q-completed-atomic-tail | Pure-tail displacement norm | ≤ | $32\sqrt d$ | A unit finite-head center and the restricted enlargement parameter. |
| q-target-sign-separated-center | Center norm of an infinite separated lens family | ≤ | $1-\varepsilon^2/256$ | A positive uniform separation of the infinite displacement sequence. |
| q-first-exit-modulus | Averaged asymptotic midpoint modulus | ≥ | $\frac{t^2}{576C_{\mathrm{ex}}^2}$ | The restricted perturbation interval and the first-exit constant in the statement. |
| q-coordinate-null-limsup | Upper limit of displacement norms | ≤ | $7\sqrt{1-\\|x\\|}$ | A fixed center and a coordinatewise-null sequence of unit-lens displacements. |
| q-forest-one-sided-obstruction | One-sided asymptotic modulus at the stated equivalence radii | = | $0$ | The equivalent norm and its comparison constants determine the radii specified in the statement. |

          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#mol:prop:joint-budget\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mol:prop:joint-budget\">1</a>, assume in addition that $P_Hy=0$ and $r=\\|x\\|>0$, and put $\\Delta=R^2-r^2$. Fix a head norming test $p=P_Hp$ with $\\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=r$. For every finite ancestral $K\\supset H$ and each sign $\\tau\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, choose any finite law of represented vectors $$A=\\sum_i a_i d_{S_i},\\qquad \\sum_i a_i^2\\le R^2,\n \\qquad \\mathbb E_\\tau A=P_K(x+\\tau y),$$ where each represented family consists of disjoint segments. Put $$b_i=r p(S_i),\\qquad A^\\circ=\\sum_i b_i d_{S_i},\\qquad\n V=\\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2,\\qquad L=r^2-\\sum_i b_i^2.$$ These laws exist by the finite atomic-ball formula. For every such choice, the replacement satisfies $$\\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint}\n \\mathbb E_\\tau(V+L)\\le\\Delta,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E_\\tau\\|Q_H(A-A^\\circ)\\|\\le\\sqrt\\Delta.$$ At each gate $g$, let $b(g)$ be the replaced crossing coefficient, or zero when there is no crossing segment. The mixed absolute crossing vector $m(g)=\\bigl(\\mathbb E_1|b(g)|+\\mathbb E_{-1}|b(g)|\\bigr)/2$ satisfies $\\|m\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{2\\Delta}$. Consequently a separate endpoint argument, followed by ancestral exhaustion, yields $$\\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint-tail}\n \\|y\\|\\le(1+2\\sqrt2)\\sqrt\\Delta.$$ If the same two nonnegative errors are bounded separately, without retaining their sum, the corresponding calculation gives $$\\label{mol:eq:strict-gauge-tail}\n \\|y\\|\\le(1+2\\sqrt3)\\sqrt\\Delta.$$",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-forest-foundations** [claim](#stmt-lem-forest-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-forest-structure): Componentwise Hilbert comparison, square-sum duality, separation, and coordinate approximation. -> Reflexivity, coordinate density, and contractive ancestral exhaustion.
- **step-forest-cones** [claim](#stmt-lem-cones) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cones): Group disjoint tests by incomparable exterior cones and dualize the square sum. -> Exact exterior cone norm formula and gate contraction.
- **step-infinite-projections** [claim](#stmt-binf-projections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-binf-projections): Restrict segment tests and extend projections to the closed coordinate span. -> Contractive projections, tail density, and supported norming tests.
- **step-infinite-atomic-hull** [claim](#stmt-binf-finite-hull) / [proof](#proof-stmt-binf-finite-hull): Compact finite atomic families, support-function separation, and affine-dependence reduction. -> Exact finite atomic balls, gauge equality, and the closed completed atomic ball.
- **step-infinite-cones** [claim](#stmt-binf-cones) / [proof](#proof-stmt-binf-cones): Combine exterior test families and apply square-sum restriction duality. -> Exterior square sums and antichain-coordinate contraction.
- **step-forest-atomic-hull** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-hull) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-hull): Separate the compact atomic convex hull using its primal support function. -> Finite atomic representations and head norming tests.
- **step-closed-forest-ball** [claim](#stmt-cor-closed-atomic-ball) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-closed-atomic-ball): Approximate completed vectors by contractive finite projections. -> The norm-closed atomic ball and unit segment norms.
- **step-opposite-crossings** [claim](#stmt-lem-opposite-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-opposite-crossing): Split the longer nested head simultaneously at opposite-sign gates. -> Opposite crossing products are paid by the test deficits.
- **step-joint-main-tail** [claim](#stmt-mol-prop-joint-budget) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-opposite-crossing-2): Scaled coefficient replacement, a joint error budget, signed recovery, and ancestral exhaustion. -> Uniform exterior control for arbitrary lens displacements in both models.
- **step-main-modulus** [claim](#stmt-mol-cor-all-radius-averaged) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mol-cor-all-radius-averaged): Apply the main tail bound on a head kernel and pass through normalized center approximation. -> All-radius averaged midpoint growth.
- **step-order-convexity** [claim](#stmt-foundation-order-convex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-foundation-order-convex): Intersect each test segment with a segment-preserving vertex set. -> Contractive order-convex projections and Euclidean antichain norms.
- **step-height-variants** [claim](#stmt-foundation-height-variants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-foundation-height-variants): Group segment tests by components, dualize, and split off the singleton level. -> Forest variant identifications and reflexivity.
- **step-weak-null-growth** [claim](#stmt-foundation-weak-null-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-foundation-weak-null-growth): Approximate normalized weakly null directions in a cofinite subspace and use ray convexity. -> Eventual strict growth along weakly null increments.
- **step-bounded-tree-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-foundation-bounded-tree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-foundation-bounded-tree): Iterate hypothetical cofinite growth down arbitrarily deep bounded trees. -> Vanishing one-sided modulus under equivalent norms.
- **step-forest-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-foundation-forest-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-foundation-forest-obstruction): Use unit root paths and weakly null coordinate children in components of unbounded height. -> No equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex forest norm.
- **step-infinite-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-binf-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-binf-obstruction): Bound coordinate combinations by their Euclidean coefficient norm and iterate child growth on unit paths. -> Whole-dual coordinate weak nullity and the infinite-tree renorming obstruction.
- **step-raw-child-budgets** [claim](#stmt-stab-sign-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-sign-obstruction): Transfer cofinite gains through the stated raw-child approximation errors. -> The displayed error and growth correspondences.
- **step-inward-crossing** [claim](#stmt-clip-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-clip-crossing): Classify crossing terms by a suffix threshold and pay large positive terms by simultaneous head splits. -> Quadratic inward perturbation cost.
- **step-two-clips** [claim](#stmt-clip-two-clips) / [proof](#proof-stmt-clip-two-clips): Take successive differences of signed positive-part cumulative potentials. -> A support-preserving difference decomposition with controlled test costs.
- **step-clipping-modulus** [claim](#stmt-clip-paired-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-clip-paired-modulus): Test opposite endpoints on the paired clips, optimize the multiplier, and approximate the center. -> The all-radius averaged clipping bound.
- **step-homogeneous-lens** [claim](#stmt-dir-primal-add-head-tail-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dir-primal-add-head-tail-lens): Optimize the head-tail testing ratio and extract a close pair of finite-head projections. -> Homogeneous endpoint growth and a separated-family center deficit.
- **step-sign-selection** [claim](#stmt-clip-selection-prop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-clip-selection-prop): Select cumulative parts and inward sign classes in each stated order. -> A useful inward test before choosing an endpoint.
- **step-frontier-shift** [claim](#stmt-clip-frontier-prop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-clip-frontier-prop): Place compensating values at gates, split large crossing heads, and select an endpoint. -> Gate-shift test bounds and their averaged midpoint consequence.
- **step-independent-threshold** [claim](#stmt-dir-primal-add-independent-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dir-primal-add-independent-threshold): Keep the suffix threshold independent of the test multiplier and account for the remaining head slack. -> Root-shift costs for the permitted multiplier range.
- **step-separated-family-tests** [claim](#stmt-dir-primal-add-separated) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dir-primal-add-separated): Extract a tail witness and add complementary endpoint evaluations to cancel unknown head terms. -> Uniform endpoint growth depending on the stated norm bound.
- **step-radial-tail** [claim](#stmt-thm-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-tail): Replace coefficients by unscaled head sums, estimate errors separately, and exhaust finite coordinates. -> The radial-deficit tail estimate.
- **step-radial-modulus** [claim](#stmt-cor-max-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-max-modulus): Solve the radial quadratic inequality and pass from finite heads to arbitrary unit centers. -> A lower bound for the maximum midpoint modulus.
- **step-endpoint-law-budgets** [claim](#stmt-prop-endpoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint): Bound each endpoint evaluation separately and compare its nonnegative crossing mass with the pooled mass. -> Separate conditional replacement and gate-mass bounds.
- **step-conditional-joint** [claim](#stmt-mol-prop-conditional-joint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mol-prop-conditional-joint): Use exact conditional head evaluations and compare conditional absolute mass with the joint mixture. -> Conditional joint and separate-error tail estimates.
- **step-presentation-identities** [claim](#stmt-presentation-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-presentation-identity): Compare gauge and polar norms through finite supported atomic hulls and coordinate density. -> Isometric equality of the gauge, polar, and forest dual completions.
- **step-subtree-products** [claim](#stmt-presentation-subtrees) / [proof](#proof-stmt-presentation-subtrees): Form finite products of convex atomic representations in incomparable subtrees and pass to limits. -> Exact finite and countable subtree square sums.
- **step-pointwise-budget** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-pointwise) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-pointwise): Expand the coefficient square and restrict the disjoint coefficient estimate. -> Pointwise deficit, replacement, exterior, and gate-coordinate estimates.
- **step-head-conflicts** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-head-conflict) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-head-conflict): Truncate the longer nested head or use simultaneous splitting. -> Opposite head products bounded by squared-test slack.
- **step-original-products** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-original-products) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-original-products): Use the Lipschitz positive-part map and Euclidean coefficient error bounds. -> An original crossing-product estimate.
- **step-gate-law-bounds** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-gate-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-gate-bound): Take independent copies and expand signed versus unsigned mean masses. -> Alternative finite-law gate mass bounds.
- **step-rooted-assembly** [claim](#stmt-stab-sign-rooted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-sign-rooted): Use independent finite convex choices across incomparable roots and antichain testing. -> Absolute-mass recovery, common-sign equality, and conditional recovery.
- **step-weighted-stability** [claim](#stmt-stab-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-weighted): Pool endpoint laws, control absolute head masses, and recover each conditional tail through its positive weight. -> Weighted endpoint stability including a nonzero mixture tail.
- **step-bounded-multipliers** [claim](#stmt-stab-bounded-multiplier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-bounded-multiplier): Apply absolute-mass assembly to a bounded multiplier and add the pooled replacement error. -> Bounded-multiplier and symmetric displacement tail estimates.
- **step-supported-pair** [claim](#stmt-stab-supported-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-supported-pair): Choose a supported midpoint normer and specialize weighted stability. -> Supported midpoint and common-test endpoint tail bounds.
- **step-original-crossing-stability** [claim](#stmt-stab-original-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-original-crossing): Separate exterior segments, transfer absolute head masses to original coefficients, and recover conditional tails. -> Endpoint tails in terms of pooled gate coordinates and head deficit.
- **step-small-center-tail** [claim](#stmt-stab-small-center-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-small-center-tail): Use an ordered conflict comparison with normalized endpoint laws and retain the center-tail error. -> A pure-tail displacement bound for a slightly enlarged lens.
- **step-stability-modulus** [claim](#stmt-stab-averaged-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-averaged-gain): Exclude bounded enlarged lenses and use either finite-head approximation or a supporting-functional kernel. -> Quadratic and parameterized averaged midpoint gains.
- **step-weighted-average-lens** [claim](#stmt-molw-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molw-average): Weight normalized endpoint laws by their actual norms and recover the displacement with the endpoint sign. -> An average-radius lens bound.
- **step-weighted-average-modulus** [claim](#stmt-molw-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molw-modulus): Solve the average-radius quadratic and approximate arbitrary unit centers. -> An all-radius averaged midpoint bound.
- **step-approximate-conditional** [claim](#stmt-mola-conditional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mola-conditional): Approximate completed endpoints by finite atom laws and retain all conditional and pooled errors. -> A prelimit conditional tail estimate and its limit.
- **step-closed-hull-signed** [claim](#stmt-mol-closed-hull-signed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mol-closed-hull-signed): Keep the endpoint sign when approximating both completed endpoints. -> A signed recovery bound without conditional mass inflation.
- **step-unscaled-tail** [claim](#stmt-molb-unscaled-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molb-unscaled-tail): Choose a center head first, retain original endpoint radii, and pool coefficient error with test slack. -> A common-head enlarged-lens bound uniform over displacements.
- **step-exact-original-tail** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-exact-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-exact-tail): Compare head-mass transfer with original-product transfer and distinguish signed from conditional reconstruction. -> Square-root and fourth-root coefficient estimates.
- **step-signed-original-tail** [claim](#stmt-mola-actual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mola-actual): Retain the endpoint sign in the original-product reconstruction. -> The forest fourth-root bound without conditional recovery inflation.
- **step-unit-head-radius** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-unit-head-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-unit-head-lens): Scale the exact coefficient square-root bound by the lens radius. -> A unit-head radial lens bound.
- **step-completed-atomic-tail** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-approximate-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-approximate-tail): Approximate normalized completed endpoints directly and retain gate cancellation errors until the limit. -> An approximate-tail bound and its restricted-radius simplification.
- **step-original-common-head** [claim](#stmt-molb-prop-original-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molb-prop-original-lens): Fix the head before the displacement and use original-product masses with explicit endpoint approximation errors. -> A common-head tail bound tending to zero with the radius enlargement.
- **step-near-unit-center** [claim](#stmt-molx-actual-center-fourth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molx-actual-center-fourth): Choose a finite test and ancestral head before approximating the lens endpoints. -> A common-head tail bound for every displacement about a near-unit center.
- **step-coefficient-moduli** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-modulus-conversions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-modulus-conversions): Convert the distinct lens estimates using their own normalization and endpoint lower bounds. -> The listed conditional and explicit averaged modulus bounds.
- **step-conditional-prefix-modulus** [claim](#stmt-stab-coef-conditional-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-coef-conditional-modulus): Keep the exterior error under its own conditional law, use prefix truncation, and normalize finite tail approximants. -> Strict finite growth, non-strict completed growth, and an averaged modulus.
- **step-fractional-matching** [claim](#stmt-molb-lem-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molb-lem-matching): Match opposite finite supplies, charge proposed splitting gains to outcome capacities, and use Cauchy–Schwarz. -> The squared minimum-mass capacity inequality.
- **step-matching-lens** [claim](#stmt-molb-prop-matching-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molb-prop-matching-lens): Apply matching capacity to corrected atom laws and restore conditional errors. -> A matching-based common-head enlarged-lens estimate.
- **step-cross-law-minimum** [claim](#stmt-molx-minimum-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molx-minimum-tail): Compare independent samples from different endpoint laws and retain the smaller cone mass. -> A minimum-mass forest tail bound.
- **step-ordered-sign-tail** [claim](#stmt-molc-oriented-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molc-oriented-lens): Retain a single conflict orientation, bound unsigned means, and recover through disjoint rooted tests. -> The ordered-sign infinite-tree lens estimate.
- **step-target-signs** [claim](#stmt-molb-prop-target-signs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molb-prop-target-signs): Choose suffix signs from target cone norms and use the same signs in both endpoint comparisons. -> A target-sign tail bound and an explicit separated-family center deficit.
- **step-preferred-parent** [claim](#stmt-stab-sign-parent-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-sign-parent-lens): Pool head sums by last head vertex, discard minority signs, and compare retained common-sign tails. -> The preferred-parent lens implication.
- **step-parent-modulus** [claim](#stmt-stab-sign-parent-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-sign-parent-modulus): Approximate the center and rule out an endpoint average compatible with the preferred-parent lens. -> A positive averaged modulus for an admissible radius choice.
- **step-forest-parent** [claim](#stmt-stab-sign-forest-parent-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-sign-forest-parent-lens): Approximate completed forest endpoints and retain common preferred-parent signs. -> A forest preferred-parent unit-lens estimate.
- **step-first-exit-majority** [claim](#stmt-stab-sign-exit-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-sign-exit-lens): Choose majority head-sum signs at exits, transfer them to original coefficients, and separate exterior errors. -> The normalized first-exit lens bound.
- **step-exit-modulus** [claim](#stmt-stab-sign-exit-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-sign-exit-modulus): Exclude enlarged finite-head lenses and transfer their averaged excess to arbitrary centers. -> An explicit quadratic averaged modulus.
- **step-maximal-gain-trimming** [claim](#stmt-stab-trim-vanishing-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-trim-vanishing-lens): Discard small, misaligned, or highly splittable crossings and control discarded mass rather than probability alone. -> A contradiction to nonvanishing tails in asymptotically unit lenses.
- **step-trimming-modulus** [claim](#stmt-stab-trim-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-trim-modulus): Assume vanishing modulus and choose approximately minimizing tail perturbations. -> Qualitative positivity of the infinite-tree averaged modulus.
- **step-finite-head-pair** [claim](#stmt-mol-finite-head-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mol-finite-head-pair): Cover the bounded finite-dimensional head image by finitely many small balls. -> A close projected pair and a separated half-difference in the same lens.
- **step-sequential-forest** [claim](#stmt-mola-sequential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mola-sequential): Use varying finite atom laws, smaller squared conflicts, and product-space Cauchy–Schwarz. -> Vanishing tails for near-unit supported forest centers with varying heads.
- **step-uniform-sequential** [claim](#stmt-mola-uniform-sequential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mola-uniform-sequential): Contradict the sequential lemma if no uniform deficit exists, then normalize enlarged finite-head lenses. -> Uniform near-unit tail control and maximum-based midpoint convexity.
- **step-separated-sequential** [claim](#stmt-mola-separated-sequential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mola-separated-sequential): Extract close head pairs and normalize the resulting supported center and tail displacement. -> A uniform center deficit for infinite separated lens families.
- **step-coordinate-null** [claim](#stmt-molx-coordinate-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molx-coordinate-null): Fix a finite ancestral head, use coordinate convergence there, and take the norm upper limit before shrinking the approximation error. -> A deficit-dependent upper limit for coordinatewise-null displacement norms.
- **step-separate-normalizations** [claim](#stmt-molc-sequential-positive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-molc-sequential-positive): Normalize endpoint laws separately, bound their residual pooled tail, and apply conditional stability. -> Qualitative positivity of the infinite-tree averaged midpoint modulus.

          "exact_quote": "Let $f=P_Hf$ and $v=Q_Hv$ be finite tests. Use either the cone budgets $m_g$ or $q_g$, or the last-head budgets in <a href=\"#clip:group-budgets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"clip:group-budgets\">[clip:group-budgets]</a>; write $c_j$ for the chosen budget of a cone or region. Fix $s>0$. At each attached group choose the shortest head suffix ending at its attachment whose $f$-sum has magnitude at least $sc_j$, if one exists and $c_j>0$. Let $\\sigma_j$ be the opposite sign of that sum, and otherwise choose either sign.*\n\n*Suppose a finite exterior test $w$ satisfies $\\rho(w)\\le\\rho(v)$, and every root prefix in group $j$ has $w$-sum in $\\sigma_j[0,c_j]$. Then $$\\label{clip:crossing-bound}\n \\rho(f+sw)^2\\le\\rho(f)^2+3s^2\\rho(v)^2.$$ The same conclusion holds with a strictly greater suffix threshold, provided the small-crossing case includes equality. With a non-strict suffix threshold, equality may also be assigned to the small-crossing case, leaving only strictly larger terms to be split.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-inward-crossing",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite head and exterior tests",
        "Cone or last-head budgets"
      ],
      "method": "Classify crossing terms by a suffix threshold and pay large positive terms by simultaneous head splits.",
      "output": "Quadratic inward perturbation cost.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-clip-crossing"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-clip-two-clips",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-clip-two-clips",
          "exact_quote": "For every $s>0$ and finite tests $f=P_Hf$, $v=Q_Hv$, there are $h_1,h_2$ supported on $\\operatorname{supp}v$ such that $$\\label{clip:difference-split}\n v=h_1-h_2,\\qquad \\rho(h_j)\\le\\rho(v),\\qquad\n \\rho(f+sh_j)^2\\le\\rho(f)^2+3s^2\\rho(v)^2\\quad(j=1,2).$$ Equivalently, $v=k_1+k_2$ with $k_1=h_1$, $k_2=-h_2$, and the two admissible perturbations are $f+sk_1$ and $f-sk_2$.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-clip-selection-prop",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-clip-selection-prop",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $x=P_Hx$, $\\|x\\|=1$, $y\\in\\ker P_H$, and $v=Q_Hv$, $\\rho(v)\\le1$, and $y(v)>\\|y\\|/2$. For every $s>0$, each of the following constructions produces an exterior test $w$ with $$|y(w)|>\\|y\\|/8,\\qquad \\rho(w)\\le1,\n \\qquad \\rho(f+sw)^2\\le1+3s^2,$$ where $f=P_Hf$ norms $x$ and the inward signs are chosen with full cone budgets $q_g$.*\n\n1.  *Take the positive and negative cumulative parts globally, choose one by its total pairing, then retain one of the two groups of cones having the same prescribed inward sign.*\n\n2.  *Form all four combinations of a cumulative positive/negative part and a prescribed inward-sign group, and select one by its pairing.*\n\n3.  *Choose a cumulative part separately in each cone, orient it inward, then retain the cones on which the resulting pairings have one common sign.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-sign-selection",

      "output": "A useful inward test before choosing an endpoint.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-clip-selection-prop"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-clip-frontier-prop",

          "anchor": "stmt-clip-frontier-prop",
          "exact_quote": "For finite $f=P_Hf$ and $v=Q_Hv$ with $\\rho(f),\\rho(v)\\le1$, and every $s>0$, there is a finitely supported gate vector $a$ such that $$\\label{clip:frontier-tests}\n \\rho(a)\\le1,\\qquad \\rho(a\\pm v)\\le2,\\qquad\n \\rho\\bigl(f+s(a\\pm v)\\bigr)^2\\le1+12s^2.$$ For either $Z=B_\\infty$ or the finite-height forest space, this selected-endpoint method gives $$\\label{clip:frontier-modulus}\n \\widehat\\delta_Z(t)\\ge\n \\frac{\\sqrt{1+t^2/48}-1}{4}\\qquad(0<t<1).$$",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-dir-primal-add-independent-threshold",
      "depends_on": [

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-dir-primal-add-independent-threshold",
          "exact_quote": "On either the finite-height forest or the infinite tree, let $f=P_Hf$, $v=Q_Hv$ be finite tests with $\\rho(f),\\rho(v)\\le1$. Let finitely many $a_g\\ge0$ satisfy $\\sum_ga_g^2\\le1$ and $|v([g,u])|\\le a_g$ on every root prefix. Fix $\\alpha>0$. For $a_g>0$, choose the shortest attached head suffix of magnitude at least $\\alpha a_g$, if one exists, and let $\\tau_g$ be its sign; otherwise put $\\tau_g=1$. Strict thresholds may be used instead. Set $$a=-\\sum_g\\tau_ga_ge_g,\\qquad h_+=a+v,\\qquad h_-=a-v.$$ For $h\\in\\{a,h_+,h_-\\}$ and $0<c\\le\\alpha/2$, $$\\label{dir:primal:add:independent-cost}\n \\rho(h)\\le2,\\qquad\n \\rho(f+ch)^2\\le1+4c\\alpha+4c^2.$$ Both the full cone norms $q_g$ and the prefix maxima $m_g$ may be used for $a_g$.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-independent-threshold",

      "output": "Root-shift costs for the permitted multiplier range.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-dir-primal-add-independent-threshold"
    },

          "source_id": "PM-331.2"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-endpoint",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-radial-tail"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-endpoint",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le d<1$, let $H$ be finite and ancestral, and suppose $x=P_Hx$, $\\|x\\|\\ge1-d$, and $\\|x\\pm y\\|\\le1$. Fix a head norming test $p$ with $\\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=\\|x\\|$, and a finite ancestral set $K\\supset H$. For each $\\tau\\in\\{-1,1\\}$ choose any finite represented atom law with mean $P_K(x+\\tau y)$, and denote its expectation by $\\mathbb E_\\tau$. For an atom $A=\\sum_i a_i d_{S_i}$, put $b_i=p(S_i)$, $A^\\circ=\\sum_i b_i d_{S_i}$, and $L=1-\\sum_i b_i^2$. Then $$\\label{eq:endpoint-coefficients}\n \\mathbb E_\\tau\\!\\left[L+\\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2\\right]\\le4d,\n \\qquad \\mathbb E_\\tau\\|A-A^\\circ\\|\\le2\\sqrt d.$$ If $b(g)$ is the replaced crossing sum at gate $g$, put $m_\\tau(g)=\\mathbb E_\\tau|b(g)|$ and $m=(m_1+m_{-1})/2$. These nonnegative vectors satisfy $$\\label{eq:endpoint-gates}\n m_\\tau\\le2m\\text{ coordinatewise},\\qquad\\|m\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{6d}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-endpoint-law-budgets",
      "inputs": [
        "A supported nearly unit center",
        "The radial coefficient identity"
      ],
      "method": "Bound each endpoint evaluation separately and compare its nonnegative crossing mass with the pooled mass.",
      "output": "Separate conditional replacement and gate-mass bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-endpoint"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-mol-prop-conditional-joint",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-joint-main-tail"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mol-prop-conditional-joint",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#mol:prop:joint-budget\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mol:prop:joint-budget\">1</a>, assume in addition that $P_Hy=0$ and $r=\\|x\\|>0$, and put $\\Delta=R^2-r^2$. Fix a head norming test $p=P_Hp$ with $\\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=r$. For every finite ancestral $K\\supset H$ and each sign $\\tau\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, choose any finite law of represented vectors $$A=\\sum_i a_i d_{S_i},\\qquad \\sum_i a_i^2\\le R^2,\n \\qquad \\mathbb E_\\tau A=P_K(x+\\tau y),$$ where each represented family consists of disjoint segments. Put $$b_i=r p(S_i),\\qquad A^\\circ=\\sum_i b_i d_{S_i},\\qquad\n V=\\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2,\\qquad L=r^2-\\sum_i b_i^2.$$ These laws exist by the finite atomic-ball formula. For every such choice, the replacement satisfies $$\\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint}\n \\mathbb E_\\tau(V+L)\\le\\Delta,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E_\\tau\\|Q_H(A-A^\\circ)\\|\\le\\sqrt\\Delta.$$ At each gate $g$, let $b(g)$ be the replaced crossing coefficient, or zero when there is no crossing segment. The mixed absolute crossing vector $m(g)=\\bigl(\\mathbb E_1|b(g)|+\\mathbb E_{-1}|b(g)|\\bigr)/2$ satisfies $\\|m\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{2\\Delta}$. Consequently a separate endpoint argument, followed by ancestral exhaustion, yields $$\\label{mol:eq:conditional-joint-tail}\n \\|y\\|\\le(1+2\\sqrt2)\\sqrt\\Delta.$$ If the same two nonnegative errors are bounded separately, without retaining their sum, the corresponding calculation gives $$\\label{mol:eq:strict-gauge-tail}\n \\|y\\|\\le(1+2\\sqrt3)\\sqrt\\Delta.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-conditional-joint",
      "inputs": [
        "The joint coefficient budget",
        "Zero-head displacement"
      ],
      "method": "Use exact conditional head evaluations and compare conditional absolute mass with the joint mixture.",
      "output": "Conditional joint and separate-error tail estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-mol-prop-conditional-joint"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-presentation-identity",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-presentation-identity",
          "exact_quote": "The coordinate identity satisfies $a(x)=q(x)=\\|x\\|$ on $c_{00}(\\mathcal F)$ and extends to surjective linear isometries $$X_a=X_q=X=J^*.$$ The finite unit balls and completed atomic ball are therefore exactly those of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:finite-hull\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:finite-hull\">7</a> and Corollary <a href=\"#cor:closed-atomic-ball\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:closed-atomic-ball\">8</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-presentation-identity"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-pointwise",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-pointwise",
          "exact_quote": "For every represented atom, the exact identity is $$\\label{stab:deficit-identity}\n 2L(A)=1-\\sum_i c_i^2+D(A)+E(A).$$ In particular, $$\\label{stab:coef:budget}\n D(A)+\\sum_i(c_i-s_i)^2\n =1+\\sum_i c_i^2-2A(p)\\le2L(A).$$ The full coefficient replacement satisfies $$\\label{stab:toolkit:replacement}\n \\max\\{\\|A-A^\\circ\\|,\\|Q_H(A-A^\\circ)\\|\\}\n \\le\\sqrt{E(A)}\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{stab:coef:exterior}\n \\|O(A)\\|\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)},\\qquad\n \\|c(A)-s(A)\\|_2\\le\\sqrt{2L(A)}.$$ At the gates one has $$\\label{stab:coef:gate-coordinates}\n A(e_g)=c_g(A)+O(A)(e_g),\\qquad\n \\|(O(A)(e_g))_g\\|_2\\le\\|O(A)\\|.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-pointwise-budget",
      "inputs": [
        "A represented atom",
        "A supported head test"
      ],
      "method": "Expand the coefficient square and restrict the disjoint coefficient estimate.",
      "output": "Pointwise deficit, replacement, exterior, and gate-coordinate estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-stab-coef-pointwise"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-head-conflict",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-opposite-crossings"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-head-conflict",
          "exact_quote": "Two represented atoms $A,B$, tested against the same $p$, satisfy $$\\label{stab:coef:head-conflict-eq}\n 2\\sum_{g:s_g(A)s_g(B)<0}|s_g(A)s_g(B)|\\le D(A)+D(B).$$ There are two direct proofs: one discards a suffix of the longer head, and the other splits the longer segment into two pieces.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-head-conflicts",
      "inputs": [
        "A common supported test",
        "Represented crossing families"

      "method": "Truncate the longer nested head or use simultaneous splitting.",
      "output": "Opposite head products bounded by squared-test slack.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-stab-coef-head-conflict"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-original-products",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-pointwise-budget",
        "step-head-conflicts"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-original-products",
          "exact_quote": "For the original crossing coefficients, $$\\label{stab:coef:original-products-eq}\n \\sum_g(-c_g(A)c_g(B))_+\n \\le L(A)+L(B)+\\sqrt{2L(A)}+\\sqrt{2L(B)},$$ where $r_+=\\max(r,0)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-original-products",
      "inputs": [
        "Head-sign conflicts",
        "Pointwise coefficient replacement"

      "method": "Use the Lipschitz positive-part map and Euclidean coefficient error bounds.",
      "output": "An original crossing-product estimate.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-stab-coef-original-products"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-gate-bound",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-pointwise-budget",
        "step-head-conflicts",
        "step-original-products"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-coef-gate-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ have a finite law of represented atoms and suppose $\\mathbb E L(A)\\le k$, where $k\\ge0$. Write $$\\beta=\\|\\mathbb E c(A)\\|_2,\\qquad\n M_c=(\\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_g,\\qquad\n M_s=(\\mathbb E|s_g(A)|)_g.$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\mathbb E s(A)\\|_2&\\le\\beta+\\sqrt{2k},\n       \\label{stab:coef:mean-head}\\\\\n \\|M_s\\|_2^2&\\le(\\beta+\\sqrt{2k})^2+4k,\n       \\label{stab:coef:absolute-head}\\\\\n \\|M_c\\|_2&\\le\\sqrt{(\\beta+\\sqrt{2k})^2+4k}+\\sqrt{2k},\n       \\label{stab:coef:via-head}\\\\\n \\|M_c\\|_2^2&\\le\\beta^2+4k+4\\sqrt{2k}.\n       \\label{stab:coef:via-products}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last inequality is obtained directly from the original products, whereas the preceding one first controls the signs of the head sums.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-gate-law-bounds",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite atom laws",
        "Head and original-product comparisons"
      ],
      "method": "Take independent copies and expand signed versus unsigned mean masses.",
      "output": "Alternative finite-law gate mass bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-stab-coef-gate-bound"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-stab-sign-rooted",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-forest-cones",
        "step-infinite-cones"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-sign-rooted",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"stab:coef:tail-assembly\" label=\"stab:coef:tail-assembly\"></span>Let $G$ be a finite antichain and, for each $g\\in G$, let $$f_g=\\sum_j a_{g,j}d_{[g,w_{g,j}]},\\qquad\n M_g=\\sum_j|a_{g,j}|,$$ where every sum is finite. Then $$\\label{stab:sign:absolute-masses}\n \\left\\|\\sum_g f_g\\right\\|\\le\\|(M_g)_g\\|_2.$$ If the nonzero coefficients have one common sign for each fixed $g$, then $$\\label{stab:sign:rooted-equality}\n \\left\\|\\sum_g f_g\\right\\|\n =\\left(\\sum_g|f_g(e_g)|^2\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Consequently, adding another rooted mixture with the same sign at each $g$ cannot decrease the norm.*\n\n\n\n*In particular, for a finite law of represented atoms, put $M_c=(\\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_g$. For any real random variable $\\theta$ with $|\\theta|\\le1$, $$\\label{stab:coef:tail-assembly-eq}\n \\|\\mathbb E(\\theta C(A))\\|\\le\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ If the law is an equal mixture of two laws with expectations $\\mathbb E_+,\\mathbb E_-$, then $$\\label{stab:coef:conditional-assembly}\n \\|\\mathbb E_\\pm C(A)\\|\n \\le\\|(\\mathbb E_\\pm|c_g(A)|)_g\\|_2\\le2\\|M_c\\|_2.$$ The same estimates pass to norm limits of finite rooted mixtures when their absolute-mass vectors converge in $\\ell_2$. In particular, countably many roots are permitted when their masses are square summable and the series at each root is absolutely summable.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-rooted-assembly",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite rooted segment mixtures"
      ],
      "method": "Use independent finite convex choices across incomparable roots and antichain testing.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-stab-weighted",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-weighted",
          "exact_quote": "Let $z_1,\\ldots,z_m\\in B_Z$ and let $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_m>0$ with $\\sum_i\\lambda_i=1$. Put $$z=\\sum_i\\lambda_i z_i,\\qquad\n \\delta_i=1-z_i(p),\\qquad\n \\delta=1-z(p)=\\sum_i\\lambda_i\\delta_i,\n \\qquad \\eta=\\|Q_Hz\\|.$$ Then, for each $i$, $$\\label{stab:weighted-bound}\n \\|Q_Hz_i\\|\n \\le\\lambda_i^{-1}\n       \\sqrt{(\\eta+\\sqrt{2\\delta})^2+4\\delta}\n       +\\sqrt{2\\delta_i}.$$ All deficits are nonnegative, and $\\delta_i\\le\\delta/\\lambda_i$. For two equally weighted endpoints this gives $$\\label{stab:nonzero-midpoint}\n \\|Q_Hz_\\pm\\|\n \\le2\\sqrt{(\\eta+\\sqrt{2\\delta})^2+4\\delta}+2\\sqrt\\delta.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],

        "Rooted assembly"

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stab-bounded-multiplier",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a finite random represented atom, and let $\\nu$ be a real random variable on the same probability space with $|\\nu|\\le1$. Set $x=\\mathbb EA$, $u=\\mathbb E(\\nu A)$, $\\delta=1-x(p)$, and $\\eta=\\|Q_Hx\\|$. Then $$\\label{stab:bounded-multiplier-bound}\n \\|Q_Hu\\|\n \\le\\sqrt{(\\eta+\\sqrt{2\\delta})^2+4\\delta}+\\sqrt{2\\delta}\n \\le\\eta+(2+2\\sqrt2)\\sqrt\\delta.$$ Consequently, whenever $x,u\\in Z$ satisfy $x\\pm u\\in B_Z$, $$\\label{stab:symmetric-tail}\n \\|Q_Hu\\|\\le\\|Q_Hx\\|+(2+2\\sqrt2)\\sqrt{1-x(p)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.2"
        }
      ],

If a common head test $p$, $\\rho(p)=1$, satisfies $h_\\pm(p)\\ge(1-d)R$, then $$\\label{stab:common-test-bound}\n \\|Q_Hh_\\pm\\|\\le R(2\\sqrt{6d}+\\sqrt{2d}).$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-clipped-difference**: The cumulative clips recover the exterior test through their difference and introduce no support outside that test.
- **invariant-clipped-contraction**: Each clipped test has segment norm no larger than the original exterior test.
- **invariant-quadratic-clipping-cost**: Both head-plus-clip tests satisfy the stated quadratic segment-norm cost.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-finite-tests-as-completion-certificate**: Treat successful finite clipping checks as a certificate for the main lens theorem on the completed spaces. -> Finite checks establish neither the completed-space passage nor the uniformly quantified lens estimate.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-head-mask**: Apply the clipping construction with an arbitrary coordinate mask as the head. -> The crossing argument requires an ancestral head so that crossing heads are suffixes of a common ancestral path.
- **anti-use-cumulative-values-as-increments**: Return clipped cumulative potentials directly as the clipped coordinate tests. -> A constant potential along an exterior edge would create a new coordinate increment where the original test vanishes.
- **anti-use-midpoint-gain-as-auc-renorming**: Infer an equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm from the positive midpoint estimates. -> The bounded-path obstruction excludes that renorming conclusion for the specified spaces.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Lemma 20 constructs finitely supported clips by taking successive differences of cumulative potentials; Lemma 19 permits cone prefix maxima as budgets. This executable restriction checks that finite construction with exact rational data. It does not certify the infinite-dimensional lens theorem or the passage to either completion.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with case, parents, head, f, v, and s; rational values are strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: A nonempty topologically ordered rooted forest with at most 8 vertices; head is ancestral; f is supported in head; v vanishes there; s is positive.
# OUTPUTS: JSON object containing rational-string arrays h1 and h2 for the cumulative clips.
# TIME: Quadratic in the vertex count for ancestor scans.
# SPACE: Linear in the vertex count.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic throughout.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and produce a nonzero exit.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def rational(value):
    if not isinstance(value, str):
        raise ValueError('rational strings required')
    return Fraction(value)


def solve(data):
    parents = data['parents']
    n = len(parents)
    if not 1 <= n <= 8:
        raise ValueError('unsupported vertex count')
    for i, parent in enumerate(parents):
        if type(parent) is not int or not -1 <= parent < i:
            raise ValueError('invalid parent ordering')
    head_list = data['head']
    if any(type(i) is not int or not 0 <= i < n for i in head_list):
        raise ValueError('invalid head vertex')
    head = set(head_list)
    if len(head) != len(head_list):
        raise ValueError('duplicate head vertex')
    if any(parents[i] >= 0 and parents[i] not in head for i in head):
        raise ValueError('head is not ancestral')
    f = [rational(value) for value in data['f']]
    v = [rational(value) for value in data['v']]
    s = rational(data['s'])
    if len(f) != n or len(v) != n or s <= 0:
        raise ValueError('invalid test arrays or scale')
    if any(f[i] != 0 for i in range(n) if i not in head):
        raise ValueError('head test has exterior support')
    if any(v[i] != 0 for i in head):
        raise ValueError('exterior test has head support')
    exterior = [i for i in range(n) if i not in head]
    gate = {}
    cumulative = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]
    budgets = {}
    for i in exterior:
        g = i
        while parents[g] >= 0 and parents[g] not in head:
            g = parents[g]
        gate[i] = g
        parent = parents[i]
        cumulative[i] = v[i] + (cumulative[parent] if parent >= 0 and parent not in head else 0)
        budgets[g] = max(budgets.get(g, Fraction(0)), abs(cumulative[i]))
    signs = {}
    for g, budget in budgets.items():
        signs[g] = 1
        if budget == 0:
            continue
        vertex = parents[g]
        total = Fraction(0)
        while vertex >= 0 and vertex in head:
            total += f[vertex]
            if abs(total) >= s * budget:
                signs[g] = -1 if total > 0 else 1
                break
            vertex = parents[vertex]
    first = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]
    second = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]
    for i in exterior:
        sigma = signs[gate[i]]
        first[i] = sigma * max(sigma * cumulative[i], Fraction(0))
        second[i] = sigma * max(-sigma * cumulative[i], Fraction(0))
    h1 = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]
    h2 = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]
    for i in exterior:
        parent = parents[i]
        h1[i] = first[i] - (first[parent] if parent >= 0 and parent not in head else 0)
        h2[i] = second[i] - (second[parent] if parent >= 0 and parent not in head else 0)
    return {'h1': [str(value) for value in h1], 'h2': [str(value) for value in h2]}


if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(json.dumps(solve(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\nfrom functools import lru_cache\n\n\ndef fractions(values):\n    if any(not isinstance(value, str) for value in values):\n        raise ValueError('rational strings required')\n    return tuple(Fraction(value) for value in values)\n\n\ndef norm_squared(values, parents):\n    n = len(parents)\n    segments = []\n    for endpoint in range(n):\n        mask = 0\n        total = Fraction(0)\n        vertex = endpoint\n        while vertex >= 0:\n            mask |= 1 << vertex\n            total += values[vertex]\n            segments.append((mask, total * total))\n            vertex = parents[vertex]\n    containing = [[] for _ in range(n)]\n    for mask, weight in segments:\n        for vertex in range(n):\n            if mask & (1 << vertex):\n                containing[vertex].append((mask, weight))\n\n    @lru_cache(None)\n    def optimum(available):\n        if available == 0:\n            return Fraction(0)\n        bit = available & -available\n        vertex = bit.bit_length() - 1\n        best = optimum(available ^ bit)\n        for mask, weight in containing[vertex]:\n            if mask & available == mask:\n                best = max(best, weight + optimum(available ^ mask))\n        return best\n\n    return optimum((1 << n) - 1)\n\n\ndef verify(data, output, expected):\n    parents = data['parents']\n    n = len(parents)\n    if not 1 <= n <= 8:\n        return False\n    if any(type(p) is not int or p < -1 or p >= i for i, p in enumerate(parents)):\n        return False\n    raw_head = data['head']\n    if any(type(i) is not int or i < 0 or i >= n for i in raw_head):\n        return False\n    head = set(raw_head)\n    if len(head) != len(raw_head):\n        return False\n    for vertex in head:\n        ancestor = parents[vertex]\n        while ancestor >= 0:\n            if ancestor not in head:\n                return False\n            ancestor = parents[ancestor]\n    f = fractions(data['f'])\n    v = fractions(data['v'])\n    s = Fraction(data['s'])\n    if len(f) != n or len(v) != n or s <= 0:\n        return False\n    if any(f[i] for i in range(n) if i not in head) or any(v[i] for i in head):\n        return False\n    if not isinstance(output, dict) or set(output) != {'h1', 'h2'}:\n        return False\n    if not isinstance(expected, dict) or set(expected) != {'h1', 'h2'}:\n        return False\n    h1, h2 = fractions(output['h1']), fractions(output['h2'])\n    e1, e2 = fractions(expected['h1']), fractions(expected['h2'])\n    if len(h1) != n or len(h2) != n or h1 != e1 or h2 != e2:\n        return False\n    if any(h1[i] - h2[i] != v[i] for i in range(n)):\n        return False\n    if any(v[i] == 0 and (h1[i] != 0 or h2[i] != 0) for i in range(n)):\n        return False\n    tail_energy = norm_squared(v, parents)\n    head_energy = norm_squared(f, parents)\n    limit = head_energy + 3 * s * s * tail_energy\n    for h in (h1, h2):\n        if norm_squared(h, parents) > tail_energy:\n            return False\n        combined = tuple(f[i] + s * h[i] for i in range(n))\n        if norm_squared(combined, parents) > limit:\n            return False\n    return True\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    try:\n        with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n            data = json.load(handle)\n        with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n            output = json.load(handle)\n        expected = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n        accepted = verify(data, output, expected)\n    except Exception:\n        accepted = False\n    sys.exit(0 if accepted else 1)\n",
    "output_contract": "One JSON object with exactly h1 and h2, each an array of rational strings indexed by the input vertices. Their difference equals v, their supports are contained in the support of v, each squared segment norm is at most that of v, and each squared segment norm of f+s*hj is at most rho(f)^2+3*s^2*rho(v)^2. The supplied vectors also specify the exact deterministic output of the chosen inward-sign construction.",
    "reason": "Lemma 20 constructs finitely supported clips by taking successive differences of cumulative potentials; Lemma 19 permits cone prefix maxima as budgets. This executable restriction checks that finite construction with exact rational data. It does not certify the infinite-dimensional lens theorem or the passage to either completion.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with case, parents, head, f, v, and s; rational values are strings.\n# PRECONDITIONS: A nonempty topologically ordered rooted forest with at most 8 vertices; head is ancestral; f is supported in head; v vanishes there; s is positive.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON object containing rational-string arrays h1 and h2 for the cumulative clips.\n# TIME: Quadratic in the vertex count for ancestor scans.\n# SPACE: Linear in the vertex count.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic throughout.\n# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and produce a nonzero exit.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\n\ndef rational(value):\n    if not isinstance(value, str):\n        raise ValueError('rational strings required')\n    return Fraction(value)\n\n\ndef solve(data):\n    parents = data['parents']\n    n = len(parents)\n    if not 1 <= n <= 8:\n        raise ValueError('unsupported vertex count')\n    for i, parent in enumerate(parents):\n        if type(parent) is not int or not -1 <= parent < i:\n            raise ValueError('invalid parent ordering')\n    head_list = data['head']\n    if any(type(i) is not int or not 0 <= i < n for i in head_list):\n        raise ValueError('invalid head vertex')\n    head = set(head_list)\n    if len(head) != len(head_list):\n        raise ValueError('duplicate head vertex')\n    if any(parents[i] >= 0 and parents[i] not in head for i in head):\n        raise ValueError('head is not ancestral')\n    f = [rational(value) for value in data['f']]\n    v = [rational(value) for value in data['v']]\n    s = rational(data['s'])\n    if len(f) != n or len(v) != n or s <= 0:\n        raise ValueError('invalid test arrays or scale')\n    if any(f[i] != 0 for i in range(n) if i not in head):\n        raise ValueError('head test has exterior support')\n    if any(v[i] != 0 for i in head):\n        raise ValueError('exterior test has head support')\n    exterior = [i for i in range(n) if i not in head]\n    gate = {}\n    cumulative = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]\n    budgets = {}\n    for i in exterior:\n        g = i\n        while parents[g] >= 0 and parents[g] not in head:\n            g = parents[g]\n        gate[i] = g\n        parent = parents[i]\n        cumulative[i] = v[i] + (cumulative[parent] if parent >= 0 and parent not in head else 0)\n        budgets[g] = max(budgets.get(g, Fraction(0)), abs(cumulative[i]))\n    signs = {}\n    for g, budget in budgets.items():\n        signs[g] = 1\n        if budget == 0:\n            continue\n        vertex = parents[g]\n        total = Fraction(0)\n        while vertex >= 0 and vertex in head:\n            total += f[vertex]\n            if abs(total) >= s * budget:\n                signs[g] = -1 if total > 0 else 1\n                break\n            vertex = parents[vertex]\n    first = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]\n    second = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]\n    for i in exterior:\n        sigma = signs[gate[i]]\n        first[i] = sigma * max(sigma * cumulative[i], Fraction(0))\n        second[i] = sigma * max(-sigma * cumulative[i], Fraction(0))\n    h1 = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]\n    h2 = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(n)]\n    for i in exterior:\n        parent = parents[i]\n        h1[i] = first[i] - (first[parent] if parent >= 0 and parent not in head else 0)\n        h2[i] = second[i] - (second[parent] if parent >= 0 and parent not in head else 0)\n    return {'h1': [str(value) for value in h1], 'h2': [str(value) for value in h2]}\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    print(json.dumps(solve(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))\n",

This can be adapted to a finite tree implementation when its norm and suffix budgets are supplied correctly. |

| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Exterior square sums separate mathematical restriction norms across cones, but no hardware mapping, arithmetic precision policy or accelerator execution strategy is constructed. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The original crossing-product inequality controls signs and evaluation deficits of represented atoms. It supplies neither a cryptographic primitive nor a security assumption or reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Weighted endpoint stability bounds tails of vectors in the specified unit ball using a common head test. The weights are convex-combination data, without a learning objective, statistical model or training construction. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The infinite-height model admits no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm, so its midpoint estimates cannot supply an assumed positive one-sided asymptotic modulus. No optimisation solver or convergence theorem is provided. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite-rank pair extraction uses total boundedness to select nearby projected vectors from an infinite bounded sequence. It specifies no distributed selection procedure, communication model or fault assumptions. |
| other (Functional analysis and mathematical norm constructions) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs the forest primal completion and its dual from disjoint segment tests, and separately constructs the infinite-height closed coordinate span. These are concrete mathematical models for studying midpoint lenses and renorming obstructions; computational access to their completed norms remains unspecified. |

          "exact_quote": "For each integer $h\\ge1$, let $T_h=\\mathbb N^{\\le h}$ be the rooted tree of words of length at most $h$, ordered by extension of words. Its root is the empty word. Keep separate copies of these trees in the forest $$\\mathcal F=\\coprod_{h\\ge1}(\\{h\\}\\times T_h).$$ Write $a\\preceq b$ if $a$ is an ancestor of $b$ in the same component. A *segment* is a nonempty interval $[a,b]=\\{v:a\\preceq v\\preceq b\\}$ in an ancestral chain. For a finitely supported real function $u$ on $\\mathcal F$, set $u(S)=\\sum_{v\\in S}u(v)$ and define $$\\label{eq:segment-norm}\n \\rho(u)=\\sup\\left\\{\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^m u(S_i)^2\\right)^{1/2}:\n  m\\ge1,\\ S_1,\\ldots,S_m\\text{ pairwise disjoint segments}\\right\\}.$$ The Euclidean triangle inequality and singleton segments show that $\\rho$ is a norm. Let $J$ be its completion and let $X=J^*$, with the dual norm denoted by $\\|\\cdot\\|$. We write $e_v\\in J$ for a coordinate unit vector. Singleton tests give $|u(v)|\\le\\rho(u)$, so coordinate evaluation extends to an element $e_v^*\\in X$. The construction in <a href=\"#eq:segment-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:segment-norm\">[eq:segment-norm]</a> uses James’s square-sum norm on disjoint segments (James 1974, 739) [[James1974](#ref-James1974)]; the branching and component heights here differ from the classical binary tree.",

        },

Singleton tests and the Euclidean triangle inequality show that this is a norm, with $\\|u\\|_2\\le\\rho(u)\\le\\|u\\|_1$. Let $J_\\infty$ be its completion and define $$B_\\infty=\\overline{\\operatorname{span}}\\{e_v^*:v\\in T_\\infty\\}\n \\subset J_\\infty^*.$$ The closure and norm in this definition are those of $J_\\infty^*$. This is the coordinate predual described in (Banakh 2000, sec. 2.II) [[Banakh2000](#ref-Banakh2000)]. On finite coefficient vectors its norm is $$\\label{binf:polar}\n \\left\\|\\sum_v a_v e_v^*\\right\\|\n =\\sup\\left\\{\\left|\\sum_v a_v u(v)\\right|:\n                 u\\in c_{00}(T_\\infty),\\ \\rho(u)\\le1\\right\\}.$$ Indeed finite tests are dense in $J_\\infty$, with rescaling permitting approximation within its unit ball. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-specified-real-coordinate-models** (formal_scope): The lens estimate concerns the specified real segment-coordinate completions. A tree-indexed representation alone does not establish that another state space has the required norm. Blocked use: ['Applying the lens bound to arbitrary hierarchical software states or unrelated tree norms.', 'Extending the result to complex scalars without a separate argument.']
- **limit-finite-ancestral-supported-center** (hypothesis): The main estimate requires a finite ancestral head supporting the center and a common radius bounding both signed endpoints. Its conclusion controls the exterior projection of an arbitrary displacement. Blocked use: ['Using arbitrary coordinate masks without checking ancestor closure.', 'Replacing the exterior displacement norm by the whole displacement norm when its head may be nonzero.', 'Using a bound on only one signed endpoint.']
- **limit-completions-remain-distinct** (formal_scope): The infinite-height coordinate predual is a proper subspace of the full dual. The forest full-dual identification cannot be transferred merely because finite segment tests agree. Blocked use: ['Identifying the infinite-height closed coordinate span with the entire dual.', 'Applying coordinate-density conclusions to arbitrary elements of the infinite-tree full dual.']
- **limit-finite-heights-and-unbounded-height-obstruction** (hypothesis): Finite component heights support the forest reflexivity argument. The forest obstruction to equivalent asymptotic uniform convexity additionally requires those heights to be unbounded. Blocked use: ['Extending the reflexivity proof to an infinite-height component without further justification.', 'Claiming the stated forest renorming obstruction for a uniformly bounded family of component heights.']
- **limit-midpoint-gain-does-not-provide-auc** (counterexample_scope): Positive symmetric midpoint moduli do not provide an equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm for the spaces considered here. Blocked use: ['Invoking a positive one-sided asymptotic modulus solely from the midpoint estimates.', "Using these midpoint estimates to discharge an optimisation theorem's AUC-renorming assumption."]
- **limit-modulus-conventions-and-choice-order** (formal_scope): The maximum and averaged midpoint moduli have distinct numerical bounds. Their definitions choose the subspace after the center and before the perturbation direction. Blocked use: ['Copying a maximum-modulus constant into an averaged-modulus conclusion without the stated comparison.', 'Choosing a different head after seeing each perturbation and claiming a common finite-codimensional estimate.']
- **limit-finite-atomic-laws-versus-closed-hull** (approximation): Exact finite atomic representations apply on finite heads. The completed atomic-ball description is a norm-closure statement and does not make every completed endpoint a finite convex combination. Blocked use: ['Assigning an exact finite atom law to an arbitrary completed endpoint.', 'Dropping projection, rescaling or approximation errors before taking the limit.']
- **limit-atom-representation-is-required-data** (hypothesis): Coefficient replacement and crossing comparisons concern the chosen segment representation, including disjointness and its coefficient budget. Blocked use: ['Discarding the segment family and treating crossing coefficients as intrinsic coordinates of the represented functional.', 'Applying the coefficient estimate to overlapping segments without another bound.']
- **limit-gates-and-parent-groups** (hypothesis): Cancellation of exterior coordinates must use first exterior vertices. Grouping exits by their last head vertex serves a different sign-selection argument and does not identify different child coordinates. Blocked use: ['Combining distinct child exits into one coordinate when using a zero exterior mean.', 'Assuming orthogonality inside a last-head region without the stated segment argument.']
- **limit-clips-require-cumulative-differences** (hypothesis): The clipping construction transforms cumulative path sums and then takes successive differences. Inward signs must follow a permitted budget and the corresponding head-suffix selection. Blocked use: ['Clipping individual coordinate increments in place of cumulative sums.', 'Returning cumulative potentials directly as coordinate tests.', 'Changing the suffix threshold convention without adjusting the small-crossing case.']
- **limit-support-and-unattached-components** (edge_case): Clipped increments must vanish wherever the original exterior test vanishes. Untouched forest components have no last head vertex and require the separate unattached-group convention. Blocked use: ['Creating descendant support from a constant cumulative potential.', 'Requiring every exterior cone to have a parent in the head.', 'Rejecting an empty head merely because no attached suffix exists.']
- **limit-conditional-recovery-has-weight-loss** (hypothesis): A pooled nonnegative mass estimate does not transfer unchanged to an individual endpoint law. Conditional recovery retains the endpoint's positive weight and its own replacement deficit. Blocked use: ['Applying a pooled replacement-error bound unchanged to either conditional law.', 'Dropping the reciprocal endpoint weight from weighted stability.', 'Confusing signed recovery with recovery of an individual conditional endpoint.']
- **limit-rooted-sign-monotonicity** (formal_scope): The same-sign norm comparison applies to rooted segment mixtures with the stated sign condition. It does not establish lattice monotonicity of the coordinate-space norm. Blocked use: ['Increasing arbitrary coordinate magnitudes and asserting norm monotonicity.', 'Combining rooted mixtures with incompatible signs while retaining the equality conclusion.']
- **limit-renorming-obstruction-needs-whole-dual-nullity** (hypothesis): The bounded-tree obstruction requires weak nullity against the whole continuous dual, uniform path bounds and nontrivial child increments at arbitrarily large depths. Blocked use: ['Substituting coordinatewise convergence for the required weak nullity without proof.', 'Using a single bounded-depth tree to establish the universal renorming obstruction.']
- **limit-qualitative-proofs-and-discarded-mass** (nonconstructive): The sequential and trimming contradictions do not themselves provide a numerical selection algorithm or computational rate. In trimming, small discarded probability alone does not control the norm of discarded mass. Blocked use: ['Claiming an executable uniform deficit selector from the qualitative contradiction alone.', 'Replacing the discarded-mass estimate by a probability bound.', 'Claiming a finite test decides the infinitely quantified completed-space result.']
- **limit-separate-normalization-residual-tail** (approximation): Separate endpoint normalizations generally leave a nonzero pooled tail. Its convergence to zero requires control of both normalization factors and displacement norms. Blocked use: ['Setting the pooled tail identically to zero after separate endpoint normalization.', 'Omitting boundedness of the displacement sequence in the limiting argument.']
- **limit-strictness-and-parameter-regimes** (edge_case): Strict finite endpoint growth can become non-strict after completion. Simplified tail estimates retain their stated restriction on the enlargement parameter. Blocked use: ['Preserving a strict finite inequality solely by passage to a norm limit.', 'Applying the simplified completed atomic-ball estimate outside its stated parameter range.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 331 (`lean/docs/331.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Asymptotic midpoint uniform convexity and unbounded diamond distortion in a reflexive tree space](../../preprints/Asymptotic-midpoint-uniform-convexity-and-unbounded-diamond-distortion-in-a-reflexive-tree-space-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint lenses in segment spaces](../../preprints/Midpoint-lenses-in-segment-spaces-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Distortion of countably branching diamonds from midpoint and tree energies](../../preprints/Diamond-distortion-from-midpoint-and-tree-energies-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Exact asymptotic moduli in a Daugavet subspace of $L_1$](../../preprints/Exact-asymptotic-moduli-in-a-Daugavet-subspace-of-L1-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint convexity from bounded tree potentials and path costs](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-bounded-tree-potentials-and-path-costs-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Independent products in real $L_1$: asymptotic midpoint convexity without AUC renormings](../../preprints/Independent-products-in-real-L1-asymptotic-midpoint-convexity-without-AUC-renormings-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint convexity from two recursive potentials](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-two-recursive-potentials-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper constructs a Banach space whose midpoint geometry is asymptotically uniformly convex even though no equivalent norm is asymptotically uniformly convex in the usual one-sided sense. The formalization proves that the full dual of the specified segment-norm completion is infinite-dimensional, separable, and reflexive; its averaged midpoint modulus is at least $\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1$ for every $t>0$, while every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity. It also proves that an embedding of a depth-$k$ countably branching diamond has distortion at least $\sqrt{1+k/12}$.
>
> The general-forest and word-forest results use the closed span of the coordinate functionals. Their conclusions retain the finite-ancestor and equivalent-norm hypotheses, including the scale $\alpha/(2\beta)$ when $0<\alpha\le\beta$ and the new norm lies between $\alpha$ and $\beta$ times the original norm; they do not identify that span with the full dual or assert reflexivity for every forest.
>
> The paper controls midpoint lenses in Banach spaces defined by tree-segment norms. The formalization proves the following estimate in both the full dual of the finite-height forest space and the coordinate predual of the infinite-tree space. If $x$ is supported on a finite ancestral set $H$, $R\ge0$, and $\|x+y\|,\|x-y\|\le R$, then the part of $y$ outside $H$ has norm at most $2\sqrt{R^2-\|x\|^2}$.
>
> The formalization also covers selected consequences for averaged midpoint moduli and separated families of vectors, together with reflexivity and renorming obstructions. This scope concerns the segment-space results; the companion results on diamond energies remain separate.
>
> The paper studies embeddings of countably branching diamond graphs into Banach spaces built from tree segments. The formalization proves that every embedding of the depth-$k$ diamond with distortion $D$, at any positive scale, satisfies $1+k/4\le D^2$ in either of two spaces: the full dual of the completed finite-height forest space and the closed coordinate span in the dual of the infinite-tree space. The bound applies to all pairs of vertices and is unconditional.
>
> The formalization also contains selected graph deductions under explicit companion assumptions. Twenty-two of those companion inputs are assumed in these deductions; the diamond distortion bound above does not require them.
>
> The paper exhibits a real $L_1$ subspace with positive averaged midpoint convexity but no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. For every closed infinite-dimensional subspace whose unit ball is precompact in measure and which has the Daugavet property, the formalization computes the moduli at every unit vector: the averaged midpoint modulus is $\max(t/2,t-1)$ and the usual one-sided asymptotic modulus is $\max(0,t-2)$ for every $t>0$. The same formulas hold after taking the infimum over unit vectors, and every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity.
>
> The formalization also constructs such a subspace on a countable product of unit intervals and proves that it has the stated measure-precompactness and Daugavet properties, so the modulus formulas apply to an actual example.
>
> The paper constructs Banach spaces from bounded tree potentials and path costs to separate averaged midpoint convexity from asymptotic uniform convexity. For the specified tree-potential completions, the formalization proves completeness, separability, infinite dimension, and an averaged midpoint modulus of at least $\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1$ for $0<t<1$. No space linearly isomorphic to one of these completions is asymptotically uniformly convex.
>
> The formalization also covers selected path-cost duality and comparison results, clipping and energy estimates, and further renorming obstructions. These retain their stated support, head, and tail hypotheses; the remaining auxiliary assertions of the paper are outside this scope.
>
> The paper forms a real $L_1$ space from the closed span of products of independent multipliers along tree paths. For a positive, nonconstant multiplier of mean one and finite second moment, the formalization proves that this space is infinite-dimensional and has no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.
>
> For every $t>0$, the averaged midpoint modulus nevertheless has explicit positive lower bounds. If $G$ is a standard real Gaussian, the exponential-multiplier bound is $\frac{7t}{480}\mathbb E[(|G|-20/t)_+]$, where $u_+=\max(u,0)$. For squared-Gaussian multipliers, the bounds are $\frac18\Pr(|G|\ge4\sqrt2(2+\sqrt2)/t)$ and $\frac14\Pr(|G|\ge8\sqrt6/t)$.
>
> The paper constructs three Banach spaces from recursive tree potentials: a root-sum space, a zero-root space, and a joined space. The formalization proves that all three are infinite-dimensional and have no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. The root-sum and joined spaces have averaged midpoint modulus at least $t^3/128$ for $0<t<1$, and the root-sum modulus is positive for every $t>0$. The zero-root estimate retains its finite ancestral support and tail hypotheses, and the zero-root and joined spaces are reflexive.
>
> The formalization also covers a separated-midpoint result for the joined space and a variable-exponent model with its recursive norm, duality, projections, renorming obstruction, and sixth- and third-order stability estimates. The separation, sequence, support, and head assumptions in these results are essential parts of their statements.
