The same finite product construction applies; choosing zero simply omits that segment. Apply it separately to either conditional law. Since $\mathbb E_\pm|c_g|\le2\mathbb E|c_g|$ coordinatewise, it also proves <a href="#stab:coef:conditional-assembly" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:conditional-assembly">[stab:coef:conditional-assembly]</a>.

Under the one-sign assumption, $M_g=|f_g(e_g)|$. Antichain testing gives the reverse inequality in <a href="#stab:sign:rooted-equality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:sign:rooted-equality">[stab:sign:rooted-equality]</a>. Adding same-sign coefficients increases every absolute root coordinate, proving the asserted monotonicity. This conclusion concerns these rooted mixtures; it does not assert lattice monotonicity of the norm on $Z$.

For the stated extensions, apply the finite inequalities first. Their upper bounds show that truncating absolutely summable coefficients at each root and then square-summable root masses gives a Cauchy sequence in $Z$. Root coordinates converge by continuity, so the equalities pass to the limit as well. No infinite product is needed to produce an atom. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-sign-rooted-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-sign-rooted</summary>

*Proof by disjoint test segments.* For $v\in c_{00}$, let $m_g(v)$ be the largest $|d_R(v)|$ among the rooted segments appearing at $g$, or zero if there are none. Choose a maximizing segment for every nonzero $m_g(v)$. They lie in incomparable subtrees and are disjoint, so $\sum_gm_g(v)^2\le\rho(v)^2$. Consequently, $$\left|\sum_g f_g(v)\right|
 \le\sum_g M_gm_g(v)\le\|(M_g)_g\|_2\rho(v).$$ Density extends this bound to $J$. For a finite atom law use the crossing tails in its support in the same calculation: $$|\mathbb E(\theta C(A))(v)|
 \le\sum_g\mathbb E|c_g|\,m_g(v)
 \le\|M_c\|_2\rho(v).$$ The conditional argument is identical, and the root tests used in the first proof again give the one-sign equality.

One may maximize over all rooted segments in a fixed finite ancestral set, or take the supremum over all such segments in a subtree. In the latter case, choose almost maximizing segments for finitely many roots before taking the limit. This proves the same estimate without assuming global attainment. The norm-limit statements then follow by the finite truncations described above. ◻

</details>

<a id="stab:section"></a>
### Stability from a nearly norming head test

A common head test controls each endpoint of a convex combination, even when the combination has a nonzero tail. The two relevant errors are the evaluation deficit and the tail of the combination. Keeping them separate also identifies the cost of unequal endpoint weights.

Throughout this section, $Z$ is either the forest coordinate space $X$ or the infinite-height coordinate predual $B_\infty$. We use the notation and estimates of the common atom toolkit: $H$ is a finite ancestral head, $G_H$ is its set of exterior gates, and $p=P_Hp\in J$ is a head test with $\rho(p)\le1$. For a represented atom $A=\sum_jc_jd_{S_j}$, its replacement is $A^\circ=\sum_js_jd_{S_j}$, where $s_j=p(S_j)$. The quantities $L(A)=1-A(p)$, $D(A)=1-\sum_js_j^2$, and $E(A)=\sum_j(c_j-s_j)^2$ are respectively its evaluation deficit, squared-test slack, and squared coefficient error.

<a id="pm-h-53"></a>
#### A common test and weighted endpoint laws

<a id="stmt-stab-weighted"></a>
<div id="stab:weighted" class="theorem">

<a id="stab:weighted-bound"></a>
<a id="stab:nonzero-midpoint"></a>
**Theorem 41**. *Let $z_1,\ldots,z_m\in B_Z$ and let $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_m>0$ with $\sum_i\lambda_i=1$. Put $$z=\sum_i\lambda_i z_i,\qquad
 \delta_i=1-z_i(p),\qquad
 \delta=1-z(p)=\sum_i\lambda_i\delta_i,
 \qquad \eta=\|Q_Hz\|.$$ Then, for each $i$, $$\label{stab:weighted-bound}
 \|Q_Hz_i\|
 \le\lambda_i^{-1}
       \sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2\delta})^2+4\delta}
       +\sqrt{2\delta_i}.$$ All deficits are nonnegative, and $\delta_i\le\delta/\lambda_i$. For two equally weighted endpoints this gives $$\label{stab:nonzero-midpoint}
 \|Q_Hz_\pm\|
 \le2\sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2\delta})^2+4\delta}+2\sqrt\delta.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-weighted"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-weighted</summary>

<a id="stab:conditional-deficits"></a>
*Proof.* First suppose each endpoint has a finite law of represented atoms. Write $\mathbb E_i$ for its law and $\mathbb E=\sum_i\lambda_i\mathbb E_i$ for their mixture. The pointwise budget of Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:pointwise" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:pointwise">36</a> gives $$\label{stab:conditional-deficits}
 \mathbb E(D+E)\le2\delta,\qquad
 \mathbb E_i(D+E)\le2\delta_i,
 \qquad
 \|\mathbb E(A-A^\circ)\|\le\sqrt{2\delta}.$$ The same argument under the $i$th law bounds its replacement error by $\sqrt{2\delta_i}$.

Let $s_g(A)$ denote the head sum of the crossing at $g$, with value zero when no segment crosses there. A segment wholly outside $H$ has replacement coefficient zero. Thus $Q_HA^\circ$ consists of segments starting at the gates, with coefficients $s_g(A)$. Put $v=\mathbb E s$ and $m=\mathbb E|s|$, coordinatewise. Gate-coordinate contraction and <a href="#stab:conditional-deficits" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:conditional-deficits">[stab:conditional-deficits]</a> give $\|v\|_2\le\eta+\sqrt{2\delta}$. Applying the signed–unsigned identity <a href="#stab:signed-unsigned" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:signed-unsigned">[stab:signed-unsigned]</a> to the mixture yields $$\|m\|_2^2\le\|v\|_2^2+2\mathbb ED
 \le(\eta+\sqrt{2\delta})^2+4\delta.$$ Equivalently, its positive and negative mean masses $l_g,n_g$ satisfy $\sum_gl_gn_g\le\delta$.

The conditional absolute masses $m_i=\mathbb E_i|s|$ satisfy $m_i\le m/\lambda_i$ coordinatewise. Rooted-mass recovery, Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a>, therefore gives $$\|Q_H\mathbb E_iA^\circ\|
 \le\|m_i\|_2\le\lambda_i^{-1}\|m\|_2.$$ This comparison concerns nonnegative Euclidean mass vectors; it does not require lattice monotonicity of the coordinate-space norm. Adding the conditional replacement error proves <a href="#stab:weighted-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:weighted-bound">[stab:weighted-bound]</a>.

For completed endpoints, choose finite coordinate approximants and divide each by the maximum of one and its norm. They converge in norm inside $B_Z$ and have finite atom laws by the finite-hull theorem for the chosen model. Evaluation and both coordinate projections are continuous, so the inequality passes to the limit. Finally, nonnegativity of the individual deficits gives $\delta_i\le\delta/\lambda_i$ and hence <a href="#stab:nonzero-midpoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:nonzero-midpoint">[stab:nonzero-midpoint]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-stab-bounded-multiplier"></a>
<div id="stab:bounded-multiplier" class="corollary">

<a id="stab:bounded-multiplier-bound"></a>
**Corollary 42** (Bounded multipliers). *Let $A$ be a finite random represented atom, and let $\nu$ be a real random variable on the same probability space with $|\nu|\le1$. Set $x=\mathbb EA$, $u=\mathbb E(\nu A)$, $\delta=1-x(p)$, and $\eta=\|Q_Hx\|$. Then $$\label{stab:bounded-multiplier-bound}
 \|Q_Hu\|
 \le\sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2\delta})^2+4\delta}+\sqrt{2\delta}
 \le\eta+(2+2\sqrt2)\sqrt\delta.$$ Consequently, whenever $x,u\in Z$ satisfy $x\pm u\in B_Z$, <a id="stab:symmetric-tail"></a>$$\label{stab:symmetric-tail}
 \|Q_Hu\|\le\|Q_Hx\|+(2+2\sqrt2)\sqrt{1-x(p)}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-bounded-multiplier"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-bounded-multiplier</summary>

*Proof.* Apply <a href="#stab:signed-unsigned" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:signed-unsigned">[stab:signed-unsigned]</a> to the head-sum arrays of $A$. The pointwise replacement budget gives $\|\mathbb Es\|_2\le\eta+\sqrt{2\delta}$, and hence $$\|\mathbb E|s|\|_2
 \le\sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2\delta})^2+4\delta}.$$ The bounded-multiplier case of Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a>, applied to the replaced atoms $A^\circ$, bounds $\|Q_H\mathbb E(\nu A^\circ)\|$ by this absolute mass. The remaining term satisfies $$\|Q_H\mathbb E(\nu(A-A^\circ))\|
 \le\mathbb E\sqrt{E(A)}\le\sqrt{2\delta}.$$ Adding proves the first inequality. The second follows from $\sqrt{a^2+b^2}\le a+b$ for $a,b\ge0$. No independence between $\nu$ and $A$ was used.

For finite convex representations of $x+u$ and $x-u$, take their equal mixture and let $\nu$ be its endpoint sign. Then $\mathbb EA=x$ and $\mathbb E(\nu A)=u$. For completed endpoints, approximate both inside their finite atomic balls and pass to the limit, as in Theorem <a href="#stab:weighted" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:weighted">41</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-stab-supported-pair"></a>
<div id="stab:supported-pair" class="corollary">

<a id="stab:supported-bound"></a>
<a id="stab:common-test-bound"></a>
**Corollary 43**. *Let $z_+,z_-\in B_Z$. If $z=(z_++z_-)/2$ is supported on $H$ and $\|z\|\ge1-\varepsilon$, where $0<\varepsilon<1$, then $$\label{stab:supported-bound}
 \|Q_Hz_\pm\|\le(2+2\sqrt6)\sqrt\varepsilon.$$ More generally, let $R>0$, $d\ge0$, and $h_+,h_-\in Z$ satisfy $\|h_\pm\|\le R$, with $h_++h_-$ supported on $H$. If a common head test $p$, $\rho(p)=1$, satisfies $h_\pm(p)\ge(1-d)R$, then $$\label{stab:common-test-bound}
 \|Q_Hh_\pm\|\le R(2\sqrt{6d}+\sqrt{2d}).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-supported-pair"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-supported-pair</summary>

*Proof.* For the first assertion choose a norming test in the finite-dimensional head. Then $\delta\le\varepsilon$ and $\eta=0$, so <a href="#stab:nonzero-midpoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:nonzero-midpoint">[stab:nonzero-midpoint]</a> proves the claim. At the level of finite laws the proof retains the separate estimates $$\mathbb E(D+E)\le2\varepsilon,\qquad
 \|\mathbb E s\|_2\le\sqrt{2\varepsilon},\qquad
 \|\mathbb E|s|\|_2\le\sqrt{6\varepsilon},\qquad
 \mathbb E_\pm\sqrt E\le2\sqrt\varepsilon.$$ For the second assertion apply Theorem <a href="#stab:weighted" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:weighted">41</a> to $z_\pm=h_\pm/R$. Both individual deficits and the mixed deficit are at most $d$, while $\eta=0$. The conditional replacement cost is therefore $\sqrt{2d}$ and its crossing cost is at most $2\sqrt{6d}$. Multiplication by $R$ proves the assertion, including $d=0$. Alternatively, projecting both endpoints onto increasing finite ancestral sets containing $H$ preserves their sum and their evaluations on $p$, and gives the same limit. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-54"></a>
#### Keeping the original crossing coefficients

The original coefficients give a second estimate in which segments lying wholly outside the head remain visible as a separate error.

<a id="stmt-stab-original-crossing"></a>
<div id="stab:original-crossing" class="proposition">

<a id="stab:gate-original-bound"></a>
**Proposition 44**. *Let $z_1,z_2\in B_Z$, $z=(z_1+z_2)/2$, and let $H$ be finite ancestral. For a head test $p$, put $\delta=1-z(p)$ and $\kappa=\|(z(e_g))_{g\in G_H}\|_2$. Then $$\label{stab:gate-original-bound}
 \|Q_Hz_i\|\le2\kappa+(6\sqrt2+6)\sqrt\delta.$$ In particular, choosing a norming head test gives <a id="stab:original-crossing-bound"></a>$$\label{stab:original-crossing-bound}
 \|Q_Hz_i\|
 \le2\|Q_Hz\|+(6\sqrt2+6)\sqrt{1-\|P_Hz\|}
 \le2\|Q_Hz\|+15\sqrt{1-\|P_Hz\|}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-original-crossing"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-original-crossing</summary>

*Proof.* Use finite atom laws first, with equal mixture $\mathbb E$. The decomposition $Q_HA=C(A)+O(A)$ from the toolkit separates crossing and wholly exterior segments. Let $c_g(A)$ and $s_g(A)$ be the original crossing coefficient and its head sum. The pointwise estimates <a href="#stab:coef:exterior" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:exterior">[stab:coef:exterior]</a> and the gate identity <a href="#stab:coef:gate-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:gate-coordinates">[stab:coef:gate-coordinates]</a> imply $$\|\mathbb Ec\|_2\le\kappa+\sqrt{2\delta},\qquad
 \|\mathbb Es-\mathbb Ec\|_2\le\sqrt{2\delta}.$$ By <a href="#stab:signed-unsigned" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:signed-unsigned">[stab:signed-unsigned]</a>, $\|\mathbb E|s|\|_2\le\|\mathbb Es\|_2+2\sqrt\delta$. The coordinate inequality $\big||c_g|-|s_g|\big|\le|c_g-s_g|$ and Jensen’s inequality therefore give <a id="stab:original-mass"></a>$$\label{stab:original-mass}
 \|\mathbb E|c|\|_2
 \le\kappa+(3\sqrt2+2)\sqrt\delta.$$ Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a> bounds the crossing mean of either conditional law by $2\|\mathbb E|c|\|_2$. Its exterior mean costs at most $$\mathbb E_i\|O(A)\|
 \le\sqrt{2\mathbb E_iL(A)}\le2\sqrt\delta.$$ Their sum proves <a href="#stab:gate-original-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:gate-original-bound">[stab:gate-original-bound]</a>. For a norming head test, $\delta=1-\|P_Hz\|$; gate contraction gives $\kappa\le\|Q_Hz\|$, and $6\sqrt2+6<15$.

Keeping the weaker conditional exterior estimate $\mathbb E_i\|O(A)\|\le2\sqrt{2\delta}$ instead records the alternative budget <a id="stab:sixteen-budget"></a>$$\label{stab:sixteen-budget}
 \|Q_Hz_i\|\le2\kappa+(8\sqrt2+4)\sqrt\delta
 <2\kappa+16\sqrt\delta\qquad(\delta>0).$$ For $\delta=0$ the last inequality is non-strict. Either the one-piece truncation or the two-piece comparison in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:head-conflict" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:head-conflict">37</a> supplies the required opposite-sign estimate. Thus this original-coefficient calculation is compatible with both surgeries. Normalized finite approximants and norm continuity give the result for completed endpoints. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-55"></a>
#### A small tail at an arbitrary center

For a fixed center we can use an ordered comparison: positive crossings from the first sample are matched only against negative crossings from the second. The deterministic step is the one-piece truncation in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:head-conflict" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:head-conflict">37</a>; its ordered expectation keeps a different budget.

<a id="stmt-stab-small-center-tail"></a>
<div id="stab:small-center-tail" class="proposition">

**Proposition 45**. *Let $0<e<1/4$, let $x\in Z$ satisfy $\|x\|=1$, and let $H$ be finite ancestral with $\|Q_Hx\|\le e$. Suppose $p=P_Hp$, $\rho(p)=1$, and $x(p)\ge1-e$. For every $u\in\ker P_H$, <a id="stab:small-center-implication"></a>$$\label{stab:small-center-implication}
 \max\{\|x+u\|,\|x-u\|\}\le1+e
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \|u\|\le25\sqrt e.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-small-center-tail"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-small-center-tail</summary>

*Proof.* Fix a finite ancestral $K\supset H$ and represent $P_K(x\pm u)/(1+e)$ by finite atom laws. Under each law the expectation on $p$ is at least $(1-e)/(1+e)\ge1-2e$. The pointwise budget gives $$\mathbb E_\pm E(A)\le4e,\qquad
 \mathbb E_\pm D(A)\le4e,\qquad
 \|\mathbb E_\pm(A-A^\circ)\|\le2\sqrt e.$$ Let $l_g,n_g$ be the positive and negative crossing masses of the equal mixture after replacement. Its mean before replacement is $P_Kx/(1+e)$, so gate contraction gives $\|l-n\|_2\le2\sqrt e+e$.

Take independent outcomes of this mixed law. At each gate compare only a positive head sum in the first outcome with a negative head sum in the second. If the sums of the longer and shorter nested heads are $b,b'$, truncation leaves the sum $b-b'$ and gains $(b-b')^2-b^2=(b')^2-2bb'\ge2|bb'|$ in the squared test. As in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:head-conflict" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:head-conflict">37</a>, different gates require different original segments, so the gains total at most $D(A)+D(A')$. Independence now gives the one-orientation estimate $2\sum_gl_gn_g\le\mathbb E(D(A)+D(A'))\le8e$. Hence <a id="stab:directional-mass"></a>$$\label{stab:directional-mass}
 \sum_gl_gn_g\le4e,\qquad
 \|l+n\|_2\le\sqrt{(2\sqrt e+e)^2+16e}.$$ Rooted-mass recovery bounds each conditional replaced tail by $2\|l+n\|_2$. Either proof in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a> applies; in its finite-test proof all maximizing segments may be taken in the finite set $K$.

Using the sufficient bound $\|I-P_H\|\le2$, restriction of each replacement error costs at most $4\sqrt e$. Half the difference of the conditional tails is $P_Ku/(1+e)$, since the center cancels. Consequently $$\|P_Ku\|\le(1+e)
 \left[2\sqrt{(2\sqrt e+e)^2+16e}+4\sqrt e\right]
 \le\frac54(\sqrt{89}+4)\sqrt e<25\sqrt e.$$ Here $e\le\sqrt e/2$. Coordinate exhaustion proves the claim. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-56"></a>
#### From endpoint stability to averaged midpoint gain

We use the averaged modulus of <a href="#eq:averaged-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:averaged-modulus">[eq:averaged-modulus]</a>. A norming-functional kernel makes both endpoint norms at least one; a finite head alone gives this lower bound for a head-supported center. These two choices yield the following consequences.

<a id="stmt-stab-averaged-gain"></a>
<div id="stab:averaged-gain" class="corollary">

**Corollary 46**. *For either coordinate model, $$\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge t^2/1024\qquad(0<t<1).$$ For every $t>0$ and every $0<e<1/4$ with $25\sqrt e<t$, one also has $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge e/2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-averaged-gain"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-averaged-gain</summary>

*Proof.* For the first assertion set $d=t^2/256$. Let $x_0$ be a finitely supported unit vector and choose $H$ containing its support. For $y\in\ker P_H$, $\|y\|\ge1$, both endpoint norms are at least one. If both were at most $1+d$, their normalized midpoint $x_0/(1+d)$ would have norm at least $1-d$. Equation <a href="#stab:supported-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:supported-bound">[stab:supported-bound]</a> would give $$\frac{t\|y\|}{1+d}\le(2+2\sqrt6)\sqrt d
 <7\sqrt d=7t/16<t/2,$$ whereas $d<1$ makes the left side greater than $t/2$. The average therefore exceeds $1+d/2$. For an arbitrary unit $x$, choose a finitely supported unit $x_0$ with $\|x-x_0\|<d/4$ and use its head subspace. The average loses less than $d/4$, leaving gain $d/4=t^2/1024$.

For the second assertion choose a head with $\|Q_Hx\|\le e$ and a norming head test with $x(p)\ge1-e$. Intersect $\ker P_H$ with the kernel of a norming functional for $x$ on $Z$. Both actual endpoints have norm at least one. For $u=ty$, $\|y\|\ge1$, Proposition <a href="#stab:small-center-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:small-center-tail">45</a> forces one endpoint to exceed $1+e$, so their average exceeds $1+e/2$. Taking the modulus proves the claim. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-57"></a>
### Endpoint distributions and conditional recovery

The probabilities assigned to two atom laws determine which combination of their endpoints can be recovered. We first weight the laws by the actual endpoint norms to control their average. We then compare signed and conditional recovery when finite laws only approximate the endpoints, and finally keep the original radius and a small center tail in the budget.

<a id="molw:sec"></a>
#### Weighting the endpoint distributions by their norms

The two actual endpoint norms determine a probability law that controls their average directly. We work in the infinite-height coordinate predual $B_\infty$ of Section <a href="#binf:definition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="binf:definition">3</a>. Its atomic gauge and finite-coordinate polar descriptions agree by Lemma <a href="#binf:finite-hull" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="binf:finite-hull">5</a>; the completion is the norm-closed coordinate span specified there, as distinguished in Remark <a href="#presentation:infinite-height" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:infinite-height">35</a>. We use its represented atoms $m=\sum_i a_i d_{S_i}$, its ancestral projections $P_A$ and $Q_A=I-P_A$, and the cone estimates from Lemmas <a href="#binf:projections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="binf:projections">4</a> and <a href="#binf:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="binf:cones">6</a>.

<a id="stmt-molw-average"></a>
<div id="molw:average" class="theorem">

<a id="molw:lens"></a>
**Theorem 47**. *Let $A\subset T_\infty$ be finite and ancestral, let $x\in B_\infty$ be supported in $A$, and let $z\in\ker P_A$. Write $$r=\|x\|,\qquad L_+=\|x+z\|,\qquad L_-=\|x-z\|,
 \qquad L=\frac{L_++L_-}{2}.$$ Then $L_+,L_-\ge r$ and $$\label{molw:lens}
 \|z\|\le C\sqrt{L(L-r)},\qquad C=\sqrt2+\sqrt6.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-molw-average"><summary>Proof of stmt-molw-average</summary>

*Proof.* Projection onto $A$ gives $L_+,L_-\ge r$. Thus $L<r$ is impossible. If $r=0$, then $x=0$ and $L_+=L_-=L=\|z\|$, and <a href="#molw:lens" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molw:lens">[molw:lens]</a> follows because $C\ge1$. This includes $L=0$. If an endpoint norm is zero, projection forces $r=0$ and then $z=0$, so no zero denominator is needed in what follows. For $r>0$, divide $x$ and $z$ by $r$; it is enough to prove the assertion with $r=1$. In this case $L_+,L_-\ge1$.

First suppose $z$ is finitely supported, and choose a finite ancestral set $B$ containing $A$ and the support of $z$. By Lemma <a href="#binf:projections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="binf:projections">4</a>, choose a head test $f=P_Af$ with $\rho(f)=1$ and $x(f)=1$. The segment test norm gives <a id="molw:test"></a>$$\label{molw:test}
 \sum_i f(S_i)^2\le1$$ for every disjoint segment family.

<a id="molw:means"></a>
Choose a sign $\sigma$ with probabilities <a id="molw:weights"></a>$$\label{molw:weights}
 \mathbb P\{\sigma=1\}=\frac{L_+}{2L},\qquad
 \mathbb P\{\sigma=-1\}=\frac{L_-}{2L}.$$ Conditionally on that sign, choose a finite distribution of represented atoms with mean $(x+\sigma z)/L_\sigma$. Such distributions exist by Lemma <a href="#binf:finite-hull" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="binf:finite-hull">5</a>; the random data retain a chosen coefficient list and segment family for every atom. These probabilities give both identities $$\label{molw:means}
 \mathbb E m=\frac{x}{L},\qquad
 \mathbb E(\sigma m)=\frac{z}{L}.$$ The first identity cancels the tail. The second recovers the desired displacement without passing to one conditional distribution.

Apply Corollary <a href="#stab:bounded-multiplier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:bounded-multiplier">42</a> to this finite law, with multiplier $\nu=\sigma$. Its mean $x/L$ is supported in $A$, so the center-tail parameter in that corollary is zero. Its evaluation deficit under $f$ is $$\delta=1-(x/L)(f)=1-1/L\ge0.$$ The first inequality in <a href="#stab:bounded-multiplier-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:bounded-multiplier-bound">[stab:bounded-multiplier-bound]</a>, together with the two identities in <a href="#molw:means" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molw:means">[molw:means]</a>, therefore gives $$\frac{\|z\|}{L}
 =\left\|Q_A\mathbb E(\sigma m)\right\|
 \le\sqrt{6\delta}+\sqrt{2\delta}
 =(\sqrt6+\sqrt2)\sqrt{1-1/L}.$$ Multiplying by $L$ proves <a href="#molw:lens" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molw:lens">[molw:lens]</a> when $r=1$. The multiplier may depend on the represented atom; the corollary requires only $|\sigma|\le1$, which holds for the endpoint sign used here.

For general $z\in\ker P_A$, use the finite tail approximants from Lemma <a href="#binf:projections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="binf:projections">4</a>. Their endpoint norms and averages converge, so the finite-support inequality passes to the limit. Finally, rescaling from $r=1$ gives $rC\sqrt{(L/r)(L/r-1)}=C\sqrt{L(L-r)}$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-molw-modulus"></a>
<div id="molw:modulus" class="corollary">

**Corollary 48**. *For every $t>0$, put <a id="molw:rho"></a>$$\label{molw:rho}
 \omega(t)=\frac{\sqrt{1+4(t/C)^2}-1}{2},\qquad C=\sqrt2+\sqrt6.$$ Then the averaged asymptotic modulus of $B_\infty$ satisfies $\widehat\delta_{B_\infty}(t)\ge\omega(t)/2>0$. The bound holds when the cofinite infimum allows every perturbation direction of norm at least one.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-molw-modulus"><summary>Proof of stmt-molw-modulus</summary>

*Proof.* For a finite-support unit center $x$, choose a finite ancestral set $A$ containing its support and take $F=\ker P_A$. If $y\in F$ and $\|y\|\ge1$, Theorem <a href="#molw:average" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="molw:average">47</a> applied to $z=ty$ yields $t^2\le C^2 L(L-1)$. Since $L\ge1$, solving this quadratic gives $L-1\ge\omega(t)$. The projection $P_A$ has finite rank, so $F$ is closed and has finite codimension.

For an arbitrary unit center $x$, density and normalization provide a finite-support unit vector $x_0$ with $\|x-x_0\|<\omega(t)/2$. Choose the subspace associated with $x_0$ before choosing $y$. Each endpoint norm changes by at most $\|x-x_0\|$, uniformly for all such $y$, so their average exceeds $1+\omega(t)/2$. Taking the infimum over $y$, the supremum over $F$, and then the infimum over unit centers gives the assertion. This argument imposes no upper bound on $t$. ◻

</details>

We now return to the finite-height forest space $X=J^*$ of Proposition <a href="#presentation:identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:identity">31</a>.

<a id="mola:conditional-section"></a>
#### Signed and conditional recovery from finite approximations

<span id="mol:closed-hull-signed-section" label="mol:closed-hull-signed-section"></span>

Finite atom laws approximate completed endpoints in norm. Their mixture therefore has a small tail even when the intended center is supported on the head. The next two estimates keep this approximation error explicit. Recovering one conditional law incurs a factor of two in its absolute boundary mass; recovering the signed mixture avoids that factor. The conditional estimate allows the displacement to have a nonzero head, whereas the signed statement below is formulated for a pure tail.

<a id="stmt-mola-conditional"></a>
<div id="mola:conditional" class="proposition">

<a id="mola:conditional-prelimit"></a>
**Proposition 49**. *Let $H$ be finite and ancestor-closed, $x=P_Hx$, and $x+h,x-h\in B_X$, with arbitrary $h\in X$. Suppose $0<d<1$ and $\|x\|\ge1-d$. For every $\eta>0$, choose finite represented-atom laws with means $z_\pm$ such that $\|z_\pm-(x\pm h)\|\le\eta$. Such laws exist, and every such pair gives $$\label{mola:conditional-prelimit}
 \|Q_Hh\|\le\eta+2\sqrt{d+\eta}
       +2\sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2(d+\eta)})^2+4(d+\eta)}.$$ Consequently, $$\|Q_Hh\|\le(2+2\sqrt6)\sqrt d.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mola-conditional"><summary>Proof of stmt-mola-conditional</summary>

*Proof.* By Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-hull" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-hull">7</a>, choose $p=P_Hp$ with $\rho(p)=1$ and $x(p)=\|x\|\ge1-d$. The two ball inclusions give $x(p)\pm h(p)\le1$, and hence $|h(p)|\le d$. Fix $\eta>0$. Corollary <a href="#cor:closed-atomic-ball" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:closed-atomic-ball">8</a> provides finite represented-atom mixtures approximating the two endpoints within $\eta$. Write $\mathbb E_+$, $\mathbb E_-$ for their expectations and $\mathbb E=(\mathbb E_++\mathbb E_-)/2$ for the equal mixture. For each atom $Z=\sum_i a_i d_{S_i}$ set $b_i=p(S_i)$, $\widehat Z=\sum_i b_i d_{S_i}$, $D=\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2$, and $L=1-\sum_i b_i^2$. Pointwise $D+L\le2(1-Z(p))$. The mixture pairs with $p$ by at least $1-d-\eta$, whereas each conditional law pairs with $p$ by at least $1-2d-\eta$. Therefore $$\mathbb ED,\mathbb EL\le2(d+\eta),\qquad
 \mathbb E_\pm D,\mathbb E_\pm L\le4d+2\eta\le4(d+\eta).$$ In particular the conditional replacement error is at most $2\sqrt{d+\eta}$.

Write $z_\pm=\mathbb E_\pm Z$ and $z=(z_++z_-)/2$. These finite atom means lie in $B_X$. Their approximation to the intended endpoints gives $$\|Q_Hz\|\le\eta,\qquad
 1-z(p)\le d+\eta,\qquad 1-z_\pm(p)\le2d+\eta.$$ Apply Theorem <a href="#stab:weighted" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:weighted">41</a> to $z_+,z_-$ with equal weights and this same head test. It gives $$\|Q_Hz_\pm\|
 \le2\sqrt{(\eta+\sqrt{2(d+\eta)})^2+4(d+\eta)}
       +\sqrt{4d+2\eta}.$$ The conditional boundary-mass factor is two, while the conditional replacement term is at most $2\sqrt{d+\eta}$. Restoring the endpoint approximation costs at most $\eta$, since $Q_Hx=0$ and $Q_H$ is contractive. This proves <a href="#mola:conditional-prelimit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mola:conditional-prelimit">[mola:conditional-prelimit]</a>. Letting $\eta\downarrow0$ gives the asserted limit. ◻

</details>

For signed recovery, retain the endpoint sign in the equal mixture of the two approximating laws. The following estimate shows exactly which conditional loss disappears. The head again remains fixed, and the displacement is not projected to finite support.

<a id="stmt-mol-closed-hull-signed"></a>
<div id="mol:closed-hull-signed" class="proposition">

<a id="mol:closed-hull-error"></a>
**Proposition 50**. *In the forest space $X=J^*$, let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$, $y=Q_Hy$, and $x\pm y\in B_X$. Put $d=1-\|x\|$. For every $\varepsilon>0$, the finite atomic approximation argument gives $$\label{mol:closed-hull-error}
 \|y\|\le e_\varepsilon+
 \sqrt{e_\varepsilon^2+4(d+\varepsilon)},\qquad
 e_\varepsilon=\varepsilon+\sqrt{2(d+\varepsilon)}.$$ Consequently $\|y\|\le(\sqrt2+\sqrt6)\sqrt d$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mol-closed-hull-signed"><summary>Proof of stmt-mol-closed-hull-signed</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a head test $p=P_Hp$ with $\rho(p)\le1$ and $x(p)=\|x\|$. Approximate $x\pm y$ within $\varepsilon$ by finite represented-atom means $z_\pm$, using Corollary <a href="#cor:closed-atomic-ball" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:closed-atomic-ball">8</a>. On their equal mixture, let $\sigma=1$ on the plus law and $\sigma=-1$ on the minus law. Then $\|\mathbb EA-x\|\le\varepsilon$ and $\|\mathbb E(\sigma A)-y\|\le\varepsilon$. Its mean test deficit is at most $d+\varepsilon$, and its mean tail has norm at most $\varepsilon$. Corollary <a href="#stab:bounded-multiplier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:bounded-multiplier">42</a> applies to this finite law with multiplier $\sigma$, and hence gives $$\left\|Q_H\mathbb E(\sigma A)\right\|
 \le\sqrt{(\varepsilon+\sqrt{2(d+\varepsilon)})^2
                   +4(d+\varepsilon)}
       +\sqrt{2(d+\varepsilon)}.$$ There is no conditional boundary-mass factor: the signed expectation recovers the difference of the two approximating endpoints directly. Since $Q_Hy=y$, adding the endpoint approximation error $\varepsilon$ proves <a href="#mol:closed-hull-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mol:closed-hull-error">[mol:closed-hull-error]</a> with the stated $e_\varepsilon$. Let $\varepsilon$ decrease to zero. For endpoints of norm at most $1+\lambda$ about a supported unit center, first divide by $1+\lambda$ and take $d=\lambda/(1+\lambda)$ in the same calculation. ◻

</details>

For a general near-unit center, first approximate it by its projection onto one finite ancestor-closed head, and divide that projected center and each displacement by one plus the center-approximation error. Both endpoint bounds then hold in the unit ball, so Proposition <a href="#mola:conditional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mola:conditional">49</a> controls every displacement tail using that same head. For an infinite family $(v_j)$ with $x\pm v_j\in B_X$, the bounded head projections $(P_Hv_j)$ are totally bounded. Thus, if the family is $\varepsilon$-separated, the center satisfies $\|x\|\le1-\delta$ for some $\delta>0$ depending only on $\varepsilon$. This is the conclusion of Corollary <a href="#mola:separated-sequential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mola:separated-sequential">75</a>.

<a id="molb:unscaled-section"></a>
#### Endpoint laws at their original radius

We work in the finite-height forest space $X$ of Proposition <a href="#presentation:identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:identity">31</a>, with primal segment norm $\rho$ on $J$. Keeping the endpoint radius in the represented coefficients gives a joint budget for their replacement error and the unused squared head test.

<a id="stmt-molb-unscaled-tail"></a>
<div id="molb:unscaled-tail" class="proposition">

<a id="molb:unscaled-head"></a>
**Proposition 51**. *Let $\delta>0$ and $x\in X$ satisfy $\|x\|=1$. There are a finite ancestral head $H$ and a test $p=P_Hp\in J$ such that $$\label{molb:unscaled-head}
 \rho(p)\le1,\qquad x(p)\ge1-\delta,\qquad
 \|Q_Hx\|\le\delta.$$ For this same head and every $y\in X$ satisfying $\|x+y\|,\|x-y\|\le1+\delta$, put $h=6\delta+\delta^2$. Then <a id="molb:unscaled-bound"></a>$$\label{molb:unscaled-bound}
 \max\{\|Q_H(x+y)\|,\|Q_H(x-y)\|,\|Q_Hy\|\}
 \le F(\delta):=
 \delta+\sqrt{2h}+2\sqrt{(2\delta+\sqrt h)^2+2h}.$$ In particular, for every $\varepsilon>0$ one can choose $\delta>0$ independently of $x$ so that this head satisfies $\|Q_Hy\|<\varepsilon$ for every such $y$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-molb-unscaled-tail"><summary>Proof of stmt-molb-unscaled-tail</summary>

*Proof.* Coordinate density and contractivity give a finite ancestral $H$ with $\|Q_Hx\|\le\delta$. Under the identification $X^*=J$ in Proposition <a href="#presentation:identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:identity">31</a>, choose a norming test $f\in J$ with $\rho(f)=1$ and $x(f)=1$. Set $p=P_Hf$. Then $\rho(p)\le1$ and $x(p)=1-(Q_Hx)(f)\ge1-\delta$, proving <a href="#molb:unscaled-head" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molb:unscaled-head">[molb:unscaled-head]</a>. This choice uses only $x$ and $\delta$.

Fix any permitted $y$. The radius-$(1+\delta)$ version of Corollary <a href="#cor:closed-atomic-ball" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:closed-atomic-ball">8</a> provides two finite laws of represented functionals $$V=\sum_i a_i d_{S_i},\qquad
 S_i\text{ pairwise disjoint},\qquad
 \sum_i a_i^2\le(1+\delta)^2,$$ whose respective means approximate $x+y$ and $x-y$ within $\delta$. Write $\mathbb E_+$ and $\mathbb E_-$ for these laws and $\mathbb E=(\mathbb E_++\mathbb E_-)/2$ for their equal mixture. Thus $$\|\mathbb E_\pm V-(x\pm y)\|\le\delta,
 \qquad \|\mathbb EV-x\|\le\delta,
 \qquad (\mathbb EV)(p)\ge1-2\delta.$$ The last bound uses $\rho(p)\le1$ and <a href="#molb:unscaled-head" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molb:unscaled-head">[molb:unscaled-head]</a>.

<a id="molb:unscaled-budget"></a>
<a id="molb:unscaled-replacement"></a>
In each represented outcome define $$b_i=p(S_i),\qquad W=\sum_i b_i d_{S_i},\qquad
 L=1-\sum_i b_i^2,\qquad E=\sum_i(a_i-b_i)^2.$$ Both $L$ and $E$ are nonnegative. Expanding the square and taking expectations gives the unscaled joint budget $$\label{molb:unscaled-budget}
 \mathbb E(E+L)
 =\mathbb E\left(1+\sum_i a_i^2-2V(p)\right)
 \le(1+\delta)^2+1-2(1-2\delta)
 =6\delta+\delta^2=h.$$ The disjoint-segment coefficient estimate <a href="#stab:sign:coefficients" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:sign:coefficients">[stab:sign:coefficients]</a> gives $\|V-W\|\le\sqrt E$. Cauchy–Schwarz, nonnegativity, and the equal mixture therefore yield $$\label{molb:unscaled-replacement}
 \mathbb E\|V-W\|\le\sqrt h,
 \qquad
 \mathbb E_\pm\|V-W\|
 \le\sqrt{\mathbb E_\pm E}\le\sqrt{2h}.$$

Let $G_H$ be the first exterior vertices. A segment wholly outside $H$ has replacement coefficient zero. At each $g\in G_H$ there is at most one crossing segment in an outcome; let $q_g$ be its head sum $b_i$, or zero if it is absent. Thus $W(e_g)=q_g$ and the restriction of $Q_HW$ to the cone at $g$ is either zero or $q_g$ times a segment functional starting at $g$. Put $m_g=\mathbb E|q_g|$ and $v_g=\mathbb E q_g$. These vectors have finite support. Antichain contraction, followed by <a href="#molb:unscaled-replacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molb:unscaled-replacement">[molb:unscaled-replacement]</a>, gives <a id="molb:unscaled-signed"></a>$$\label{molb:unscaled-signed}
 \|v\|_2\le\|Q_H\mathbb EW\|
 \le\|Q_Hx\|+\|\mathbb EV-x\|
       +\|\mathbb E(W-V)\|
 \le2\delta+\sqrt h.$$

Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:opposite-crossing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:opposite-crossing">9</a> to two independent copies of the represented segment families, both tested against $p$. Its simultaneous two-piece splits retain the longer segment’s exterior endpoint, and their available squared-test budgets are $L$ and $L'$. Consequently $$2\sum_{g:q_gq'_g<0}|q_gq'_g|\le L+L'.$$ Independence factors every product expectation. In combination with <a href="#molb:unscaled-budget" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molb:unscaled-budget">[molb:unscaled-budget]</a>, this gives <a id="molb:unscaled-unsigned"></a>$$\label{molb:unscaled-unsigned}
 \|m\|_2^2-\|v\|_2^2
 =2\mathbb E\sum_{g:q_gq'_g<0}|q_gq'_g|
 \le2\mathbb EL\le2h,
 \qquad
 \|m\|_2\le\sqrt{(2\delta+\sqrt h)^2+2h}.$$

For either conditional law, the absolute mass in the cone at $g$ is at most $\mathbb E_\pm|q_g|\le2m_g$. The rooted-mixture estimate in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a>, equivalently the subtree decomposition of Proposition <a href="#presentation:subtrees" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:subtrees">33</a>, therefore gives <a id="molb:unscaled-conditional"></a>$$\label{molb:unscaled-conditional}
 \|Q_H\mathbb E_\pm W\|\le2\|m\|_2.$$ Restoring the conditional coefficient error and the endpoint approximation yields $$\|Q_H(x\pm y)\|
 \le\delta+\sqrt{2h}+2\|m\|_2
 \le F(\delta).$$ Taking half the difference of the two endpoint tails proves the same bound for $Q_Hy$. Finally $F(\delta)\to0$ as $\delta\downarrow0$. Choosing $F(\delta)<\varepsilon$ before choosing $x$, and then choosing $H$ as above before $y$, proves the uniform assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stab:coef:section"></a>
### Original coefficients: cancellation and recovery

Retaining the original crossing coefficients leaves two terms to control: segments wholly outside the head and segments that cross its boundary. The toolkit gives two estimates for the latter. Transferring absolute head masses back to the coefficients yields a square-root bound; perturbing their pairwise products first yields a fourth-root bound. These are different estimates for the same coefficient data.

A second choice concerns recovery. A signed mixture directly represents the displacement, whereas selecting one endpoint from the pooled law can introduce a factor of two. We first compare these choices for exact finite laws, then retain approximation errors and a nonzero center tail. The final subsections convert the resulting estimates into midpoint bounds.

Unless a statement specifies the forest space $X$, we use either coordinate model $Z$ and the notation of Section <a href="#stab:toolkit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:toolkit">15</a>. Thus $L(A)$ is the evaluation deficit, $D(A)$ the squared-test slack, and $O(A),C(A)$ the exterior and crossing parts of a represented atom. All representations retain their chosen segment families and coefficients.

<a id="stab:coef:exact"></a>
#### Exact finite laws: two different estimates

<a id="stmt-stab-coef-exact-tail"></a>
<div id="stab:coef:exact-tail" class="proposition">

<a id="stab:coef:square-root"></a>
<a id="stab:coef:fourth-root"></a>
**Proposition 52**. *Let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$, $P_Hz=0$, and $\|x\pm z\|\le1$. Put $d=1-\|x\|\in[0,1]$ and $$\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(d)=2\bigl(\sqrt{2d}+\sqrt{6d+4\sqrt{2d}}\bigr)
 \qquad(d\ge0).$$ The original coefficients give both $$\begin{aligned}
 \|z\|&\le(\sqrt{12}+2\sqrt2)\sqrt d,
        \label{stab:coef:square-root}\\
 \|z\|&\le\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(d).
        \label{stab:coef:fourth-root}
\end{aligned}$$ More generally, if $0\le d_0<1$ and $\|x\|\ge1-d_0$, the right side of <a href="#stab:coef:fourth-root" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:fourth-root">[stab:coef:fourth-root]</a> may be replaced by $\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(d_0)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-coef-exact-tail"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-coef-exact-tail</summary>

<a id="stab:coef:exact-laws"></a>
<a id="stab:coef:exact-means"></a>
<a id="stab:coef:twelve"></a>
*Proof.* First suppose that $x,z$ are supported in a finite ancestral $K\supset H$. Choose a head test $p$ with $\rho(p)\le1$ and $x(p)=\|x\|$; when $x\ne0$ it can be chosen of norm one, and when $x=0$ take $p=0$. The finite atomic-ball identity represents each endpoint as a finite law of represented atoms. Let $\tau$ be the endpoint sign and use the equal mixture of the laws. Then $$\label{stab:coef:exact-laws}
 \mathbb EA=x,\qquad \mathbb E(\tau A)=z,\qquad
 \mathbb E_\pm L=\mathbb E L=d.$$ Here the last equality uses $z(p)=0$. The gate coordinates of the mixed mean vanish. Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:pointwise" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:pointwise">36</a> therefore gives $$\label{stab:coef:exact-means}
 \beta=\|\mathbb E c\|_2
 =\|(\mathbb E O(A)(e_g))_g\|_2\le\sqrt{2d},
 \qquad \|\mathbb E s\|_2\le2\sqrt{2d}.$$ Thus <a href="#stab:coef:absolute-head" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:absolute-head">[stab:coef:absolute-head]</a> and <a href="#stab:coef:via-head" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:via-head">[stab:coef:via-head]</a> yield $$\label{stab:coef:twelve}
 \|M_s\|_2^2\le12d,\qquad
 \|M_c\|_2\le\sqrt{12d}+\sqrt{2d}.$$ The signed mixture in <a href="#stab:coef:exact-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:exact-laws">[stab:coef:exact-laws]</a>, followed by Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a>, gives $$\|z\|=\|\mathbb E(\tau Q_HA)\|
 \le\|M_c\|_2+\mathbb E\|O(A)\|
 \le\sqrt{12d}+2\sqrt{2d}.$$ This signed reconstruction has no conditional factor of two.

<a id="stab:coef:fourth-moments"></a>
For the second route use the original-product estimate <a href="#stab:coef:via-products" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:via-products">[stab:coef:via-products]</a>. If $u_g=\mathbb E(c_g)_+$ and $w_g=\mathbb E(-c_g)_+$, it says $$\label{stab:coef:fourth-moments}
 \sum_g u_gw_g\le d+\sqrt{2d},\quad
 \|u-w\|_2\le\sqrt{2d},\quad
 \|u+w\|_2^2\le6d+4\sqrt{2d}.$$ Let $\mathbf1_+$ indicate the plus law in the mixture. Now $\mathbb E(\mathbf1_+Q_HA)=z/2$. Applying the test-segment proof of Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a> to $\mathbf1_+C(A)$, and bounding the exterior term by its pooled mean, gives $$\frac{\|z\|}{2}
 \le\sqrt{6d+4\sqrt{2d}}+\sqrt{2d}.$$ The factor of two comes from this indicator reconstruction. This proves <a href="#stab:coef:fourth-root" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:fourth-root">[stab:coef:fourth-root]</a>. The fourth-root order comes from the product perturbation, while the leading factor two comes from the indicator recovery; the two losses have separate causes.

The same loss can be recorded as two separate conditional bounds. If $d\le d_0$, positivity of the conditional weights and <a href="#stab:coef:conditional-assembly" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:conditional-assembly">[stab:coef:conditional-assembly]</a> give <a id="stab:coef:fourth-conditional"></a>$$\label{stab:coef:fourth-conditional}
 \|\mathbb E_+O(A)\|\le2\sqrt{2d_0},\qquad
 \|\mathbb E_+C(A)\|
 \le2\sqrt{6d_0+4\sqrt{2d_0}}.$$ Indeed each conditional mean of a nonnegative quantity is at most twice its pooled mean. Since $z=\mathbb E_+Q_HA$, adding these bounds gives $\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(d_0)$ directly, with the exterior and crossing errors still distinguished.

For a general $z$, let $K$ increase through finite ancestral sets containing $H$. Contractivity gives $\|x\pm P_Kz\|\le1$, and $P_HP_Kz=0$. Apply the finite calculation and use $P_Kz\to z$ in $Z$. The function $\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}$ is nondecreasing, so $d\le d_0$ proves the last assertion. This also handles nonattainment in the completed space without a representation of its endpoints. ◻

</details>

<a id="mola:actual-section"></a>
##### Signed recovery in the forest

The product estimate also permits signed recovery. The next forest statement uses the same absolute-mass budget as <a href="#stab:coef:fourth-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:fourth-moments">[stab:coef:fourth-moments]</a>, but retains the endpoint sign instead of selecting one conditional law. Its function $g$ is exactly $\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}/2$.

<a id="stmt-mola-actual"></a>
<div id="mola:actual" class="proposition">

**Proposition 53**. *In the forest space $X$, suppose $H$ is finite and ancestor-closed, $x=P_Hx$, $y=Q_Hy$, and $\|x+y\|,\|x-y\|\le1$. Then $$\|y\|\le g(1-\|x\|),\qquad
 g(s)=\sqrt{2s}+\sqrt{6s+4\sqrt{2s}}\quad(s\ge0).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mola-actual"><summary>Proof of stmt-mola-actual</summary>

*Proof.* For finite $y$, use the exact laws of <a href="#stab:coef:exact-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:exact-laws">[stab:coef:exact-laws]</a>, with $z=y$ and $d=s=1-\|x\|$. The original crossing coefficients obey <a href="#stab:coef:fourth-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:fourth-moments">[stab:coef:fourth-moments]</a>, so $$\|M_c\|_2\le\sqrt{6s+4\sqrt{2s}},\qquad
 \mathbb E\|O(A)\|\le\sqrt{2s}.$$ The signed reconstruction $y=\mathbb E(\tau Q_HA)$ and Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a> give $$\|y\|\le\|M_c\|_2+\mathbb E\|O(A)\|\le g(s).$$ There is no conditional factor of two in this calculation. It keeps the original-product transfer error, which is responsible for the fourth-root order. For general $y$, apply this finite estimate to $P_Ky$ along a finite ancestral exhaustion containing $H$. Contractivity preserves both endpoint bounds, and norm convergence proves the assertion. When $x=0$, the head test used in the exact-law calculation is $p=0$, as before. ◻

</details>

We now return to either coordinate model $Z$.

<a id="stmt-stab-coef-unit-head-lens"></a>
<div id="stab:coef:unit-head-lens" class="corollary">

**Corollary 54**. *If $x=P_Hx$ has norm one, $P_Hy=0$, and $\|x\pm y\|\le R$ for $R\ge1$, then <a id="stab:coef:unit-head-lens-eq"></a>$$\label{stab:coef:unit-head-lens-eq}
 \|y\|\le(\sqrt{12}+2\sqrt2)\sqrt{R(R-1)}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-coef-unit-head-lens"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-coef-unit-head-lens</summary>

*Proof.* Apply <a href="#stab:coef:square-root" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:square-root">[stab:coef:square-root]</a> to $x/R,y/R$, whose deficit is $h=1-1/R$, and multiply by $R$. In particular, both the mean coefficient error squared and the mean head slack are at most $2h$; the wholly exterior term has mean norm at most $\sqrt{2h}$. The crossing means have norms at most $\sqrt{2h}$ and $2\sqrt{2h}$ before absolute values, and the unsigned head mean has squared norm at most $12h$. These are the terms retained in the stated constant. ◻

</details>

<a id="stab:coef:approximate"></a>
#### Approximate laws in the completed atomic ball

The completed atomic-ball description also lets us approximate the endpoints themselves, rather than first projecting the displacement to finite support. The norm errors then remain in the cancellation equation. The gauge and coordinate-span presentations agree, so both routes are available in either model. Direct approximation does not require a completed endpoint to be an exact finite convex combination.

<a id="stmt-stab-coef-approximate-tail"></a>
<div id="stab:coef:approximate-tail" class="proposition">

<a id="stab:coef:approximate-tail-eq"></a>
**Proposition 55**. *Let $H$ be finite and ancestral, $x=P_Hx$ have norm one, and $P_Hy=0$. For $d>0$, the assumptions $\|x\pm y\|\le1+d$ imply $$\label{stab:coef:approximate-tail-eq}
 \|y\|\le(1+d)(7\sqrt2+4)\sqrt d.$$ In particular, $\|y\|\le32\sqrt d$ when $0<d\le1$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-coef-approximate-tail"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-coef-approximate-tail</summary>

<a id="stab:coef:approximate-deficit"></a>
*Proof.* Choose a norming head test $p$ as above. Equivalently, in the gauge presentation choose a norm-one functional norming $x$ and compose it with $P_H$; its coordinate values define $p$, since optimizing over disjoint-segment atoms gives $\sum_i p(S_i)^2\le1$. For $\epsilon>0$, choose finite convex combinations $w^\pm$ of atoms with $$\left\|w^\pm-\frac{x\pm y}{1+d}\right\|<\epsilon.$$ Such approximants follow from the closed atomic-ball description, not an exact convex representation in the completion. This closure also follows directly from the strict-gauge property: approximate a completed unit-ball vector $u$ by finite $u_n$ with $\|u_n-u\|<1/n$. Then $u_n/(1+2/n)$ has gauge strictly below one and belongs to the convex hull of atoms, while still converging to $u$. Use the two endpoint approximants’ finite laws and their equal mixture, and put $k=d+\epsilon$. Since $y(p)=0$, $$\label{stab:coef:approximate-deficit}
 \mathbb E_\pm L\le1-\frac1{1+d}+\epsilon\le k,
 \qquad \mathbb E L\le k,
 \qquad \left\|\mathbb EA-\frac{x}{1+d}\right\|<\epsilon.$$ The gate restriction is contractive and vanishes on $x$. Thus <a href="#stab:coef:gate-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:gate-coordinates">[stab:coef:gate-coordinates]</a> yields <a id="stab:coef:approximate-means"></a>$$\label{stab:coef:approximate-means}
 \|\mathbb Ec\|_2\le\epsilon+\sqrt{2k},\qquad
 \|\mathbb Es\|_2\le\epsilon+2\sqrt{2k}.$$ Use the splitting proof of Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:head-conflict" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:head-conflict">37</a>. Independence gives the same conflict budget as in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:gate-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:gate-bound">39</a>, so <a id="stab:coef:approximate-absolute-head"></a><a id="stab:coef:approximate-absolute-coef"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \|M_s\|_2^2&\le(\epsilon+2\sqrt{2k})^2+4k,
       \label{stab:coef:approximate-absolute-head}\\
 \|M_c\|_2&\le\epsilon+3\sqrt{2k}+2\sqrt k.
       \label{stab:coef:approximate-absolute-coef}
\end{aligned}$$ For the second line, first bound the square root in the first line by $\epsilon+2\sqrt{2k}+2\sqrt k$, and then use the mean coefficient error $\sqrt{2k}$.

The exterior term under the plus law has norm at most $\sqrt{2k}$ by <a href="#stab:coef:approximate-deficit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:approximate-deficit">[stab:coef:approximate-deficit]</a>. Apply the convex-product proof of Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a> to its crossing part. The conditional absolute coefficient mean is bounded coordinatewise by twice the pooled mean. Hence <a id="stab:coef:approximate-conditional"></a>$$\label{stab:coef:approximate-conditional}
 \|Q_Hw^+\|\le\sqrt{2k}
       +2(\epsilon+3\sqrt{2k}+2\sqrt k).$$ Since $Q_H$ is contractive and $Q_H(x+y)=y$, add $\epsilon$ to this bound for $\|y\|/(1+d)$. Let $\epsilon\downarrow0$. This proves <a href="#stab:coef:approximate-tail-eq" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:approximate-tail-eq">[stab:coef:approximate-tail-eq]</a>. Only when $d\le1$ may one use $1+d\le2$ to obtain $2(7\sqrt2+4)\sqrt d<32\sqrt d$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-65"></a>
#### A common head at a general center

We now work in the finite-height forest space $X=J^*$. For each head $H$, write $P=P_H$ and $Q=Q_H$. The next two applications choose the head before the displacement. Their center and radius assumptions differ, but they use the same original-coefficient mass estimate. For a finite atom law tested by $p=P_Hp$ with $\rho(p)\le1$, put $$k=\mathbb E L(A),\qquad
 \kappa=\|((\mathbb EA)(e_g))_{g\in G_H}\|_2,\qquad
 M_c=(\mathbb E|c_g(A)|)_{g\in G_H}.$$ The gate-coordinate identity gives $\beta=\|\mathbb Ec\|_2\le\kappa+\sqrt{2k}$. Hence the original-product bound in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:gate-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:gate-bound">39</a> gives $$\|M_c\|_2\le
 \sqrt{(\kappa+\sqrt{2k})^2+4k+4\sqrt{2k}}.$$ For an equal mixture, each conditional crossing tail costs at most $2\|M_c\|_2$, and its wholly exterior part costs at most $2\sqrt{2k}$. These follow from Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a> and the nonnegative pooled exterior budget. The proofs below specify $k$, $\kappa$, and the endpoint-approximation error in their respective settings.

<a id="molb:sec:original-coefficients"></a>
##### Unit centers and an enlarged lens

<a id="stmt-molb-prop-original-lens"></a>
<div id="molb:prop:original-lens" class="proposition">

**Proposition 56**. *For $0<\delta<1$ and every unit $x\in X$, one can choose a finite ancestor-closed $H$ such that $\|x\pm y\|\le1+\delta$ implies $$\|Qy\|\le\eta(\delta):=(1+\delta)
 \left\{2\sqrt{(2\delta+\sqrt{6\delta})^2
                    +4(3\delta+\sqrt{6\delta})}
             +2\sqrt{6\delta}+\delta\right\}.$$ In particular $\eta(\delta)\to0$ as $\delta\downarrow0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-molb-prop-original-lens"><summary>Proof of stmt-molb-prop-original-lens</summary>

*Proof.* Choose $H$ so that $\|Q_Hx\|\le\delta$, and choose a primal unit test $p=P_Hp$ with $x(p)\ge1-\delta$. Such a test norms $P_Hx$, whose norm is at least $1-\delta>0$. Both choices depend only on $x,\delta$, before $y$ is given.

Approximate each scaled endpoint $(x\pm y)/(1+\delta)$ within $\delta$ by a finite atom mean, and mix the two laws equally. With $k=\mathbb E(1-A(p))$, the pooled evaluation and tail satisfy $$k\le1-\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}+\delta\le3\delta,
 \qquad
 \|Q_H\mathbb EA\|\le\frac{\|Q_Hx\|}{1+\delta}+\delta
 \le2\delta.$$ Thus the pooled gate norm $\kappa$ is at most $2\delta$. The product-transfer estimate used here is <a id="molb:eq:original-products"></a>$$\label{molb:eq:original-products}
 \sum_g(-c_g(A)c_g(A'))_+
 \le L(A)+L(A')+\sqrt{2L(A)}+\sqrt{2L(A')},$$ proved in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:original-products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:original-products">38</a> by the one-Lipschitz property of the positive part and the head-conflict bound. Applying the displayed mass estimate above gives $$\|M_c\|_2\le
 B:=\sqrt{(2\delta+\sqrt{2k})^2+4(k+\sqrt{2k})}.$$ For either conditional law, the crossing tail has norm at most $2B$ and the wholly exterior part has norm at most $2\sqrt{2k}$. Restore the endpoint approximation error $\delta$, take half the difference of the two endpoint tails, and multiply by $1+\delta$. The bound is therefore $$\|Q_Hy\|\le(1+\delta)(2B+2\sqrt{2k}+\delta).$$ Using $k\le3\delta$ gives exactly the stated $\eta(\delta)$. Its leading order is $\delta^{1/4}$, inherited from the original-product estimate. ◻

</details>

<a id="molx:actual-center-section"></a>
##### Near-unit centers in the unit ball

Here the endpoints lie in the unit ball and the center is only assumed nearly unit. The finite head must again be fixed uniformly for all displacements in its lens.

<a id="stmt-molx-actual-center-fourth"></a>
<div id="molx:actual-center-fourth" class="proposition">

**Proposition 57**. *For $0<\lambda<1$ and $x\in B_X$ with $\|x\|\ge1-\lambda$, there is a finite ancestral set $H$, depending only on $x$ and $\lambda$, such that every $y\in X$ with $x\pm y\in B_X$ satisfies <a id="molx:actual-center-fourth-bound"></a>$$\label{molx:actual-center-fourth-bound}
 \|Q_Hy\|\le G_{\mathrm{orig}}(\lambda)
 :=\lambda+2\sqrt{6\lambda}
 +2\sqrt{(2\lambda+\sqrt{6\lambda})^2
                  +12\lambda+4\sqrt{6\lambda}}.$$ In particular $G_{\mathrm{orig}}(\lambda)\to0$ as $\lambda\downarrow0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-molx-actual-center-fourth"><summary>Proof of stmt-molx-actual-center-fourth</summary>

*Proof.* By density of finitely supported vectors in $J$, choose $p\in B_J$ with finite support and $x(p)\ge\|x\|-\lambda$. Use the contractive exhaustion in Lemma <a href="#lem:forest-structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:forest-structure">2</a> to choose a finite ancestral $H$ containing this support and satisfying $\|Q_Hx\|\le\lambda$. Make these choices before selecting $y$.

By Corollary <a href="#cor:closed-atomic-ball" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:closed-atomic-ball">8</a>, choose finite represented atom laws with means $z_+,z_-$ such that $\|z_\pm-(x\pm y)\|\le\lambda$. Write $\mathbb E_\pm$ for their expectations and $\mathbb E=(\mathbb E_++\mathbb E_-)/2$. For $z=(z_++z_-)/2$ one has <a id="molx:actual-center-budget"></a>$$\label{molx:actual-center-budget}
 \|Q_Hz\|\le2\lambda,\qquad
 z(p)\ge1-3\lambda.$$

Use the notation of Section <a href="#stab:toolkit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:toolkit">15</a>. For an atom $A=\sum_i c_i d_{S_i}$, put $s_i=p(S_i)$ and $L=1-A(p)$. Its wholly exterior part is $O(A)$, and $c_g,s_g$ are its original and corrected crossing coefficients at $g\in G_H$. The pointwise budgets in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:pointwise" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:pointwise">36</a> give $$\|O(A)\|\le\sqrt{2L},\qquad
 \|c-s\|_2\le\sqrt{2L},\qquad
 \|c\|_2,\|s\|_2\le1.$$ Here the last two vectors are the crossing arrays. In particular $\mathbb EL\le3\lambda$ and $\mathbb E\sqrt{2L}\le\sqrt{6\lambda}$.

<a id="molx:actual-center-mass"></a>
For two independent represented atoms, the original-product estimate of Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:original-products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:original-products">38</a> reads <a id="molx:actual-center-transfer"></a>$$\label{molx:actual-center-transfer}
 \sum_g(-c_gc'_g)_+
 \le L+L'+\sqrt{2L}+\sqrt{2L'}.$$ Its head comparison may use either the truncation or the two-piece proof in Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:head-conflict" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:head-conflict">37</a>; both give the same transfer bound. This is the estimate underlying the common mass bound above. Thus, for $a_g=\mathbb E(c_g)_+$ and $b_g=\mathbb E(-c_g)_+$, independence yields $$4\sum_g a_gb_g\le12\lambda+4\sqrt{6\lambda}.$$ The gate-coordinate identity <a href="#stab:coef:gate-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:gate-coordinates">[stab:coef:gate-coordinates]</a> also gives $$\|a-b\|_2=\|\mathbb Ec\|_2
 \le\|Q_Hz\|+\mathbb E\|O(A)\|
 \le2\lambda+\sqrt{6\lambda}.$$ Consequently the original absolute mass $m=a+b=\mathbb E|c|$ satisfies $$\label{molx:actual-center-mass}
 \|m\|_2^2\le(2\lambda+\sqrt{6\lambda})^2
                         +12\lambda+4\sqrt{6\lambda}.$$

For either endpoint law, positivity of the mixture weights gives $$\mathbb E_\pm\|O(A)\|\le2\sqrt{6\lambda},\qquad
 \mathbb E_\pm|c_g|\le2m_g.$$ Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a>, or its proof by maximizing one test segment in each cone, therefore bounds the whole crossing tail by $2\|m\|_2$. The segments selected in different cones are disjoint, so their primal test sums have squared sum at most one; this is what turns the absolute masses into the whole-tail bound. Thus $\|Q_Hz_\pm\|\le2\sqrt{6\lambda}+2\|m\|_2$. Finally $\|y-(z_+-z_-)/2\|\le\lambda$, and hence $$\|Q_Hy\|\le\lambda+
 \tfrac12\bigl(\|Q_Hz_+\|+\|Q_Hz_-\|\bigr).$$ Substitute <a href="#molx:actual-center-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molx:actual-center-mass">[molx:actual-center-mass]</a>. All choices of $H$ and $p$ preceded $y$, as required. ◻

</details>

<a id="stab:coef:moduli"></a>
#### Midpoint consequences with their original losses

We return to either coordinate model $Z$ and use the averaged modulus defined in <a href="#eq:averaged-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:averaged-modulus">[eq:averaged-modulus]</a>, with perturbations of norm at least one. The following conversions retain their different lower bounds on the endpoint norms.

<a id="stmt-stab-coef-modulus-conversions"></a>
<div id="stab:coef:modulus-conversions" class="proposition">

**Proposition 58**. *For $0<t<1$, each of the following conclusions holds.*

1.  *If $e>0$ satisfies $$(\sqrt{12}+2\sqrt2)\sqrt{(1+e)e}<t/2,$$ then $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge e/4$.*

2.  *If $0<c<1$, $R=1+4c$, and $R\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(5c/R)<t$, then $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge c$.*

3.  *If $d>0$, $3d<1$, and $(1+2d)\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(3d)<t$, then $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge d/2$.*

4.  *The completed atomic-ball route gives $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge t^2/16384$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-coef-modulus-conversions"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-coef-modulus-conversions</summary>

*Proof.* For the first assertion, approximate any unit $x$ by a unit finite head $x_0=P_Hx_0$ with $\|x-x_0\|<e/4$, and use $F=\ker P_H$. For $y\in F$ with $\|y\|\ge1$, Corollary <a href="#stab:coef:unit-head-lens" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:unit-head-lens">54</a> rules out both $\|x_0\pm ty\|\le1+e$, since their lens bound is less than $t/2<t\|y\|$. Both endpoint norms are at least one by projection. Their average is therefore greater than $1+e/2$, and replacing $x_0$ by $x$ leaves it greater than $1+e/4$.

For the second assertion choose $H$ with $\|x-P_Hx\|\le c$ and again let $F=\ker P_H$. Both $\|x\pm ty\|\ge\|P_Hx\|\ge1-c$. If their average were at most $1+c$, each would be at most $1+3c$. It would follow that $\|P_Hx\pm ty\|\le1+4c=R$. For every finite ancestral $K\supset H$, apply <a href="#stab:coef:fourth-root" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:fourth-root">[stab:coef:fourth-root]</a> to $P_Hx/R$ and $tP_Ky/R$. Its deficit is at most $1-(1-c)/R=5c/R$, so $$t\|P_Ky\|\le R\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(5c/R)<t.$$ Exhaustion gives $t\|y\|<t$, a contradiction. Notice that the definition of $\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}$ for all nonnegative arguments makes this parameter substitution meaningful even before $c$ is made small.

For the third assertion choose $H$ with $\|x-P_Hx\|<d$, and let $f\in Z^*$ have norm one and $f(x)=1$. Put $F=\ker P_H\cap\ker f$. Then $\|x\pm ty\|\ge1$ for $y\in F$. If both were at most $1+d$, then $\|P_Hx\pm ty\|\le1+2d$, and the normalized head would have norm at least $$\frac{1-d}{1+2d}\ge1-3d.$$ Proposition <a href="#stab:coef:exact-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:exact-tail">52</a> would give $t\le(1+2d)\omega_{\mathrm{coef}}(3d)$ for $\|y\|\ge1$, contrary to the chosen parameter. Thus the average is greater than $1+d/2$.

Finally set $d=(t/64)^2$ for the fourth assertion. Choose $H$ so that $\|x-P_Hx\|<d/8$. The normalized head $x_0=P_Hx/\|P_Hx\|$ then exists and satisfies $\|x-x_0\|<d/4$, because normalization costs at most the projection error again. For $y\in\ker P_H$ of norm at least one, Proposition <a href="#stab:coef:approximate-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:approximate-tail">55</a> and $32\sqrt d=t/2<t$ show that one endpoint $x_0\pm ty$ has norm greater than $1+d$. Both have norm at least one. Their average exceeds $1+d/2$, and replacing $x_0$ by $x$ leaves more than $1+d/4=1+t^2/16384$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stab:coef:conditional"></a>
#### A conditional estimate using prefix truncation

One can also keep an endpoint law separate while using the head-sign comparison. The following argument retains the wholly exterior term under that law; its coefficient is smaller than the one obtained by bounding every conditional term through the pooled expectation.

<a id="stmt-stab-coef-conditional-modulus"></a>
<div id="stab:coef:conditional-modulus" class="proposition">

<a id="stab:coef:finite-strict"></a>
**Proposition 59**. *For $0<t<1$, set $\delta=(t/25)^2$. If $x_0=P_Hx_0$ and $y$ are unit vectors, $P_Hy=0$, and both are finitely supported, then $$\label{stab:coef:finite-strict}
 \max\{\|x_0+ty\|,\|x_0-ty\|\}>1+\delta.$$ For general unit $y\in\ker P_H$, the same inequality holds with $\ge$ in place of $>$. Moreover, <a id="stab:coef:conditional-modulus-eq"></a>$$\label{stab:coef:conditional-modulus-eq}
 \widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge t^2/2500.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-stab-coef-conditional-modulus"><summary>Proof of stmt-stab-coef-conditional-modulus</summary>

<a id="stab:coef:twelve-conditional"></a>
*Proof.* If <a href="#stab:coef:finite-strict" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:finite-strict">[stab:coef:finite-strict]</a> failed, represent the two scaled endpoints by exact finite atom laws and use their equal mixture as in <a href="#stab:coef:exact-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:exact-laws">[stab:coef:exact-laws]</a>. Put $\eta=\delta/(1+\delta)$; this is the deficit of the scaled unit head. The conditional and pooled deficits all equal $\eta$. Using the *truncation* proof of Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:head-conflict" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:head-conflict">37</a>, the calculations <a href="#stab:coef:exact-means" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:exact-means">[stab:coef:exact-means]</a>–<a href="#stab:coef:twelve" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:twelve">[stab:coef:twelve]</a> give $$\|\mathbb Ec\|_2\le\sqrt{2\eta},\quad
 \|\mathbb Es\|_2\le2\sqrt{2\eta},\quad
 \|M_s\|_2^2\le12\eta,\quad
 \|M_c\|_2\le\sqrt{12\eta}+\sqrt{2\eta}.$$ In the plus law, $\mathbb E_+\|O(A)\|\le\sqrt{2\eta}$ because its own mean deficit is $\eta$. For the crossing part use the disjoint-test-segment proof of Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:tail-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:tail-assembly">[stab:coef:tail-assembly]</a> and the bound of its conditional absolute mean by twice the pooled one. Therefore $$\label{stab:coef:twelve-conditional}
 \frac{t}{1+\delta}
 \le2(\sqrt{12\eta}+\sqrt{2\eta})+\sqrt{2\eta}
 \le12\sqrt\delta.$$ Indeed $2\sqrt{12}+3\sqrt2<12$ and $\eta\le\delta$. But $\delta<1/625$ and $$12(1+\delta)\sqrt\delta
 =\frac{12}{25}(1+\delta)t<t,$$ contradicting <a href="#stab:coef:twelve-conditional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="stab:coef:twelve-conditional">[stab:coef:twelve-conditional]</a>.

For a completed unit tail $y$, take finite ancestral projections $P_Ky\to y$ with $K\supset H$. For all sufficiently large $K$ the projections are nonzero. Normalize them to unit vectors; they still vanish on $H$ and converge to $y$. Passing to the limit in the finite strict inequality yields the stated non-strict one.

Given an arbitrary unit center $x$, choose $H$ so that its normalized head $x_0$ satisfies $\|x-x_0\|<\delta/2$. Choose a norm-one $f\in Z^*$ with $f(x)=1$, and set $F=\ker P_H\cap\ker f$. For unit $y\in F$, both actual endpoint norms are at least one, and the maximum is at least $1+\delta/2$. Their average is at least $1+\delta/4$. For a fixed direction, the average $s\mapsto(\|x+sy\|+\|x-sy\|)/2$ is even and convex, hence nondecreasing for $s\ge0$. This extends the bound to all $y\in F$ of norm at least one and gives $\widehat\delta_Z(t)\ge\delta/4=t^2/2500$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-70"></a>
#### Separated families in a lens

For the forest space $X$, the function $g(s)$ of Proposition <a href="#mola:actual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mola:actual">53</a> tends to zero and is of order $s^{1/4}$ as $s\downarrow0$. To see the separated-lens consequence directly, fix $t>0$ and choose $0<\Delta<1/2$ such that $$(1+2\Delta)g(4\Delta)+\Delta<t.$$ If an infinite family $(y_j)$ satisfies $\|x\|>1-\Delta$, $\|x\pm y_j\|\le1$, and $\|y_i-y_j\|\ge2t$ for distinct indices, choose a finite head with $\|Q_Hx\|\le\Delta$ and a pair with $\|P_H(y_i-y_j)/2\|\le\Delta$. Put $v=(y_i-y_j)/2$. The vectors $x\pm v$ are averages of opposite endpoints and lie in $B_X$. The vectors $P_Hx/(1+2\Delta)$ and $Q_Hv/(1+2\Delta)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#mola:actual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mola:actual">53</a>; the first has deficit at most $4\Delta$, whereas the second has norm at least $(t-\Delta)/(1+2\Delta)$. This contradicts the choice of $\Delta$.

The actual-center estimate gives a direct version with one head for the whole family. For example, choose $\lambda\in(0,1)$ so that $2G_{\mathrm{orig}}(\lambda)<\varepsilon/2$. If $x\pm y_j\in B_X$ for an infinite $\varepsilon$-separated family and $\|x\|\ge1-\lambda$, the same head works for every $j$. The bounded sequence $(P_Hy_j)$ has two terms at distance less than $\varepsilon/2$, while the corresponding tail difference has norm at most $2G_{\mathrm{orig}}(\lambda)<\varepsilon/2$, a contradiction.

<a id="molb:sec:variants"></a>
### Alternative cancellation estimates

The first two comparisons use different information about the same crossing coefficients. Fractional matching assigns cancellation to the slack of individual outcomes. A cross-law comparison instead keeps the smaller absolute mass supplied by the two endpoints in each cone. We then record the losses from using only one ordered sign conflict and from retaining weaker projection and trimming estimates. These are comparisons of proof mechanisms and error budgets; their lens bounds need not improve the main theorem.

The matching and cross-law arguments below concern the finite-height forest space $X=J^*$. The ordered and weaker-trimming arguments concern $B_\infty$, with their own hypotheses stated at the point of use. Write $\mathbf1_I=d_I$ for a segment functional. The atomic, gauge, and coordinate-polar descriptions of $X$ agree by Proposition <a href="#presentation:identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:identity">31</a>. Its finite head normers and atomic approximations come from Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-hull" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-hull">7</a> and Corollary <a href="#cor:closed-atomic-ball" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:closed-atomic-ball">8</a>.

For a finite ancestor-closed head $H$, put $P=P_H$ and $Q=I-P$. The minimal excluded nodes $g$ are its gates, and $T_g$ denotes the full descendant cone at $g$. The contractive projections and the cone formula of Proposition <a href="#presentation:subtrees" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:subtrees">33</a> imply the two estimates used below: gate-coordinate restriction is contractive into $\ell_2$, and a combination of rooted tail segments with absolute coefficient totals $a_g$ has norm at most $\|(a_g)\|_2$. The latter follows by applying the triangle inequality in each cone and then the cone formula. Countable cone sums and norm limits use the completed version of that proposition.

<a id="molb:sec:matching"></a>
#### Fractional matching of opposite masses

<a id="stmt-molb-lem-matching"></a>
<div id="molb:lem:matching" class="lemma">

<a id="molb:eq:matching-capacity"></a>
**Lemma 60**. *Fix a primal unit vector $z$ supported on $H$, and a finite probability distribution of represented atoms $A_\omega=\sum_Ic_{\omega I}\mathbf1_I$, with probabilities $p_\omega$. Write $$b_{\omega I}=z(I),\qquad
 L_\omega=1-\sum_Ib_{\omega I}^2,\qquad
 B_\omega=\sum_Ib_{\omega I}Q\mathbf1_I.$$ At each gate let $P_g$ and $M_g$ be the positive and absolute negative coefficient masses in $\mathbb E B$. Then $$\label{molb:eq:matching-capacity}
 \sum_g\min(P_g,M_g)^2\le\mathbb E L.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-molb-lem-matching"><summary>Proof of stmt-molb-lem-matching</summary>

*Proof.* Only crossing segments contribute to $B_\omega$. At any gate an outcome supplies at most one crossing, with signed coefficient $b_{\omega g}$. Regard its absolute mass $p_\omega|b_{\omega g}|$ as a supply. Match positive and negative supplies at this gate by repeatedly assigning the smaller of the two remaining supplies to a pair and deleting every exhausted supply. This finite procedure produces pair masses $m\ge0$ whose total is $\min(P_g,M_g)$. The total mass using any one outcome at this gate is at most its supply.

In a pair of positive mass, the two head intervals end at the parent of the gate. They are strictly nested: equality would give equal primal evaluations, whereas the signs are opposite. Denote the absolute evaluations of the longer and shorter heads by $u>0$ and $v>0$, respectively. Splitting the longer crossing immediately before the shorter head gives a squared-test gain $$D=(u+v)^2+v^2-u^2\ge uv.$$ Charge $(m/u)D$ to the outcome containing the longer crossing. This charge is at least $mv$.

Here is the capacity argument for all these charges. Fix an outcome $\omega$ and one of its crossing segments $I$. Its absolute evaluation $u$ is fixed. The sum of the fractions $m/u$ over pairs in which this segment supplies the longer head is at most $p_\omega$. Consequently its total charge is at most $p_\omega$ times the largest proposed gain on this segment, or zero if there is no proposal. Choose one split giving this largest gain on every affected segment of the outcome. These segments were disjoint, so all chosen splits can be made together. Their total gain is at most $L_\omega$, because the new family is a legal test on the primal unit vector. Summing gives a bound $p_\omega L_\omega$ for the charge to this outcome. Thus $$\sum_{g}\sum_{\text{pairs at }g}mv\le\mathbb E L.$$

At a fixed gate the shorter member of a pair contributes the quantity $m/v$. The supply constraint for each outcome implies that the sum of these quantities over pairs having that outcome as shorter member is at most its probability. There is at most one crossing per outcome at the gate, hence $\sum m/v\le\sum_\omega p_\omega=1$. Cauchy–Schwarz now gives $$\min(P_g,M_g)^2=(\sum m)^2
       \le(\sum mv)(\sum m/v)\le\sum mv.$$ Sum over gates to obtain <a href="#molb:eq:matching-capacity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molb:eq:matching-capacity">[molb:eq:matching-capacity]</a>. ◻

</details>

The independent-sample argument gives a stronger numerical bound for these same masses. Indeed Lemma <a href="#stab:coef:head-conflict" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stab:coef:head-conflict">37</a> gives $4\sum_gP_gM_g\le2\mathbb EL$, so $$\sum_g\min(P_g,M_g)^2\le\sum_gP_gM_g\le\tfrac12\mathbb EL.$$ The fractional proof above instead displays a matching and charges its pairs to individual segment-test slacks. The next application retains its stated capacity bound to show how that argument controls a whole lens.

<a id="stmt-molb-prop-matching-lens"></a>
<div id="molb:prop:matching-lens" class="proposition">

<a id="molb:eq:matching-lens"></a>
**Proposition 61**. *For $0<h<1/4$ and every unit $x\in X$, there is a finite ancestor-closed $H$, depending only on $x,h$, such that $\|x\pm y\|\le1+h$ implies $$\label{molb:eq:matching-lens}
 \|Qy\|\le h+(1+h)
  \bigl[h+2\sqrt{6h}+2(2h+3\sqrt{6h})\bigr].$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-molb-prop-matching-lens"><summary>Proof of stmt-molb-prop-matching-lens</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a finitely supported primal unit vector $z$ with $x(z)>1-h$, and enlarge its ancestor closure so that $\|Qx\|<h$. Approximate each $(x\pm y)/(1+h)$ within $h$ by a finite mean of atoms. Let $\mathbb E$ be their equal mixture and $\mathbb E_\pm$ the two conditional means. The identity $$2(1-A(z))=(1-\sum c_I^2)+(1-\sum b_I^2)+\sum(c_I-b_I)^2$$ has three nonnegative terms on the right. Also $\mathbb EA(z)\ge(1-h)/(1+h)-h\ge1-3h$. Therefore $$\mathbb EL\le6h,\qquad
 \mathbb E\sum(c_I-b_I)^2\le6h,\qquad
 \mathbb E\|QA-B\|\le\sqrt{6h}.$$ The corresponding conditional replacement error is at most $2\sqrt{6h}$. Since $\mathbb EA$ approximates $x/(1+h)$ within $h$, gate-coordinate contraction gives $$\|P-M\|_2\le\|\mathbb EB\|\le2h+\sqrt{6h}.$$ By Lemma <a href="#molb:lem:matching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="molb:lem:matching">60</a> and the coordinatewise identity $P+M=|P-M|+2\min(P,M)$, $\|P+M\|_2\le2h+3\sqrt{6h}$. Each conditional absolute coefficient mass is at most twice the mixed mass, whence $\|\mathbb E_+B\|\le2(2h+3\sqrt{6h})$. Approximation and replacement bound the tail of $(x+y)/(1+h)$ by the bracket in <a href="#molb:eq:matching-lens" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="molb:eq:matching-lens">[molb:eq:matching-lens]</a>; subtracting $Qx$ proves the result. The displayed bound tends to zero with $h$ uniformly in the unit center. ◻

</details>

<a id="molx:separate-laws"></a>
#### Comparing the two endpoint laws

The tail can also be recovered by comparing the two endpoint laws separately. This argument keeps the smaller absolute mass in each subtree. It gives a different proof, with the following constant.

<a id="stmt-molx-minimum-tail"></a>
<div id="molx:minimum-tail" class="proposition">

<a id="molx:minimum-conclusion"></a>
**Proposition 62**. *Let $X=J^*$ be the finite-height forest space defined by <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a>, with the atomic and polar norm identifications of Proposition <a href="#presentation:identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="presentation:identity">31</a>. For a finite ancestor-closed set $A\subset\mathcal F$, let $P_A$ be coordinate restriction on $X$ and let $Q_A=I-P_A$. If $$P_Ax=x,\qquad Q_Ay=y,\qquad \|x+y\|,\|x-y\|\le1,$$ then, with $d=1-\|x\|$, $$\label{molx:minimum-conclusion}
 \|y\|\le(\sqrt6+2\sqrt2)\sqrt d\le6\sqrt d.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-molx-minimum-tail"><summary>Proof of stmt-molx-minimum-tail</summary>

*Proof.* Let $R$ be the minimal vertices of $\mathcal F\setminus A$, and let $U_r$ be the descendant subtree rooted at $r\in R$. Lemma <a href="#lem:forest-structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:forest-structure">2</a> supplies the contractive projections, and Lemma <a href="#lem:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cones">3</a> supplies both the exact subtree norm identity <a href="#eq:cone-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cone-decomposition">[eq:cone-decomposition]</a> and the contractive root-coordinate map.

If $x=0$, the conclusion follows from $\|y\|\le1$. Otherwise, Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-hull" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-hull">7</a> gives a head-supported test $a\in J$ with $\rho(a)=1$ and $x(a)=\|x\|$. Since $P_Ay=0$, both endpoints $z^\pm=x\pm y$ have value $1-d$ on $a$.

Write each represented atom as $$m=\sum_i c_i d_{S_i},\qquad \sum_i c_i^2\le1,$$ with its disjoint segment family included in the data. Corollary <a href="#cor:closed-atomic-ball" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:closed-atomic-ball">8</a> supplies the norm approximations used below.

<a id="molx:separate-deficits"></a>
<a id="molx:separate-error"></a>
